Note that in all cases the matrices are periodic of period
4m,\ 4m+2,\ 2n
for
\hat{gl}_{2m}^{(2)}
,
\hat{gl}_{2m+1}^{(2)}
,
\hat{so}_{2n}^{(2)}
, respectively.

Remark 2.2

The automorphism
\sigma
induces an automorphism on
d_{\infty}
(resp.
b_{\infty}
),
which is given by conjugation by
J
for
\hat{gl}_{n}^{(2)}
, and
O
for

\hat{so}_{2n}^{(2)}
,
where

\displaystyle J=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{2m}E_{k(2m)+j-m,k(2m)+j-m},\quad n=2m,

(2.32)

\displaystyle\big(\mbox{resp. }J=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{2m+1}E_{k(2m+1)+j-m+1,k(2m+1)+j-m+1},\quad n=2m+1\big),

\displaystyle O=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{j=1}^{2n}(-1)^{\delta_{jn}}E_{2kn+j,2kn+j}.

Note that the matrices
(a_{ij})_{i,j\in\mathbb{Z}}
that correspond to an element of
\hat{gl}_{n}^{(2)}
satisfy

a_{i+pn,j+pn}=J^{p}a_{i,j}J^{p}=c_{i}^{2p}c_{j}^{2p}a_{ij},\quad p\in\mathbb{Z}.

(2.33)

As before, we can restrict the cocycle of
x_{\infty}
to
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{(2)}
.
One can check that this gives the cocycle (
2.26
) for
\hat{gl}_{2m+1}^{(2)}
, and the following cocycle in the remaining cases:

\displaystyle\hat{gl}_{2m}^{(2)}:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\omega(t^{k}a,t^{\ell}b)=\frac{k}{2}\delta_{k,-\ell}\left({\rm Tr}(ab)+\sum_{i=1}^{m}{\rm Tr}((e_{ii}-e_{2m+1-i,2m+1-i})[a,b])\right),

\displaystyle\hat{so}_{2n}^{(2)}:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\omega(t^{k}a,t^{\ell}b)=\frac{k}{2}\delta_{k,-\ell}\left({\rm Tr}(ab)+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}{\rm Tr}((e_{ii}-e_{2n+1-i,2n+1-i})[a,b])\right).\

Lemma 2.3

We have the following bilinear forms on the subspace of the loop space
\mathbb{C}[t,t^{-1}]^{N}
, restricted from
\mathbb{C}^{\infty}
:

\displaystyle(t^{k}v,t^{\ell}w)_{\hat{gl}_{2n}^{(2)}}=

\displaystyle(-1)^{k}\delta_{k,\ell}(v,w)_{so_{2n}},\quad

\displaystyle N={2n},

\displaystyle(t^{k}v,t^{\ell}w)_{\hat{gl}_{2n+1}^{(2)}}=

\displaystyle(-1)^{k}\delta_{k,\ell}(v,w)_{so_{2n+1}},\quad

\displaystyle N=2n+1,

\displaystyle(t^{k}v,t^{\ell}w)_{\hat{so}_{2n}^{(2)}}=

\displaystyle(-1)^{k}\delta_{k,\ell}(Ov,w)_{so_{2n}},\quad

\displaystyle N=2n.

3
Representations of classical infinite matrix Lie algebras

In this section we will recall the spin and Weyl modules for the classical infinite matrix algebras. These modules will be fundamental for the description of representations of the classical affine Lie algebras, used in this paper.

3.1
The spin module for
a_{\infty}

Consider the Clifford algebra
C\ell
as a unital associative algebra with generators
\psi_{i}^{\pm}
,
i\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
, and relations

\psi^{\lambda}_{i}\psi^{\mu}_{j}+\psi^{\mu}_{j}\psi^{\lambda}_{i}=\delta_{\lambda,-\mu}\delta_{i,-j},\quad i,j\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z},\ \lambda,\mu=+\ \mbox{or }-.

(3.1)

Its corresponding spin module
F=F_{a}
is defined as the irreducible
C\ell
-module, which admits the vacuum vector
|0\rangle
, subject to the relation

\psi^{\pm}_{j}|0\rangle=0\ \text{for}\ j>0.

The well-known representation
r
of
a_{\infty}
on
F
is defined by

r(K)=I,\quad r(E_{ij})=:\psi^{+}_{-i+\frac{1}{2}}\psi^{-}_{j-\frac{1}{2}}:,\quad i,j\in\mathbb{Z},

(3.2)

where as usual the normally ordered product,
:\psi_{i}\psi_{j}:
is defined as equal to
\psi_{i}\psi_{j}
if
i\leq j
and
-\psi_{j}\psi_{i}
otherwise.
Define the charge on
F
by setting

\text{charge}(|0\rangle)=0\ \text{and charge}(\psi^{\pm}_{j})=\pm 1.

Then
F
decomposes into charge sectors

F=\bigoplus_{m\in{\mathbb{Z}}}F^{(m)},\quad\text{
where }F^{(m)}=\{v\in F\,|\,\text{charge}(v)=m\}.

(3.3)

Each subspace
F^{(m)}
is an irreducible highest weight

a_{\infty}
-module
[
18
]
,
[
13
]
, for which

|\pm m\rangle=\psi^{\pm}_{\frac{1}{2}-m}\psi^{\pm}_{\frac{3}{2}-m}\cdots\psi^{\pm}_{-\frac{1}{2}}|0\rangle,\qquad m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0},

is its highest weight vector, i.e.

r(E_{ij})|\pm m\rangle=0\ \text{for}\ i<j.

The highest weight is given by
r(E_{ii})|m\rangle=|m\rangle
for
1\leq i\leq m
,
r(E_{ii})|-m\rangle=-|-m\rangle
for
1-m\leq i\leq 0
, if
m\geq 1
, and
r(E_{ii}|m\rangle=0
in all other cases.

The elements

\psi_{i_{1}}^{+}\psi_{i_{2}}^{+}\cdots\psi_{i_{p}}^{+}\psi_{j_{1}}^{-}\psi_{j_{2}}^{-}\cdots\psi_{j_{p}}^{-}|m\rangle,

with
i_{1}<i_{2}<\cdots<i_{p}<-m
and
j_{1}<j_{2}<\cdots<j_{p}<m
form a basis of
F^{(m)}
.

Finally, using the embedding (
2.22
), the spin module
F
restricted to
a_{\infty}
is
\hat{gl}_{n}
-module, such that each
F^{(m)}
is an irreducible level
r(K)=1
highest weight
\hat{gl}_{n}
-module
[
18
]
.

3.2
The spin module for
b_{\infty}
and
d_{\infty}

Recall the bilinear forms (
2.19
) and the description of
x_{\infty}
for
x=b
and
d
in Subsection
2.3
. Then
the elements
B_{jk}=E_{-j,k}-E_{-k,j}=-B_{kj}
with
j>k
,

C_{j+\frac{1}{2},k-\frac{1}{2}}=(-1)^{k}E_{-j,k}-(-)^{j}E_{-k+1,j+1}=C_{k-\frac{1}{2},j+\frac{1}{2}}
,

D_{j+\frac{1}{2},k-\frac{1}{2}}=E_{-j,k}-E_{-k+1,j+1}=-D_{k-\frac{1}{2},j+\frac{1}{2}}
with
j\geq k
, given by (
2.20
) together with
K
,
form a basis of
b_{\infty}
,
c_{\infty}
,
d_{\infty}
, respectively.
Note that the representation
r
restricted to
x_{\infty}
, is given by

\displaystyle r(B_{jk})

\displaystyle=:\psi^{+}_{j+\frac{1}{2}}\psi^{-}_{k-\frac{1}{2}}-\psi^{+}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}\psi^{-}_{j-\frac{1}{2}}:,\

\displaystyle r(K)=I;

\displaystyle r(C_{j+\frac{1}{2},k-\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k}:\psi^{+}_{j+\frac{1}{2}}\psi^{-}_{k-\frac{1}{2}}-(-1)^{j}\psi^{+}_{k-\frac{1}{2}}\psi^{-}_{j+\frac{1}{2}}:,\

\displaystyle r(K)=I;

\displaystyle r(D_{j+\frac{1}{2},k-\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle=:\psi^{+}_{j+\frac{1}{2}}\psi^{-}_{k-\frac{1}{2}}-\psi^{+}_{k-\frac{1}{2}}\psi^{-}_{j+\frac{1}{2}}:,\

\displaystyle r(K)=I.

This suggests to define involutions
\iota_{b}
and
\iota_{d}
and an automorphism
\iota_{c}
of order 4 on the Clifford algebra
C\ell
(which respects the relations (
3.1
)):

\displaystyle\iota_{b}(\psi^{+}_{j+\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle=\psi^{-}_{j-\frac{1}{2}},\quad

\displaystyle\iota_{b}(\psi^{-}_{k-\frac{1}{2}})=\psi^{+}_{k+\frac{1}{2}},

(3.4)

\displaystyle\iota_{c}(\psi^{+}_{j+\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{j}\psi^{-}_{j+\frac{1}{2}},\quad

\displaystyle\iota_{c}(\psi^{-}_{k-\frac{1}{2}})=(-1)^{k}\psi^{+}_{k-\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\iota_{d}(\psi^{+}_{j+\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle=\psi^{-}_{j+\frac{1}{2}},\quad

\displaystyle\iota_{d}(\psi^{-}_{k-\frac{1}{2}})=\psi^{+}_{k-\frac{1}{2}}.

This induces via
r
the following involution on
\overline{a}_{\infty}

\iota_{b}(E_{jk})=-E_{-k,-j},\quad\iota_{c}(E_{jk})=-(-1)^{j+k}E_{-k+1,-j+1},\quad\mbox{and }\iota_{d}(E_{jk})=-E_{-k+1,-j+1},

thus

x_{\infty}=\{g\in a_{\infty}|\iota_{x}(g)=g\}\quad\mbox{for }x=b,\ c\ \mbox{or }d.

Introduce the following elements of
C\ell
:

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{\psi_{i+\frac{1}{2}}^{+}+\psi_{i-\frac{1}{2}}^{-}}{\sqrt{2}}\quad\mbox{for }i\in\mathbb{Z},

(3.5)

\displaystyle\phi_{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{\psi_{i}^{+}+\psi_{i}^{-}}{\sqrt{2}}\quad\mbox{for }i\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}.

They generate the Clifford algebra
C\ell_{x}
, where
x=b
or
d
, subject to
relations

\begin{split}\tilde{\phi}_{i}\tilde{\phi}_{j}+\tilde{\phi}_{j}\tilde{\phi}_{i}=\delta_{i,-j},\ i,j\in\mathbb{Z},\quad\mbox{if }x=b,\\
\phi_{i}\phi_{j}+\phi_{j}\phi_{i}=\delta_{i,-j},\ i,j\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z},\quad\mbox{if }x=d.\end{split}

(3.6)

We define the spin module
F_{x}
over
C\ell_{x}
for
x=b
and
d
, and its dual respectively, generated by the vacuum vector
|0\rangle
(resp.
\langle 0|
), subject to the conditions:

\begin{split}\tilde{\phi}_{j}|0\rangle&=\phi_{j}|0\rangle=0\ \mbox{for }j>0\ (\mbox{resp. }\langle 0|\tilde{\phi}_{j}=\langle 0|\phi_{j}=0\ \mbox{for }j<0),\\
\tilde{\phi}_{0}|0\rangle&=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}|0\rangle\ (\mbox{resp. }\langle 0|\tilde{\phi}_{0}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\langle 0|).\end{split}

(3.7)

The elements
\tilde{\phi}_{j_{1}}\tilde{\phi}_{j_{2}}\cdots\tilde{\phi}_{j_{p}}|0\rangle
(resp.

\phi_{j_{1}}\phi_{j_{2}}\cdots\ \phi_{j_{p}}|0\rangle
)
with
j_{1}<j_{2}<\cdots<j_{p}<0
form a basis of
F_{b}
(resp.
F_{d}
).
We define the spin representation
r_{b}
of
b_{\infty}
in
F_{b}
, and
r_{d}
of
d_{\infty}
in
F_{d}
, by

\displaystyle r_{b}(B_{jk})

\displaystyle=:\tilde{\phi}_{j}\tilde{\phi}_{k}:\ \mbox{for }i,j\in\mathbb{Z},\quad r_{b}(K)=\frac{1}{2}I\ (=\gamma_{b}I),

(3.8)

\displaystyle r_{d}(D_{jk})

\displaystyle=:\phi_{j}\phi_{k}:\ \mbox{for }i,j\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z},\quad r_{d}(K)=\frac{1}{2}I\ (=\gamma_{d}I).

Then
F_{b}
is an irreducible highest weight
b_{\infty}
-module with highest weight vector
|0\rangle
, but
F_{d}
splits into two irreducible highest weight
d_{\infty}
-modules
F_{d}^{\overline{0}}\oplus F_{d}^{\overline{1}}
, defined by the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-gradation given by
{\rm deg}(|0\rangle)=\overline{0}
and
{\rm deg}(\tilde{\phi}_{i})=\overline{1}
. The highest weight vector of
F_{d}^{\overline{0}}
is
|\overline{0}\rangle=|0\rangle
and of
F_{d}^{\overline{1}}
is
|\overline{1}\rangle=\sqrt{2}\tilde{\phi}_{-\frac{1}{2}}|0\rangle
.

The embeddings (
2.23
) of
\hat{so}_{n}
in
d_{\infty}
(resp. (
2.25
)
of
\hat{so}_{2m+1}
in
b_{\infty}
) give, via restriction of the
x_{\infty}
spin modules,
irreducible highest weight representations of
\hat{so}_{n}
with highest weights
\Lambda_{0}
and
\Lambda_{1}
of
F_{d}^{\overline{0}}
, respectively
F_{d}^{\overline{1}}
(resp. an irreducible highest weight representation of
\hat{so}_{2m+1}
with highest weight
\Lambda_{m}
)
[
23
]
,
[
25
]
. Hereafter the
\Lambda_{j}
denote the fundamental weights of
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}
.

Remark 3.1

If we choose another embedding for
\tilde{so}_{2m}
, via the identification

e_{2km+jmn}=t^{-k}v_{j},\quad k\in\mathbb{Z},\ 1\leq j\leq 2m,

we obtain an irreducible representations of
\hat{so}_{2m}
with other highest weights, viz.
\Lambda_{m}
and
\Lambda_{m-1}
of
F_{d}^{\overline{0}}
, respectively
F_{d}^{\overline{1}}
. Since, the description in this case is similar, one can use an outer automorphism of the Lie algebra to interchange these representations with the ones with highest weights
\Lambda_{0}
and
\Lambda_{1}
. Hence, we will not describe this case here.
In the twisted cases, the embeddings (
2.30
) and (
2.31
) in
b_{\infty}
, give an irreducible representation of
\hat{gl}_{2m+1}^{(2)}
, resp.
\hat{so}_{2n}^{(2)}
,
with highest weight
\Lambda_{0}
, resp.
\Lambda_{n-1}
. The embedding (
2.29
) of
\hat{gl}_{2m}^{(2)}
into
d_{\infty}
gives two irreducible highest weight representations of
\hat{gl}_{2m}^{(2)}
with highest weights
\Lambda_{0}
and
\Lambda_{1}
in
F_{d}^{\overline{0}}
, respectively
F_{d}^{\overline{1}}
. Again, as for
\hat{so}_{2m}
, we can define also for
\hat{so}_{2n}^{(2)}
a different embedding, which produces the irreducible representation with highest weight
\Lambda_{0}
. However, also here we can apply an outer automorphism wich interchanges this representation with the representation with highest weight
\Lambda_{n-1}
.

Remark 3.2

The automorphisms
\sigma
, which define the twisted affine lie algebras, induce an automorphism on
x_{\infty}
for
x=b
or
d
.
Viewing
x_{\infty}
as subalgebra of
a_{\infty}
, we find that
\sigma(E_{ij})=JE_{ij}J
for
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{(2)}=\hat{gl}_{n}^{(2)}
, where
J
is given by (
2.32
) and
\sigma(E_{ij})=OE_{ij}O
for
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{(2)}=\hat{so}_{2n}^{(2)}
, where
O
is given by (
2.32
).
We can extend this automorphism to an automorphism on the Clifford algebra
C\ell_{d}
(resp
C\ell_{b}
), which we denote by
\sigma
, viz.
\sigma(\phi_{j})=J\phi_{j}=\sigma_{j}\phi_{j}
, for
j\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
(resp.
\sigma(\tilde{\phi}_{i})=J\phi_{i}=\sigma_{i}\tilde{\phi}_{i}
for
i\in\mathbb{Z}
). Note, that
J
acts as matrix multiplication from the left on a column vector
\phi
. More explicitly,

\displaystyle\hat{gl}_{2m}^{(2)}:

\displaystyle\sigma(\phi_{k(2m)+j-m-\frac{1}{2}})=c_{k}^{2}\phi_{k(2m)+j-m-\frac{1}{2}},\quad k\in\mathbb{Z},\ j=1,\ldots,2n,

\displaystyle\hat{gl}_{2m+1}^{(2)}:

\displaystyle\sigma(\tilde{\phi}_{k(2m+1)+j-m+1})=c_{k}^{2}\tilde{\phi}_{k(2m+1)+j-m+1},\quad k\in\mathbb{Z},\ j=1,\ldots,2n+1,

\displaystyle\hat{so}_{2n}^{(2)}:

\displaystyle\sigma(\tilde{\phi}_{k(2n)+j})=\begin{cases}\tilde{\phi}_{k(2n)+j}&j=1,\ldots,n-1,n+1,\ldots 2n,\\
(-1)^{k}\tilde{\phi}_{k(2n)+n}&j=n,\end{cases}\ k\in\mathbb{Z}.

where the
c_{k}
are given by (
2.28
). Observe, for later calculations that
\sigma_{i}=\sigma_{-i}
This automorphism can be extended to the spin module by
\sigma(|0\rangle)=|0\rangle
, since it leaves the annihilation space of
|0\rangle
(which is the subspace of
\mathbb{C}^{\infty}
consisting of all the elements
\phi
such that
\phi|0\rangle=0
) invariant.

3.3
The Weyl module for
c_{\infty}
(cf.
[
8
]
)

Introduce
free symplectic bosons
b_{i}
,
i\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
, that form the Weyl algebra
C\ell_{c}
with
commutation relations

b_{i}b_{j}-b_{j}b_{i}=(-1)^{i-\frac{1}{2}}\delta_{i,-j},\quad i,j\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}.

Define the space
F_{c}
as an irreducible
C\ell_{c}
-module, generated by the vacuum vector
|0\rangle
(resp. its dual by
\langle 0|
) subject to conditions

b_{j}|0\rangle=0\qquad(\mbox{resp. }\langle 0|b_{-j}=0)\quad\mbox{for }j>0.

The elements

b_{j_{1}}b_{j_{2}}\cdots b_{j_{p}}|0\rangle,\quad\mbox{with }j_{1}\leq j_{2}\leq\cdots j_{p}<0,

form a basis of
F_{c}
. Define a representation of
c_{\infty}
in
F_{c}
, by

r_{c}(C_{ij})=:b_{i}b_{j}:\ \mbox{for }i,j\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z},\quad r_{c}(K)=-\frac{1}{2}I.

(3.9)

As in the case of
F_{d}
, the
c_{\infty}
-module
F_{c}
splits in two irreducible modules with highest weights
-\frac{1}{2}\Lambda_{0}
and
-\frac{3}{2}\Lambda_{0}+\Lambda_{1}
in
F_{c}^{\overline{0}}
, respectively
F_{c}^{\overline{1}}
.
The embedding (
2.27
) gives a highest weight representations of
\hat{sp}_{2n}
with the same highest weight vectors as for
c_{\infty}
.

3.4
Generating fermionic and bosonic fields

In view of Sections 3.1-3.3,
introduce the following generating fields of fermions and symplectic bosons

\displaystyle\psi^{\pm}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}}\psi_{j}^{\pm}z^{-j-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle(\mbox{charged free fermions}),

(3.10)

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\tilde{\phi}_{j}z^{-j}

\displaystyle(\mbox{twisted neutral free fermions}),

\displaystyle b(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}}b_{j}z^{-j-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle(\mbox{neutral free symplectic bosons}),

\displaystyle\phi(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}}\phi_{j}z^{-j-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle(\mbox{neutral free fermions}).

In terms of these fields we can define generating series for the representation of
x_{\infty}
in
F_{x}
for
x=a,b,c,d
, viz.

\displaystyle a_{\infty}:

\displaystyle A(y,z)=\sum_{i,j\in\mathbb{Z}}r_{a}(E_{ij})y^{i-1}z^{-j}=:\psi^{+}(y)\psi^{-}(z):,

(3.11)

\displaystyle b_{\infty}:

\displaystyle B(y,z)=\sum_{i,j\in\mathbb{Z}}r_{b}(B_{ij})y^{-i}z^{-j}=:\tilde{\phi}(y)\tilde{\phi}(z):,

\displaystyle c_{\infty}:

\displaystyle C(y,z)=\sum_{i,j\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}}r_{c}(C_{ij})y^{-i-\frac{1}{2}}z^{-j-\frac{1}{2}}=:b(y)b(z):,

\displaystyle d_{\infty}:

\displaystyle D(y,z)=\sum_{i,j\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}}r_{d}(D_{ij})y^{-i-\frac{1}{2}}z^{-j-\frac{1}{2}}=:\phi(y)\phi(z):.

Unfortunately, the fermionic fields that define
b_{\infty}
and
d_{\infty}
are not so convenient if one wants to express them in terms of vertex operators.
For that reason we define new fields. In the case of
b_{\infty}
and
d_{\infty}
let

\tilde{\phi}^{1}(z)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\tilde{\phi}^{1}_{j}z^{-j},

where

\displaystyle\mbox{for }b_{\infty}:

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}^{1}_{2j}=\tilde{\phi}_{2j},\quad\tilde{\phi}_{2j+1}^{1}=\sqrt{-1}\tilde{\phi}_{2j+1},\quad j\in\mathbb{Z};

(3.12)

\displaystyle\mbox{for }d_{\infty}:

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}^{1}_{0}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\phi_{\frac{1}{2}}+\phi_{-\frac{1}{2}}),\quad\tilde{\phi}_{-2j}^{1}=\phi_{-2j-\frac{1}{2}},\quad\tilde{\phi}_{-2j+1}^{1}=\phi_{-2j+\frac{1}{2}},\quad j\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}.

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{2j}^{1}=\phi_{2j+\frac{1}{2}},\quad\tilde{\phi}^{1}_{2j-1}=\sqrt{-1}\phi_{2j-\frac{1}{2}},\quad(j\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}),\quad\sigma_{0}=\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{\sqrt{2}}(\phi_{\frac{1}{2}}-\phi_{-\frac{1}{2}}).

Then these elements satisfy the anti-commutation relations (
1.6
) of the twisted neutral fermionic field of the Introduction:

\tilde{\phi}^{1}_{j}\tilde{\phi}^{1}_{k}+\tilde{\phi}^{1}_{k}\tilde{\phi}^{1}_{j}=(-1)^{j}\delta_{j,-k},\quad\sigma_{0}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\quad\mbox{and }\sigma_{0}\tilde{\phi}^{1}_{j}+\tilde{\phi}^{1}_{j}\sigma_{0}=0

and

(\tilde{\phi}_{0}^{1}-\sqrt{-1}\sigma_{0})|0\rangle=0,\qquad\tilde{\phi}^{1}_{j}|0\rangle=0,\quad j>0.

(3.13)

Letting
\tilde{\phi}^{1}(z)=\sum_{j\in z}\tilde{\phi}_{j}z^{-j}
, we find the following generating series for the basis of

\displaystyle b_{\infty}:

\displaystyle E^{11}(y,z)=:\tilde{\phi}^{1}(y)\tilde{\phi}^{1}(z):,

\displaystyle d_{\infty}:

\displaystyle E^{11}(y,z)=:\tilde{\phi}^{1}(y)\tilde{\phi}^{1}(z):,\quad\mbox{and }E^{1}(z)=:\tilde{\phi}^{1}(z)\sigma_{0}:.

Note that in the latter case the modes of
E^{11}(y,z)
form a
b_{\infty}
subalgebra inside
d_{\infty}
.

3.5
Multicomponent realizations of
F_{x}
and
x_{\infty}

It is possible to relabel the generators of the Clifford and Weyl algebra to obtain multicomponent realizations of the module
F_{x}
for
x=a,b,c
and
d
.

In the case of
a_{\infty}
, we can relabel in such a way that one obtains instead of one pair of fields
\psi^{\pm}(z)
,
s
pairs of such fields, where
s
is a positive integer.
The simplest such a relabeling is (
1.27
) (cf.
[
15
]
, p. 3253):

\psi^{\pm a}_{j}=\psi^{\pm}_{sj\pm\frac{1}{2}(s-2a+1)},\quad a=1,\ldots,s,

(3.14)

for which the subscripts on the right-hand side are
\equiv\pm\frac{1}{2}(1-2a)
.
Then we
have

\psi^{+a}_{i}\psi^{+b}_{j}+\psi^{+b}_{j}\psi^{+a}_{i}=\psi^{-a}_{i}\psi^{-b}_{j}+\psi^{-b}_{j}\psi^{-a}_{i}=0,\quad\psi^{+a}_{i}\psi^{-b}_{j}+\psi^{-b}_{j}\psi^{-a}_{i}=\delta_{ab}\delta_{i,-j},

(3.15)

and

\psi^{\pm a}_{j}|0\rangle=0\qquad\mbox{for }j>0,\quad a=1,\ldots,s.

(3.16)

We define the corresponding fields by

\psi^{\pm a}(z)=\sum_{j\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}}\psi_{j}^{\pm a}z^{-j-\frac{1}{2}},\qquad a=1,\ldots,s.

(3.17)

As a result, we obtain

s^{2}
generating fields for
a_{\infty}
, namely

A^{ab}(y,z)=:\psi^{+a}(y)\psi^{-b}(z):,\quad\ 1\leq a,b\leq s.

In the cases
x=b
and
d
the multicomponent realization of
F_{x}
and
x_{\infty}

can be given in terms of
s\geq 0
pairs of charged fermionic fields and
r\geq 0
neutral twisted fermionic fields, where
s+r>0
. In addition one needs one fermion
\sigma_{0}
in case
x=b
(resp.
d
) if
r
is even (resp. odd).
We relabel as follows. In principle if we relabel the
\phi_{i}
with
i>0
and the
\tilde{\phi}_{i}
with i
\geq 0
, the ones with a negative index are then fixed. Let’s describe the case that
x=b
. We do the relabeling in blocks first we relabel
2s
fermions to obtain the charged ones as follows

\phi_{(2s+r)+j+1+q}=\psi^{-1}_{j+\frac{1}{2}},\phi_{(2s+r)+j+2+q}=\psi^{+1}_{j+\frac{1}{2}},\phi_{(2s+r)+j+3+q}=\psi^{-2}_{j+\frac{1}{2}},\phi_{(2s+r)+j+4+q}=\psi^{+2}_{j+\frac{1}{2}},\ldots,\phi_{(2s+r)+j+2s+q}=\psi^{+s}_{j+\frac{1}{2}}
. Here
q
is some positive integer which depends on
r
. Then we relabel
r
fermions to obtain
\tilde{\phi}^{s+c}_{j}
with
j>0
. We do this as follows

\tilde{\phi}_{j}^{s+1}={\rm constant}\tilde{\phi}_{(r+2s)j+2s+1+q},\tilde{\phi}_{j}^{s+2}={\rm constant}\tilde{\phi}_{(r+2s)j+2s+2+q},\ldots,\tilde{\phi}_{j}^{s+r}={\rm constant}\tilde{\phi}_{(r+2s)j+2s+r+q}
. We need the constant to get the right commutation relations.
Finally, we want to obtain the
\tilde{\phi}^{r+c}_{0}
’s. Note that we want the following commutation relations for them

(\tilde{\phi}^{r+c}_{0})^{2}=\frac{1}{2}
. There is however only one fermion which has this relation, viz.
\tilde{\phi}_{0}
. So we have to create
r-1
of such fermions. This can be done as follows, we can combine a creation and annihilation fermion
\tilde{\phi}_{-j}
and
\tilde{\phi}_{j}
. Note that we haven’t relabeled the
\tilde{\phi}_{j}
with
1\leq j\leq q
yet .
Let

\tilde{\phi}^{s+1}_{0}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\tilde{\phi}_{1}+\tilde{\phi}_{-1}),\quad\tilde{\phi}^{s+2}_{0}=\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{\sqrt{2}}(\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{-1}),

Then these elements satisfy
(\tilde{\phi}^{s+1}_{0})^{2}=(\tilde{\phi}^{s+2}_{0})^{2}=\frac{1}{2}
. We can do a similar thing for the other
r-3
ones. If
r
is odd, we obtain all the
elements
\tilde{\phi}_{0}^{s+a}
in this way, where in particularly
\tilde{\phi}^{s+r}_{0}=\tilde{\phi}_{0}
. So in this case
q=\frac{r-1}{2}
. If
r
is even, we haven’t relabeled
\tilde{\phi}_{0}
,
we thus set
\sigma_{0}=\tilde{\phi}_{0}
. Note that
q=\frac{r}{2}
in this case. In the
x=d
, we do a similar thing, the role of
r
even and odd is interchanged in this case.

Explicitly, we use the following
relabeling of the generators
\tilde{\phi}_{j}
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}
(resp.
\phi_{j}
,
j\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
) of
C\ell_{b}
(resp.
C\ell_{d}
). For
x=b
we let
r=2q+1
if
r\geq 1
is odd, and
r=2q
if
r\geq 0
is even. and introduce the following generators of
C\ell_{b}
, where
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
1\leq a\leq s
,
1\leq c\leq r
:

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}^{s+b}_{\pm(2j+1)}=\sqrt{-1}\tilde{\phi}_{\pm(2j(2s+r)+q+2s+b)},\quad\tilde{\phi}^{s+b}_{\pm 2j+2}=\tilde{\phi}_{\pm((2j+1)(2s+r)+q+2s+b)},

(3.18)

\displaystyle\psi^{\mp a}_{\pm(j+\frac{1}{2})}=\tilde{\phi}_{\pm(j(2s+r)+q+2a-1)},\quad\psi^{\pm a}_{\pm(j+\frac{1}{2})}=\tilde{\phi}_{\pm(j(2s+r)+q+2a)},

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}^{s+2c-1}_{0}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\tilde{\phi}_{c}+\tilde{\phi}_{-c}),\quad\tilde{\phi}^{s+2c}_{0}=\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{\sqrt{2}}(\tilde{\phi}_{c}-\tilde{\phi}_{-c}),

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}^{s+r}_{0}=\tilde{\phi}_{0},\quad\mbox{if }r=2q+1,\sigma_{0}=\tilde{\phi}_{0},\quad\mbox{if }r=2q.

For
x=d
, we let
r=2q-1
if
r\geq 1
is odd, and
r=2q
if
r\geq 0
is even, and introduce the following generators of
C\ell_{d}
, where
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
1\leq a\leq s
,
1\leq b\leq r
,
1\leq c\leq q
:

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}^{s+b}_{\pm(2j+1)}=\sqrt{-1}\phi_{\pm(2j(2s+r)+q+2s+b-\frac{1}{2})},\quad\tilde{\phi}^{s+b}_{\pm 2j+2}=\phi_{\pm((2j+1)(2s+r)+q+2s+b-\frac{1}{2})},

(3.19)

\displaystyle\psi^{\mp a}_{\pm(j+\frac{1}{2})}=\phi_{\pm(j(2s+r)+q+2a-\frac{3}{2})},\quad\psi^{\pm a}_{\pm(j+\frac{1}{2})}=\phi_{\pm(j(2s+r)+q+2a-\frac{1}{2})},

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}^{s+2c-1}_{0}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\phi_{c-\frac{1}{2}}+\phi_{\frac{1}{2}-c}),\quad\tilde{\phi}^{s+2c}_{0}=\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{\sqrt{2}}(\phi_{c-\frac{1}{2}}-\phi_{\frac{1}{2}-c}),

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}^{s+r}_{0}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\phi_{q-\frac{1}{2}}+\phi_{\frac{1}{2}-q}),\quad\sigma_{0}=\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{\sqrt{2}}(\phi_{q-\frac{1}{2}}-\phi_{\frac{1}{2}-q}),\quad\mbox{if }r=2q-1.

As a result we obtain in both cases
s
pairs of charged fermionic fields

\psi^{\pm a}(z)=\sum_{j\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}}\psi^{\pm a}_{j}z^{-j-\frac{1}{2}},\quad 1\leq a\leq s,

and
r
twisted neutral fields

\tilde{\phi}^{\pm b}(z)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\tilde{\phi}^{b}_{j}z^{-j},\quad s<b\leq r+s.

Besides we have an extra fermion
\sigma_{0}
if
x=b
and
r
is even, and if
x=d
and
r
is odd.

The commutation relations between the charged fermions, as well as their action on the vacuum are (
3.15
) and (
3.16
). The remaining commutation relations are

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}\psi^{\pm a}_{i}+\psi^{\pm a}_{i}\sigma_{0}=\sigma_{0}\tilde{\phi}^{b}_{i}+\tilde{\phi}^{b}_{i}\sigma_{0}=0,\ \sigma_{0}^{2}=\frac{1}{2},\ \psi^{\pm a}_{i}\tilde{\phi}^{b}_{j}+\tilde{\phi}^{b}_{j}\psi^{\pm a}_{i}=0,

(3.20)

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}^{b}_{j}\tilde{\phi}^{c}_{k}+\tilde{\phi}^{c}_{k}\tilde{\phi}^{b}_{=}(-1)^{j}\delta_{bc}\delta_{j,-k},\quad i\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z},\ j,k\in\mathbb{Z},\ 1\leq a\leq s<b,c\leq r+s,

and we have the following action on the vacuum

(\tilde{\phi}_{0}^{s+2k-1}-\sqrt{-1}\tilde{\phi}_{0}^{s+2k})|0\rangle=\tilde{\phi}^{b}_{j}|0\rangle=0,\qquad\mbox{for }j>0,\ 1\leq k\leq\frac{r}{2},

(3.21)

together with

\sigma_{0}|0\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}|0\rangle,\quad\mbox{if }r\ \mbox{even},\quad\tilde{\phi}_{0}^{s+r}|0\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}|0\rangle,\quad\mbox{if }r\ \mbox{odd},

(3.22)

if
x=b

and (cf. (
3.13
)):

(\tilde{\phi}_{0}^{s+r}-\sqrt{-1}\sigma_{0})|0\rangle=0,\quad\mbox{if }r\ \mbox{ is odd},

(3.23)

if
x=d
.

The generating fields for the representation
r_{x}
for
x=b
or
d
then are respectively (
\delta,\epsilon=+
or
-
,
1\leq a,b\leq s<c,d\leq s+r
):

\displaystyle E^{\delta a,\epsilon b}(y,z)=:\psi^{\delta a}(y)\psi^{\epsilon b}(z):,\quad\mbox{if }r>0,

(3.24)

\displaystyle E^{\delta a,c}(y,z)=:\psi^{\delta a}(y)\tilde{\phi}^{c}(z):,\quad\mbox{if }r,s>0,

\displaystyle E^{c,d}(y,z)=:\tilde{\phi}^{c}(y)\tilde{\phi}^{d}(z):,\quad\mbox{if }s>0,

\displaystyle E^{\delta a}(z)=:\psi^{\delta a}(z)\sigma_{0}:,\quad\mbox{if }s>0,\ (x=b\ \mbox{and }r\ \mbox{even})\ \mbox{or }(x=d\ \mbox{and }r\ \mbox{odd}),

\displaystyle E^{c}(z)=:\tilde{\phi}^{c}(z)\sigma_{0}:,\quad\mbox{if }r>0,\ (x=b\ \mbox{and }r\ \mbox{even})\ \mbox{or }(x=d\ \mbox{and }r\ \mbox{odd}).

For
c_{\infty}
, we can choose a different basis of the Weyl algebra such that we get
r\geq 0
neutral symplectic bosonic fields as in the previous section. In analogy with the
x=b,d
case, one can also obtain
s\geq 0
so-called charged symplectic bosonic fields
b^{+a}(z)
and
b^{-a}(z)
, for
1\leq a\leq s
.
One can obtain this for instance by relabeling as follows. If
r
is even (
j>0
,
1\leq a\leq s
,
1\leq c\leq\frac{r}{2}
),

\displaystyle b_{\pm(2j+\frac{1}{2})}^{s+2c-1}=b_{\pm(2j(r+2s)+2c-\frac{3}{2})},\quad b_{\pm(2j+\frac{1}{2})}^{s+2c}=\sqrt{-1}b_{\pm(2j(r+2s)+2c-\frac{1}{2})},

(3.25)

\displaystyle b_{\pm(2j+\frac{3}{2})}^{s+2c-1}=\sqrt{-1}b_{\pm((2j+1)(r+2s)+2c-\frac{3}{2})},\quad b_{\pm(2j+\frac{3}{2})}^{s+2c}=b_{\pm((2j+1)(r+2s)+2c-\frac{1}{2})},

\displaystyle b_{\pm(j+\frac{1}{2})}^{\pm a}=b_{\pm(j(r+2s)+r+2a-\frac{3}{2})},\quad b_{\mp(j+\frac{1}{2})}^{\pm a}=b_{\mp(j(r+2s)+r+2a-\frac{1}{2})}.

If
r
is odd (
j>0
,
1\leq a\leq s
,
1\leq c<\frac{r}{2}
),

\displaystyle b_{\pm(j+\frac{1}{2})}^{s+r}=b_{\pm(j(r+2s)+r-\frac{1}{2})},

(3.26)

\displaystyle b_{\pm(j+\frac{1}{2})}^{s+2c-1}=b_{\pm(j(r+2s)+2c-\frac{3}{2})},\quad b_{\pm(j+\frac{1}{2})}^{s+2c}=\sqrt{-1}b_{\pm(j(r+2s)+2c-\frac{1}{2})},

\displaystyle b_{\pm(2j+\frac{1}{2})}^{\pm a}=\sqrt{-1}b_{\pm(2j(r+2s)+r+2a-\frac{3}{2})},\quad b_{\mp(2j+\frac{3}{2})}^{\pm a}=\sqrt{-1}b_{\mp((2j+1)(r+2s)+r+2a-\frac{3}{2})},

\displaystyle b_{\pm(2j+\frac{1}{2})}^{\mp a}=b_{\pm(2j(r+2s)+r+2a+\frac{1}{2})},\quad b_{\mp(2j+\frac{3}{2})}^{\mp a}=\sqrt{-1}b_{\mp((2j+1)(r+2s)+r+2a+\frac{1}{2})}.

For
1\leq a\leq s
we have

b^{\pm a}(z)=\sum_{i\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}}b^{\pm a}_{i}z^{-i-\frac{1}{2}},\quad b^{c}(z)=\sum_{i\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}}b^{c}_{i}z^{-i-\frac{1}{2}},\quad 1\leq a\leq s<c\leq r+s,

which satisfy

\displaystyle b^{+a}_{i}b^{+c}_{j}-b^{+c}_{j}b^{+a}_{i}=b^{-a}_{i}b^{-c}_{j}-b^{-c}_{j}b^{-a}_{i}=0,\ b^{+a}_{i}b^{-c}_{j}-b^{-c}_{j}b^{-a}_{i}=\delta_{ab}\delta_{i,-j},

(3.27)

\displaystyle b_{i}^{e}b_{j}^{d}-b_{j}^{d}b_{i}^{e}=(-1)^{i-\frac{1}{2}}\delta_{de}\delta_{i,-j},\ b^{\pm a}_{i}b^{d}_{j}-b^{d}_{j}b^{\pm a}_{i}=0,

for
1\leq a,c\leq s<d
,
e\leq s+r,\ i,j\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
.

The action on the vacuum vector is as follows

b_{j}^{\pm a}|0\rangle=b_{j}^{c}|0\rangle=0\quad\mbox{for }j>0.

(3.28)

The generating fields for the representation
r_{c}
then are (
\delta,\epsilon=+
or
-
,
1\leq a,c\leq s<d,e\leq s+r
)

\displaystyle C^{\delta a,\epsilon c}(y,z)=:b^{\delta a}(y)b^{\epsilon c}(y):,\ C^{\delta a,d}(y,z)=:b^{\delta a}(y)b^{d}(z):,\ C^{de}(y,z)=:b^{d}(y)b^{e}(z):.

(3.29)

Remark 3.3

Note that the relabeling that we have used here is different from the relabeling in Section
4
. There the relabeling is induced by the embedding of
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}
and
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{(2)}
into
x_{\infty}
and the element in the Weyl group of
\mathfrak{g}
on which the construction is based. If we would use the relabeling as above, then the construction of Section
4
would not give matrices in
\overline{x}_{\infty}
.

3.6
Vertex operator realizations of
F_{x}

3.6.1
Bosonization of type
a
,
b
and
d

In Section
3.5
we have obtained fermionic fields of two types, viz. the charged fermionic fields
\psi^{\pm a}(z)
,
1\leq a\leq s
, and the twisted neutral fermionic fields
\tilde{\phi}^{b}(z)
,
s<b\leq r+s
, and in some cases an additional operator
\sigma_{0}
. To the charged and twisted neutral fermionic fields, we can associate free bosonic fields

\displaystyle\alpha^{a}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\alpha_{j}^{b}z^{-j-1}=:\psi^{+b}(z)\psi^{-b}(z):,\ 1\leq b\leq s,

(3.30)

\displaystyle\alpha^{s+b}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in 1+2\mathbb{Z}}\alpha_{j}^{s+b}z^{-j}=\frac{1}{2}:\tilde{\phi}^{s+b}(z)\tilde{\phi}^{s+b}(-z):,\ 1\leq b\leq r.

Their modes satisfy the relations of a Heisenberg Lie algebra:

\displaystyle=j\delta_{ab}\delta_{jk}\ 1\leq a\leq s,\ 1\leq b\leq s+r,\ j,k\in\mathbb{Z},\ k\ \mbox{odd }\mbox{if }b>s,

(3.31)

\displaystyle[\alpha^{a}_{j},\alpha^{b}_{k}]

\displaystyle=\frac{j}{2}\delta_{ab}\delta_{jk},\ s+1\leq a\leq r+s,\ 1\leq b\leq s+r,\ j,k\in\mathbb{Z},\ j\ \mbox{odd},\ k\ \mbox{odd }\mbox{if }b>s.

The action on the vacuum vector is

\alpha_{j}^{a}|0\rangle=0,\quad\mbox{for }j\geq 0.

Note that

[\alpha_{i}^{b},\psi^{\pm a}(z)]=\pm\delta_{ab}z^{i}\psi^{\pm a}(z)\quad\mbox{for }i\in\mathbb{Z},\ i\ \mbox{odd }\mbox{if }b>s.

(3.32)

This makes it possible (see
[
22
]
or
[
15
]
) to express these fermionic fields almost completely in terms of the Heisenberg algebra, viz.
for
1\leq a\leq s
we have

\psi^{\pm a}(z)=Q_{a}^{\pm 1}z^{\pm\alpha^{a}_{0}}\exp\left(\mp\sum_{k<0}\frac{\alpha^{a}_{k}}{k}z^{-k}\right)\exp\left(\mp\sum_{k>0}\frac{\alpha^{a}_{k}}{k}z^{-k}\right),

(3.33)

where the operators
Q_{a}
are uniquely defined by

Q_{a}|0\rangle=\psi^{+a}_{-\frac{1}{2}}|0\rangle,\quad Q_{a}\psi^{\pm b}_{i}=(-1)^{\delta_{ab}-1}\psi^{\pm b}_{i\mp\delta_{ab}}Q_{a}.

(3.34)

It is straightforward to check that for
a<b
,
1\leq a,b,c\leq s
we have

Q_{a}Q_{b}=-Q_{b}Q_{a}\quad[\alpha^{a}_{i},Q_{c}]=\delta_{ac}\delta_{i0}Q_{c}.

(3.35)

If
x=a
, we have
s
pairs of charged free fermions.
When
x=b
or
d
we have in addition also
r
twisded neutral free fermionic fields.

For the neutral twisted fields, the commutation relations of the Heisenberg elements
\alpha^{b}_{k}
,
k
odd, with
s<b\leq s+r
:

[\alpha^{b}_{k},\tilde{\phi}^{c}(z)]=\delta_{b,c}z^{k}\tilde{\phi}^{c}(z)\quad\mbox{for }k\in\mathbb{Z}_{odd}.

(3.36)

Thus for
s<b\leq r+s
we have

\tilde{\phi}^{b}(z)=R_{b-s}\exp\left(-2\sum_{k<0,\,{\rm odd}}\frac{\alpha^{b}_{k}}{k}z^{-k}\right)\exp\left(-2\sum_{k>0,\,{\rm odd}}\frac{\alpha^{b}_{k}}{k}z^{-k}\right),

(3.37)

R_{b}|0\rangle=\tilde{\phi}^{s+b}_{0}|0\rangle,\quad\mbox{and }R_{b}R_{c}+R_{c}R_{b}=\delta_{bc},\ R_{b}Q_{a}+Q_{a}R_{b}=0.

(3.38)

If
x=b
and
r
is even, or
x=d
and
r
is odd we have in addition one extra operator
\sigma_{0}
such that
\sigma_{0}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}
.
This operator commutes with all the operators
\alpha^{a}_{k}
, for
1\leq a\leq r+s
of the Heisenberg algebra and it anti-commutes with the operators
Q_{a}
(
1\leq b\leq s
) and
R_{b}
(
s<b\leq r+s
).

If
x=b
and
r
is odd, then
\sigma_{0}|0\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}|0\rangle
. If
x=d
and
r
is even, then
\sigma_{0}|0\rangle=-\sqrt{-1}\tilde{\phi}_{0}^{r+s}|0\rangle
.

The
q
dimension formula for the
a_{\infty}
module
F^{(m)}
is as follows,

\dim_{q}F^{(m)}=\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{2}{j_1, \ldots j_s\in\mathbb Z}{j_1+\cdots+j_s=m}}q^{\frac{j_{1}^{2}+j_{2}^{2}+\cdots+j_{s}^{2}}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(1-q^{i})^{s}}.

(3.39)

In the
b
case, the spin module
F
is irreducible for
b_{\infty}
and in that case, we find the following
q
-dimension formula.

\dim_{q}F=2^{[\frac{r}{2}]}\sum_{{j_{1},\ldots j_{s}\in\mathbb{Z}}}q^{\frac{j_{1}^{2}+j_{2}^{2}+\cdots+j_{s}^{2}}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(1-q^{i})^{s}(1-q^{2i-1})^{r}}.

F
splits in to two irreducible
d_{\infty}
modules
F^{\overline{0}}
and
F^{\overline{1}}
, which both have the same
q
-dimension formula, except when
r=0
. We have

\dim_{q}F^{\overline{\epsilon}}=\begin{cases}2^{[\frac{r-1}{2}]}\sum_{{j_{1},\ldots j_{s}\in\mathbb{Z}}}q^{\frac{j_{1}^{2}+j_{2}^{2}+\cdots+j_{s}^{2}}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(1-q^{i})^{s}(1-q^{2i-1})^{r}},&\mbox{for }r\neq 0,\\
&\\
\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{2}{j_1, \ldots j_s\in\mathbb Z}{j_1+\cdots+j_s\in\epsilon+\mathbb Z}}q^{\frac{j_{1}^{2}+j_{2}^{2}+\cdots+j_{s}^{2}}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(1-q^{i})^{s}},&\mbox{when }r=0.\end{cases}

(3.40)

3.6.2
Bosonization of type
c

Recall that in the multicomponent construction of
C\ell_{c}
, we have two types of symplectic bosonic fields, viz. charged (resp. neutral) fields
b^{\pm a}(z)
,
1\leq a\leq s
(resp.
b^{c}(z)
,
s<c\leq r+s
), satisfying (
3.27
)
and (
3.28
).

\displaystyle\alpha^{a}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\alpha_{j}^{a}z^{-j-1}=:b^{+a}(z)b^{-a}(z):\quad 1\leq a\leq s,

(3.41)

\displaystyle\alpha^{c}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in 1+2\mathbb{Z}}\alpha_{j}^{c}z^{-j}=\frac{1}{2}:b^{c}(z)b^{c}(-z):\quad s<c\leq s+r,

and their modes satisfy the commutation relations of a Heisenberg Lie algebra:

\displaystyle=-j\delta_{ab}\delta_{jk}\ 1\leq a\leq s,\ 1\leq b\leq s+r,\ j,k\in\mathbb{Z},\ k\ \mbox{odd }\mbox{if }b>s,

(3.42)

\displaystyle[\alpha^{a}_{j},\alpha^{b}_{k}]

\displaystyle=-\frac{j}{2}\delta_{ab}\delta_{jk},\ s+1\leq a\leq r+s,\ 1\leq b\leq s+r,\ j,k\in\mathbb{Z},\ j\ \mbox{odd},\ k\ \mbox{odd }\mbox{if }b>s,

\displaystyle\alpha_{j}^{a}|0\rangle

\displaystyle=0,\quad\mbox{for }j\geq 0.

We start with the charged fields.
The commutation relations can be rewritten as

b^{\pm a}(y)b^{\pm c}(z)-b^{\pm c}(z)b^{\pm a}(y)=0,\quad b^{+a}(y)b^{-c}(z)-b^{-c}(z)b^{+a}(y)=\delta_{ac}\delta(y-z).

Then

[\alpha^{i}_{k},b^{\pm a}(z)]=\mp z^{k}\delta_{i,a}b^{\pm a}(z),\quad\mbox{for }k\in\mathbb{Z},

(3.43)

hence,

b^{\pm a}(z)=E_{-}^{a}(z)^{\pm 1}Q^{\pm a}(z)E_{+}^{a}(z)^{\pm 1}

(3.44)

where

E_{-}^{a}(z)=\exp\left(-\sum_{k<0}\frac{\alpha^{a}_{k}}{k}z^{-k}\right),\qquad E_{+}^{a}(z)=\exp\left(-\sum_{k>0}\frac{\alpha^{a}_{k}}{k}{z^{-k}}\right),

(3.45)

and

[\alpha_{k}^{i},Q^{\pm a}(z)]=\mp\delta_{i,a}\delta_{k,0}Q^{\pm a}(z)\quad\mbox{for }k\in\mathbb{Z}.

Following
[
1
]
, we set

\Theta^{\pm a}(z)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\Theta^{\pm a}_{k}z^{-k-1}:=E_{-}^{a}(z)^{\mp 1}b^{\pm a}(z)z^{\mp\alpha_{0}^{a}}E_{+}^{a}(z)^{\mp 1},

(3.46)

Then

\Theta^{\pm a}(z)|0\rangle=E_{-}^{a}(z)^{\mp 1}\sum_{i>0}b^{\pm a}_{-i}|0\rangle z^{i-\frac{1}{2}}\in F_{c}[[z]],

hence,

\Theta^{\pm a}_{k}|0\rangle=0\quad\mbox{for }k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}.

Lemma 3.4

Let
i_{z,y}R(y,z)
denote the power series expansion of a rational function
R(y,z)
in the domain
|z|>|y|
and
let
\lambda,\mu=\pm 1
. Then

\displaystyle E_{-}^{a}(y)^{\lambda}E_{+}^{b}(z)^{\mu}

\displaystyle=i_{z,y}(1-\frac{y}{z})^{\lambda\mu\delta_{ac}}E_{+}^{b}(z)^{\mu}E_{-}^{a}(y)^{\lambda},

\displaystyle E_{+}^{a}(z)b^{\pm c}(y)

\displaystyle=i_{z,y}(1-\frac{y}{z})^{\mp\delta_{ac}}b^{\pm c}(y)E_{+}^{a}(z),

\displaystyle E_{-}^{a}(y)b^{\pm c}(z)

\displaystyle=i_{z,y}(1-\frac{y}{z})^{\pm\delta_{ac}}b^{\pm c}(z)E_{-}^{a}(y),

\displaystyle[\alpha^{i}(y),\theta^{\pm a}(z)]

\displaystyle=\mp\frac{\delta_{i,a}}{y}\theta^{\pm a}(z).

Proof
Let
|z|>|y|
, then

\displaystyle E_{-}^{a}(y)^{\lambda}E_{+}^{b}(z)^{\mu}

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{\lambda\mu}{k^{2}}\left(\frac{y}{z}\right)^{k}[\alpha^{a}_{-k},\alpha^{b}_{k}]\right)E_{+}^{b}(z)^{\mu}E_{-}^{a}(y)^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\delta_{ab}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{\lambda\mu}{k}\left(\frac{y}{z}\right)^{k}\right)E_{+}^{b}(z)^{\mu}E_{-}^{a}(y)^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=i_{z,y}(1-\frac{y}{z})^{\lambda\mu\delta_{ab}}E_{+}^{b}(z)^{\mu}E_{-}^{a}(y)^{\lambda}.

Next, since

\alpha^{a}_{k}b^{\pm c}(z)=b^{\pm c}(z)(\alpha^{a}_{k}\mp\delta_{ac}z^{k}),\quad\mbox{for }k\in\mathbb{Z},

also

\displaystyle E_{+}^{a}(z)b^{\pm c}(y)

\displaystyle=b^{\pm c}(y)\exp\left(-\sum_{k>0}\frac{1}{k}{z^{-k}}(\alpha^{a}_{k}\mp\delta_{ac}y^{k})\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\pm\delta_{ac}\sum_{k>0}\frac{1}{k}\left(\frac{y}{z}\right)^{k}\right)b^{\pm c}(y)E_{+}^{a}(z)

\displaystyle=i_{z,y}(1-\frac{y}{z})^{\mp\delta_{ac}}b^{\pm c}(y)E_{+}^{a}(z).

The last identity follows from (
3.43
).
\square

Using this lemma and the expression (
3.46
), we find that for
\lambda=\pm
we have

\displaystyle\Theta^{+a}(y)\Theta^{\lambda b}(z)

\displaystyle=i_{y,z}(y-z)^{\lambda\delta_{ab}}E_{-}^{a}(y)^{-1}E_{-}^{b}(z)^{-\lambda 1}b^{+a}(y)b^{\lambda b}(z)y^{-\alpha_{0}^{a}}z^{-\lambda\alpha_{0}^{b}}E_{+}^{a}(y)^{-1}E_{+}^{b}(z)^{-\lambda 1}

\displaystyle=i_{y,z}(y-z)^{\lambda\delta_{ab}}E_{-}^{a}(y)^{-1}E_{-}^{b}(z)^{-\lambda 1}(:b^{+a}(y)b^{\lambda b}(z):+

\displaystyle+\delta_{ab}\delta_{\lambda,-}i_{y,z}(y-z)^{\lambda 1})y^{-\alpha_{0}^{a}}z^{-\lambda\alpha_{0}^{b}}E_{+}^{a}(y)^{-1}E_{+}^{b}(z)^{-\lambda 1},

\displaystyle\Theta^{\lambda b}(z)\Theta^{+a}(y)

\displaystyle=i_{z,y}(z-y)^{\lambda\delta_{ab}}E_{-}^{a}(y)^{-1}E_{-}^{b}(z)^{-\lambda 1}(:b^{\lambda b}(z)b^{+a}(y):

\displaystyle-\delta_{ab}\delta_{\lambda,-}i_{z,y}(z-y)^{\lambda 1})y^{-\alpha_{0}^{a}}z^{-\lambda\alpha_{0}^{b}}E_{+}^{a}(y)^{-1}E_{+}^{b}(z)^{-\lambda 1}.

From (
3.43
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\Theta^{+a}(y)\Theta^{+b}(z)-(-1)^{\delta_{ab}}\theta^{+b}(z)\Theta^{+a}(y)

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\Theta^{+a}(y)\Theta^{-b}(z)-\Theta^{-b}(z)\Theta^{+a}(y)

\displaystyle=0,\quad\mbox{if }a\neq b,

and

\displaystyle\Theta^{+a}(y)\Theta^{-a}(z)+\Theta^{-a}(z)\Theta^{+a}(y)=

(3.47)

\displaystyle E_{-}^{a}(y)^{-1}E_{-}^{a}(z)(\delta(y-z):b^{+a}(y)b^{-a}(z):+\partial_{z}\delta(y-z))E_{+}^{a}(y)^{-1}E_{+}^{a}(z)y^{-\alpha_{0}^{a}}z^{\alpha_{0}^{a}}=

\displaystyle\delta(y-z)\alpha^{a}(z)+\partial_{z}\delta(y-z)-\delta(y-z)\alpha^{a}(z)

\displaystyle=\partial_{z}\delta(y-z).

Here we have used Taylor’s formula. In a similar way one can prove that

\Theta^{-a}(y)\Theta^{-b}(z)-(-1)^{\delta_{ab}}\Theta^{-b}(z)\Theta^{-a}(y)=0.

(3.48)

Note that still

[\alpha_{k}^{a},\Theta^{\pm b}(z)]=\mp\delta_{ab}\delta_{k0}\Theta^{\pm b}(z).

(3.49)

For convenience of counting the energy degree, we introduce an operator
Q_{a}
by setting
\Theta^{\pm a}=Q_{a}^{\mp 1}\theta^{\pm a}(z)
, where
[\alpha_{0},Q_{a}]=Q_{a}
, then
\theta^{\pm a}(z)
satisfies the same commutation relations as

\Theta^{\pm a}(z)
, the only difference is that
[\alpha_{k}^{a},\theta^{\pm b}(z)]=0
.
Thus,

b^{\pm a}(z)=Q_{a}^{\mp 1}z^{\pm\alpha_{0}^{a}}E_{-}^{a}(z)^{\pm 1}\theta^{\pm a}(z)E_{+}^{a}(z)^{\mp 1}.

(3.50)

This last formula was already present in
[
10
]
in the situation when there is only one such pair of symplectic boson fields.
Note that
Q_{a}^{\pm 1}
is an operator that always appears together with each
\theta^{\pm a}_{j}
. In principle we do not need to introduce it, but for the moment this operator will be convenient when calculating the energy decomposition of
F
. We will show this in the next paragraph. Note also that
Q_{a}b^{\pm c}(z)=z^{\pm\delta_{ac}}b^{\pm c}(z)Q_{a}
.

Assume for the moment that there is only one such pair
b^{\pm}(z)
.
Then

(b^{\pm}_{-\frac{1}{2}})^{n}|0\rangle
, which has energy
\frac{n}{2}
, is equal to the constant coefficient of
b^{\pm}(z_{n})\cdots b^{\pm}(z_{1})|0\rangle
. Using
(
3.50
), this is equal to

\theta^{\pm}_{-n}\theta^{\pm}_{1-n}\cdots\theta^{\pm}_{-2}\theta^{\pm}_{-1}Q^{\mp n}|0\rangle.

(3.51)

More generally the energy of

\theta^{-}_{-j_{m}}\cdots\theta^{-}_{-j_{1}}\theta^{+}_{-k_{n}}\cdots\theta^{+}_{-k_{1}}Q^{m-n}|0\rangle,\quad\mbox{where $j_{m}>\cdots j_{1}>0$ and $k_{n}>\cdots>k_{1}>0$, }

(3.52)

is equal to

j_{1}+\cdots+j_{m}+k_{1}+\cdots+k_{n}-\frac{(m-n)^{2}}{2}.

(3.53)

This can be seen as follows. Consider, the element

b^{-}_{m-n-j_{m}+\frac{1}{2}}\cdots b^{-}_{-n-j_{1}+\frac{1}{2}}b^{+}_{n-k_{n}-\frac{1}{2}}\cdots b^{+}_{-k_{1}+\frac{1}{2}}|0\rangle,

which, clearly has energy (
3.53
). It is the coefficient of

y_{m}^{j_{m}+n-m}\cdots y_{2}^{j_{2}+n-2}y_{1}^{j_{1}+n-1}z_{n}^{k_{n}-n}\cdots z_{2}^{k_{2}-2}z_{1}^{k_{1}-1}

in

\displaystyle b^{-}(y_{m})\cdots b^{-}(y_{1})b^{+}(z_{n})\cdots b^{+}(z_{1})|0\rangle=

\displaystyle z_{2}^{-1}z_{3}^{-2}z_{n}^{-n-1}y_{1}^{n}y_{2}^{n-1}\cdots y_{m}^{n-m+1}\theta^{-}(y_{m})\cdots\theta^{-}(y_{1})\theta^{+}(z_{n})\theta^{+}(z_{1})Q^{m-n}|0\rangle+\cdots=

\displaystyle y_{m}^{j_{m}+n-m}\cdots y_{1}^{j_{1}+n-1}z_{n}^{k_{n}-n}\cdots z_{1}^{k_{1}-1}\theta^{+}_{-j_{m}}\cdots\theta^{+}_{-j_{1}}\theta^{+}_{-k_{n}}\cdots\theta^{+}_{-k_{1}}Q^{m-n}|0\rangle+\cdots\

Hence,

{\rm energy}(\theta^{\pm}_{-j})=j,\quad{\rm energy}(Q^{m}|0\rangle)=-\frac{m^{2}}{2},\qquad j,m\in\mathbb{Z},\ j>0.

An element of the form (
3.52
) can be obtained by action of several elements of the form

\theta_{i}^{\pm}\theta_{k}^{\mp}

on an element (
3.51
).

Lemma 3.5

Let

F_{ann}=\{w\in F|\,\alpha_{k}w=0,\ \mbox{ for all }k>0\}.

Then the elements (
3.52
) form a basis for
F_{ann}
.

Proof

It suffices to consider a linear combination of elements of the form (
3.51
), where the energy, say
N
, is fixed and where the charge
m-n
, the eigenvalue of
\alpha_{0}
, is fixed. Since the energy is
N
, there can only be a finite number of elements of the form (
3.52
) which have this energy. Now let

\sum_{i=1}^{p}\lambda_{i}\theta^{-}_{-j_{m_{i}}^{i}}\cdots\theta^{-}_{-j_{1}^{i}}\theta^{+}_{-k_{n_{i}}^{i}}\cdots\theta^{+}_{-k_{1}^{i}}Q^{m-n}|0\rangle=0,

(3.54)

where we take a linear combination of all elements wich have energy
N
and charge
m-n
. If we compare two elements in this sum, then there always exists a pair
\theta^{\pm}_{a}
and
\theta^{\pm}_{b}
, such that one appears in one of the two elements and is missing in the other, and vice versa. Hence, we can show that the coefficient
\lambda_{i}=0
, by letting

\theta^{-}_{-j_{m_{i}}^{i}}\theta^{+}_{j_{m_{i}}^{i}}\cdots\theta^{-}_{-j_{1}^{i}}\theta^{+}_{j_{1}^{i}}\theta^{+}_{-k_{n_{i}}^{i}}\theta^{-}_{k_{n_{i}}^{i}}\cdots\theta^{+}_{-k_{1}^{i}}\theta^{-}_{k_{1}^{i}}

act on this sum, which kills of all terms except for
\lambda_{i}\theta^{-}_{-j_{m_{i}}^{i}}\cdots\theta^{-}_{-j_{1}^{i}}\theta^{+}_{-k_{n_{i}}^{i}}\cdots\theta^{+}_{-k_{1}^{i}}
. Hence, elements of the form (
3.52
) form a basis of
F_{ann}
.
\square

So using (
3.53
), we have the following:

Corollary 3.6

The

q,t
-dimension of
F_{ann}
is equal to

\displaystyle\sum_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}t~^{m-n}q^{-\frac{(m-n)^{2}}{2}}s_{m}(q)s_{n}(q),

(3.55)

where
s_{m}(q)
is the power series in
q
, which is defined as the coefficient of
s^{m}
in the expansion of
\prod_{j=1}^{\infty}(1+sq^{j})=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}s_{m}(q)s^{m}
.

Note that the coefficients of
q^{k}
in
s_{m}(q)
are the possible ways that the number
k
can be written as the sum of
m
distinct positive integers.
It is well known that

s_{m}(q)=\frac{q^{\frac{m^{2}+m}{2}}}{(1-q)\cdots(1-q^{m})}.

Thus we find that

\dim_{q,t}F=\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}\frac{1}{1-q^{i}}\sum_{j,k=0}^{\infty}\frac{q^{\frac{k}{2}}t^{k}}{(1-q)\cdots(1-q^{k})}q^{jk}\frac{q^{\frac{j}{2}}t^{-j}}{(1-q)\cdots(1-q^{j})}.

(3.56)

On the other hand, by looking at the energy and charge of
F
in terms of the symplectic bosons
b^{\pm}_{j}
, we find that

\dim_{q,t}F=\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}\frac{1}{(1-tq^{i-\frac{1}{2}})(1-t^{-1}q^{i-\frac{1}{2}})}.

(3.57)

This means that the right-hand side of (
3.57
) is equal to the right-hand side of (
3.56
), which gives a remarkable identity, which we could not find in the literature. In
[
1
]
, Section 3, and
[
11
]
, Section 7.2 and the Appendix, there were some other formula’s for the q-dimensions of symplectic bosons.

Next, we want to describe the neutral fields, see
[
1
]
or
[
26
]
for more details.
The commutation relations can be rewritten to

b^{a}(y)b^{c}(z)-b^{c}(z)b^{a}(y)=\delta_{ac}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{y}\left(-\frac{y}{z}\right)^{k}=\delta_{ac}\delta(y+z).

Since for
a>s
,

[\alpha_{k}^{a},b^{c}(z)]=-\delta_{ac}z^{k}b^{c}(z),\quad[\alpha_{k}^{a},\alpha_{\ell}^{c}]=-\frac{\delta_{ac}}{2}k,\quad\mbox{for }k\in 1+2\mathbb{Z},\ \ell\in\mathbb{Z},\ \mbox{odd if }c>s.

We define

\Theta^{a}(z)=\sum_{k\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}}\theta^{a}_{k}z^{-k-\frac{1}{2}}:=V_{-}^{a}(z)^{-1}b^{a}(z)V_{+}^{a}(z)^{-1},

where (cf. (
1.47
))

V_{-}^{a}(z)=\exp\left(-2\sum_{k<0,\,\rm odd}\frac{\alpha^{a}_{k}}{k}z^{-k}\right),\qquad V_{+}^{a}(z)=\exp\left(-2\sum_{k>0,\,\rm odd}\frac{\alpha^{a}_{k}}{k}{z^{-k}}\right),

(3.58)

Then

\Theta^{a}(z)|0\rangle=V_{-}^{a}(z)^{-1}\sum_{i>0}b^{a}_{-i}z^{i-\frac{1}{2}}|0\rangle\in F_{c}[[z]],

hence

\theta^{a}_{k}|0\rangle=0\quad\mbox{for }k\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}.

In a similar way as before, see
[
26
]
for details (note
\alpha_{k}=-J_{k}
),
we obtain that

\Theta^{a}(y)\Theta^{b}(-z)-(-1)^{\delta_{ab}}\Theta^{b}(-z)\Theta^{a}(y)=2\delta_{ab}z^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{z}(z^{\frac{1}{2}}\delta(y-z)).

Note that we also have

\Theta^{\pm a}(y)\Theta^{b}(z)-\Theta^{b}(z)\Theta^{\pm a}(y)=0\quad\mbox{for }a\leq s,\ b>s.

We thus have

Proposition 3.7

For
a\leq s
and
j>s
we have

\displaystyle b^{\pm a}(z)=

\displaystyle Q_{a}^{\pm 1}z^{\pm\alpha_{0}^{a}}E_{-}^{a}(z)^{\pm 1}\theta^{\pm a}(z)E_{+}^{a}(z)^{\mp 1}=E_{-}^{a}(z)^{\pm 1}\Theta^{\pm a}(z)E_{+}^{a}(z)^{\mp 1}z^{\pm\alpha_{0}^{a}},

(3.59)

\displaystyle b^{j}(z)=

\displaystyle V_{-}^{j}(z)\Theta^{j}(z)V_{+}^{j}(z),

with
E_{\pm}^{a}(z)
(
V_{\pm}^{j}(z)
) given by (
3.45
) (resp. (
4.39
)), such that the last equation of Lemma
3.4
holds. The modes of the fields

\theta^{\pm a}(z)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\theta^{\pm a}_{k}z^{-k-1}
and

\Theta^{j}(z)=\sum_{k\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}}\theta^{j}_{k}z^{-k-\frac{1}{2}}
satisfy the following (anti-)commutation relations

\displaystyle\theta^{\pm a}_{k}\theta^{\pm b}_{\ell}-\theta^{\pm b}_{\ell}\theta^{\pm a}_{k}=\theta^{\pm a}_{k}\theta^{j}_{\ell}-\theta^{j}_{\ell}\theta^{\pm a}_{k}=0,

\displaystyle\theta^{+a}_{k}\theta^{-b}_{\ell}-(-1)^{\delta_{ab}}\theta^{-b}_{\ell}\theta^{+a}_{k}=\delta_{ab}\delta_{k,-\ell}k,

\displaystyle\theta^{i}_{k}\theta^{j}_{\ell}-(-1)^{\delta_{ij}}\theta^{j}_{\ell}\theta^{i}_{k}=(-1)^{k-\frac{1}{2}}\delta_{ij}\delta_{k,-\ell}2k.

Remark 3.8

In
[
1
]
I. Anguelova bosonizes the fields
\Theta^{\pm a}(z)
further, following an idea of
[
10
]
. We will not do that here, but continue to work with the fields
\Theta^{\pm a}(z)
and find a realization for them, more or less as in
[
14
]
.

The Weyl module
F
splits in two irreducible
c_{\infty}
modules
F^{\overline{\epsilon}}
,
{\overline{\epsilon}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}

The
q
-dimension formula can be most conveniently described by using the variable
x
such that
x^{2}=1
. We have
s
(resp.
r
) charged (resp. neutral) symplectic bosonic fields. Therefore we combine
s
copies of (
3.56
) and
r
copies copies of (
1.51
), which gives

\displaystyle\dim_{q}F^{\overline{0}}+xF^{\overline{1}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(1-xq^{j-\frac{1}{2}})^{r+2s}}

(3.60)

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{j,k=0}^{\infty}\frac{q^{\frac{k}{2}}x^{k}}{(1-q)\cdots(1-q^{k})}q^{jk}\frac{q^{\frac{j}{2}}x^{j}}{(1-q)\cdots(1-q^{j})}\right)^{s}\times

\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{(1+xq^{j-\frac{1}{2}})^{r}}{(1-q^{j})^{s}(1-q^{2j-1})^{r}}.

3.7
The bosonic realization of
F_{x}

3.7.1
The bosonic realization of
F_{x}
, the Clifford algebra case

In Subsection
3.6.1
we have found bosonizations of the multicomponent fermionic fields that generate the Clifford algebra
C\ell_{x}
for
x=a,b,d
and described the spin module
F_{x}
. The description was based on the Heisenberg algebra and some additional operators
Q_{a}
and
R_{b}
.
In this subsection we will give the corresponding bosonic description of
F_{x}
, using the Fock space representation of the Heisenberg Lie algebra:

\alpha_{k}^{j}=\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{(j)}_{k}},\quad\alpha_{-k}^{j}=kt_{k}^{(j)},\quad k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0},\ 1\leq j\leq s,

(3.61)

for the charged fields and

\alpha_{k}^{j}=\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{(j)}_{k}},\quad\alpha_{-k}^{j}=\frac{k}{2}t_{k}^{(j)},\quad\ k\in\mathbb{Z}_{odd>0},\quad s<j\leq r+s,\\

(3.62)

for the neutral fields.

For
x=a
, we let

B_{s}=\mathbb{C}[q_{j},q_{j}^{-1},t_{1}^{(j)},t_{2}^{(j)},\ldots|\,1\leq j\leq s].

Recalling the operators
Q_{j}
on
F_{a}
, defined by (
3.34
),
we define an isomorphism
\sigma:F_{a}\to B_{s}
by the following properties

\sigma(Q_{1}^{k_{1}}Q_{2}^{k_{2}}\cdots Q_{s}^{k_{s}}|0\rangle)=q_{1}^{k_{1}}q_{2}^{k_{2}}\cdots q_{s}^{k_{s}},

(3.63)

and

\displaystyle\sigma r(\alpha_{0}^{j})\sigma^{-1}=q_{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial q_{j}},\quad\sigma r(\alpha_{k}^{j})\sigma^{-1}=\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{(j)}_{k}},\quad\sigma r(\alpha_{-k}^{j})\sigma^{-1}=kt_{k}^{(j)},\quad k>0,

(3.64)

see
[
22
]
or
[
15
]
, for more details. One also has

\sigma Q_{j}\sigma^{-1}=(-1)^{\sum_{k=1}^{j-1}q_{k}\frac{\partial}{\partial q_{k}}}q_{j}.

(3.65)

Thus substituting (
3.64
) in (
3.33
), we obtain (
1.32
).
It is straightforward to check (see
[
22
]
or
[
15
]
) that for
1\leq a,b\leq s
, with
a\neq b
:

\displaystyle\sigma E^{+a,-b}(w,z)\sigma^{-1}=

\displaystyle\sigma:\psi^{+a}(w)\psi^{-b}(z):\sigma^{-1}

(3.66)

\displaystyle=q_{a}(-1)^{\sum_{\ell=1}^{a-1}q_{\ell}\frac{\partial}{\partial q_{\ell}}}

\displaystyle q_{b}^{-1}(-1)^{\sum_{\ell=1}^{b-1}q_{\ell}\frac{\partial}{\partial q_{\ell}}}w^{q_{a}\frac{\partial}{\partial q_{a}}}z^{-q_{b}\frac{\partial}{\partial q_{b}}}e^{z\cdot t^{(a)}-w\cdot t^{(b)}}e^{w^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(b)}}-z^{-1}\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(a)}}},

\displaystyle\sigma E^{+a,-a}(w,z)\sigma^{-1}=

\displaystyle\sigma:\psi^{+a}(w)\psi^{-a}(z):\sigma^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i_{w,z}\frac{1}{w-z}\big[\left(\frac{w}{z}\right)^{q_{a}\frac{\partial}{\partial q_{a}}}e^{z\cdot t^{(a)}-w\cdot t^{(a)}}e^{w^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(a)}}-z^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(a)}}}-1\big].

For
x=b
or
d
, for which there is in general a combination of
r
neutral and
s
charged fields, we have to extend the Fock space
B_{s}
by
p
variables
\theta_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq p
, for
r=2p
or
r=2p+1
, in the
b
-case, and
r=2p
or
r=2p-1
in the
d
-case,
which anticommute:

\theta_{i}\theta_{j}=-\theta_{j}\theta_{i}
.
So for
x=b
or
d
we let

B_{s,r}=\mathbb{C}[\theta_{i},q_{j},q_{j}^{-1},t^{(\ell)}|\,1\leq i\leq p,\ 1\leq j\leq s,\ 1\leq\ell\leq s+r],

(3.67)

where
t^{(\ell)}=(t^{(\ell)}_{1},t^{(\ell)}_{2},t^{(\ell)}_{3},\ldots)
if
1\leq\ell\leq s
and
t^{(\ell)}=(t^{(\ell)}_{1},t^{(\ell)}_{3},t^{(\ell)}_{5},\ldots)
if
\ell>s
.
We define a vector space isomorphism
\sigma:F_{x}\to B_{s,r}
by

\displaystyle\sigma(Q_{1}^{a_{1}}\cdots Q_{s}^{a_{s}}R_{1}^{b_{1}}\cdots R_{\tilde{r}}^{b_{\tilde{r}}}|0\rangle)=q_{1}^{a_{1}}q_{2}^{a_{2}}\cdots q_{s}^{a_{s}}\left(R_{1}(\theta)\right)^{b_{1}}\left(R_{2}(\theta)\right)^{b_{2}}\cdots\left(R_{\tilde{r}}(\theta)\right)^{b_{\tilde{r}}}1,

where

\tilde{r}=r
in the
b
-case and
\tilde{r}=r
if
r
is even and
\tilde{r}=r+1
if
r
is odd in the
d
-case, and

R_{2j-1}(\theta):=\frac{\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta_{j}}+\theta_{j}}{\sqrt{2}},\quad R_{2j}(\theta):=\sqrt{-1}\frac{\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta_{j}}-\theta_{j}}{\sqrt{2}},\quad 1\leq j\leq p.

(3.68)

Then
\sigma r_{x}(\alpha_{k}^{j})\sigma^{-1}
, for
j\leq s
, can be realized as in
(
3.61
), and for
j>s
we set:

\sigma r_{x}(\alpha_{k}^{j})\sigma^{-1}=\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{(j)}_{k}},\quad\sigma r(\alpha_{-k}^{j})\sigma^{-1}=\frac{k}{2}t_{k}^{(j)},\quad j>s,\ k>0.\\

(3.69)

The action of
Q_{j}
is is given by (
3.65
).
The action of
R_{j}
for
1\leq j\leq 2p
is given by

\displaystyle\sigma R_{j}\sigma^{-1}=(-1)^{\sum_{k=1}^{s}q_{k}\frac{\partial}{\partial q_{k}}}R_{j}(\theta),

(3.70)

where
R_{j}(\theta)
is given by (
3.68
). For
b_{\infty}
and
r
odd, we also have

R_{r}(\theta)=(-1)^{\sum_{j=1}^{p}\theta_{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta_{j}}}

Thus, as before,
\sigma\psi^{\pm j}(z)\sigma^{-1}
, for
1\leq j\leq r
, is given by
(
1.32
).
For
1\leq j\leq r
(see e.g.
[
16
]
or
[
25
]
) we have that
\sigma\tilde{\phi}^{s+j}(z)\sigma^{-1}
is given by

\sigma\tilde{\phi}^{s+b}(z)\sigma_{s,r}^{-1}=(-1)^{\sum_{j=1}^{s}q_{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial q_{j}}}R_{b}(\theta)e^{z\circ t^{(s+b)}}e^{-2(z^{-1}\circ\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(s+b)}})}.

(3.71)

Finally, if
r=2p
is even for
b_{\infty}
or
r=2p-1
is odd for
d_{\infty}
,
there is also an operator
\sigma_{0}
. One has

\sigma\sigma_{0}\sigma^{-1}=S(\theta)=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(-1)^{\sum_{k=1}^{s}q_{k}\frac{\partial}{\partial q_{k}}+\sum_{j=1}^{p}\theta_{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta_{j}}},&x=b,\\
\sqrt{-1}(-1)^{\sum_{k=1}^{s}q_{k}\frac{\partial}{\partial q_{k}}}\frac{\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta_{p}}-\theta_{p}}{\sqrt{2}},&x=d.\end{cases}

(3.72)

Using the results of Subsection
3.6.1
we obtain a representation of
b_{\infty}
or
d_{\infty}
on the Fock space
B_{r,s}
. It is straightforward to check that for
1\leq a,b\leq s
, the equations (
3.66
) still hold.
Let
a,d\leq s
and
1\leq b,c\leq r
, then

\displaystyle\sigma E^{\pm a,\pm d}(w,z)\sigma^{-1}=\sigma:\psi^{\pm a}(w)\psi^{\pm d}(z):\sigma^{-1}=

(3.73)

\displaystyle=(w-z)^{\delta_{ad}}q_{a}^{\pm 1}(-1)^{\sum_{\ell=1}^{a-1}q_{\ell}\frac{\partial}{\partial q_{\ell}}}q_{d}^{\pm 1}(-1)^{\sum_{\ell=1}^{d-1}q_{\ell}\frac{\partial}{\partial q_{\ell}}}w^{\pm q_{a}\frac{\partial}{\partial q_{a}}}z^{-\pm q_{d}\frac{\partial}{\partial q_{d}}}\times

\displaystyle e^{\pm(z\cdot t^{(a)}+w\cdot t^{(b)})}e^{\mp(z^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(a)}}+w^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(b)}})},

\displaystyle\sigma E^{s+b,s+c}(w,z)\sigma^{-1}=\sigma:\tilde{\phi}^{s+b}(w)\tilde{\phi}^{s+c}(z):\sigma^{-1}

\displaystyle=i_{w,z}\left(\frac{w-z}{w+z}\right)^{\delta_{bc}}\big[R_{b}(\theta)R_{c}(\theta)e^{(z\circ t^{(s+b)}+w\circ t^{(s+c)})}e^{-2(z^{-1}\circ\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(s+b)}}+w^{-1}\circ\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(s+c)}})}-\delta_{bc}\big],

\displaystyle\sigma E^{\pm a,s+b}(w,z)\sigma^{-1}=\sigma:\psi^{\pm a}(w)\tilde{\phi}^{s+b}(z):\sigma^{-1}=

\displaystyle=q_{a}(-1)^{\sum_{i=a}^{s}q_{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial q_{i}}}w^{\pm q_{a}\frac{\partial}{\partial q_{a}}}R_{b}(\theta)e^{\pm z\cdot t^{(a)}+w\circ t^{(s+b)}}e^{\mp z^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(a)}}-2(w^{-1}\circ\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(s+b)}})}.

If
\sigma_{0}
is present, we also have

\displaystyle\sigma E^{\pm a}(z)\sigma^{-1}=\sigma:\psi^{\pm a}(z)\sigma_{0}:\sigma^{-1}=\ q_{a}^{\pm 1}(-1)^{\sum_{\ell=1}^{a-1}q_{\ell}\frac{\partial}{\partial q_{\ell}}}S(\theta)z^{\pm q_{a}\frac{\partial}{\partial q_{a}}}e^{\pm z\cdot t^{(a)}}e^{\mp z^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(a)}}},

(3.74)

\displaystyle\sigma E^{s+b}(z)\sigma^{-1}=\sigma:\tilde{\phi}^{s+b}(z)\sigma_{0}:\sigma^{-1}=R_{b}(\theta)S(\theta)e^{z\circ t^{(s+b)}}e^{-2(z^{-1}\circ\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(s+b)}})},

where
S(\theta)
is given by (
3.72
).

3.7.2
The bosonic realization of
F_{c}
, the Weyl algebra case

We use the results of Subsection

3.6.2
to obtain a bosonic realization of
F_{c}
.
Let

\displaystyle C_{s,r}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}[t^{(a)}_{i},t^{(c)}_{j},\xi^{a}_{k},\xi^{c}_{\ell}|\,1\leq a\leq s<c\leq s+r;,i\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0},\,j\in\mathbb{Z}_{odd>0},\,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\neq 0},\,\ell\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}],

(3.75)

where
\xi^{a}_{k}
are anticommuting variables,
and
define the vector space isomorphism
\sigma:F_{c}\to C_{s,r}
as follows.
Again we use the standard realization of the Heisenberg algebra (
1\leq a\leq s<c\leq s+r
,
i\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{odd>0}
)

\displaystyle\sigma\alpha^{a}_{-i}\sigma^{-1}

\displaystyle=it^{(a)}_{i},\quad\sigma\alpha^{a}_{i}\sigma^{-1}=-\frac{\partial}{\partial t_{i}^{(a)}},

\displaystyle\sigma\alpha^{c}_{-j}\sigma^{-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}jt^{(c)}_{j},\quad\sigma\alpha^{c}_{j}\sigma^{-1}=-\frac{\partial}{\partial t_{j}^{(c)}}.

We realize the modes of the fields
\Theta^{\pm a}(z)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Theta^{\pm a}_{j}z^{-j-1}=Q_{a}^{\mp a}\theta^{\pm a}(z)
for
1\leq a\leq s
and
the modes of
\Theta^{c}(z)
for
s<c\leq r
, as multiplication and differentiation of Grassmann variables, viz. for
a=1,\ldots,s
,

\sigma\Theta^{\pm a}(z)\sigma^{-1}=\Xi^{\pm a}(z)S_{a},\qquad\sigma\Theta^{c}(z)\sigma^{-1}=\Xi^{c}(z)S_{c},

where (
a\leq s<c
)

\displaystyle\Xi^{+a}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{0}^{a}}z^{-1}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}-k\xi_{k}^{a}z^{k-1}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{-k}^{a}}z^{-k-1},

(3.76)

\displaystyle\Xi^{-a}(z)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{0}^{a}}z^{-1}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k\xi_{-k}^{a}z^{k-1}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{k}^{a}}z^{-k-1},

\displaystyle\Xi^{c}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\left((-1)^{i+\frac{1}{2}}2i\xi^{c}_{i}z^{i-\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi^{c}_{i}}z^{-i-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

Here the operator
S_{a}
acts as

S_{a}(1)=1,\qquad S_{a}\xi_{k}^{b}S_{a}^{-1}=\begin{cases}-\xi_{k}^{b}&\mbox{if }a>b,\\
\xi_{k}^{b}&\mbox{if }a\leq b,\end{cases},\qquad S_{a}\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{k}^{b}}S_{a}^{-1}=\begin{cases}-\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{k}^{b}}&\mbox{if }a>b,\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{k}^{b}}&\mbox{if }a\leq b.\end{cases}

Hence, for the modes we find that

\displaystyle\sigma\Theta^{\pm a}_{0}\sigma^{-1}

\displaystyle=\pm\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{\mp j}^{a}}S_{a},\quad\sigma\Theta^{\pm a}_{-j}\sigma^{-1}=\mp j\xi^{a}_{\pm j}S_{a},\quad\sigma\Theta^{\pm a}_{j}\sigma^{-1}=-\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{\mp j}^{a}}S_{a},\quad j\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0},

\displaystyle\sigma\theta^{c}_{-k}\sigma^{-1}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k+\frac{1}{2}}2k\xi^{c}_{k}S_{c},\quad\sigma\theta^{c}_{k}\sigma^{-1}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{k}^{c}}S_{c},\quad k\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}_{>0}

We still need to define
\sigma\alpha_{0}^{a}\sigma^{-1}
for
1\leq a\leq s
. Formula (
3.49
) suggest the following:

\sigma\alpha_{0}^{a}\sigma^{-1}=T_{a}:=-\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\xi_{k}^{a}\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{k}^{a}}+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\xi_{-k}^{a}\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{-k}^{a}}.

(3.77)

Remark 3.9

Note that here we use the fields
\Theta^{\pm a}(z)
instead of
\theta^{\pm a}(z)
, which means that we do not consider the operators
Q_{a}
. When using
\theta^{\pm a}(z)
instead of
\Theta^{\pm a}(z)
, the operator
Q_{a}
appears in the field

\theta^{\pm a}(z)
and we can assume that
\sigma Q_{a}\sigma^{-1}=q_{a}
, but then
\sigma\alpha_{0}^{a}\sigma^{-1}=q_{a}\frac{\partial}{\partial q_{a}}
.

Using all this and formulas (
3.59
), we obtain for
1\leq a\leq s<c\leq r+s
:

\displaystyle\sigma b^{\pm a}(z)\sigma^{-1}

\displaystyle=e^{\pm z\cdot t^{(a)}}\Xi^{+a}(z)z^{\pm T_{a}}e^{\pm z\cdot\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(a)}}}S_{a},

(3.78)

\displaystyle\sigma b^{c}(z)\sigma^{-1}

\displaystyle=e^{z\circ t^{(c)}}\Xi^{c}(z)e^{2(z\circ\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(c)}})}S_{c}.

Remark 3.10

In this way we always want to present the Grassmann variables in a prefered order, namely the lexicographic ordering, i.e.

\xi_{i}^{k}<_{\rm lex}\xi_{j}^{\ell}
, when
k<\ell
, or when
k=\ell
and
i<j
.
Hence we always write a monomial in the following ordered way.

\xi_{i_{1}}^{k_{1}}\xi_{i_{2}}^{k_{2}}\cdots\xi_{i_{m}}^{k_{m}},\quad\mbox{with }(i_{1},k_{1})<_{\rm lex}(i_{2},k_{2})<_{\rm lex}\cdots<_{\rm lex}(i_{m},k_{m}).

We use the vertex operators (
3.78
) to derive for
1\leq a\leq d\leq s

\displaystyle\sigma C^{\pm a,\pm d}(z,w)\sigma^{-1}=\sigma:b^{\pm a}(z)b^{\pm d}(w):\sigma^{-1}

(3.79)

\displaystyle=i_{z,w}\left(\frac{1}{z-w}\right)^{\delta_{ad}}e^{\pm(z\cdot{t^{(a)}}+w\cdot{t^{(d)}}}\Xi^{\pm a}(z)\Xi^{\pm d}(w)z^{\pm T_{a}}w^{\pm T_{d}}e^{\pm(z^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(a)}}+w^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(d)}})}S_{a}S_{d},

\displaystyle\sigma C^{+a,-d}(z,w)\sigma^{-1}=\sigma:b^{+a}(z)b^{-d}(w):\sigma^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(z-w\right)^{\delta_{ad}}e^{z\cdot{t^{(a)}}-w\cdot{t^{(d)}}}\Xi^{+a}(z)\Xi^{-d}(w)z^{T_{a}}w^{-T_{d}}e^{(z^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(a)}}-w^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(d)}}}S_{a}S_{d}-i_{z,w}\left(\frac{\delta_{ad}}{z-w}\right),

and for
s<a\leq c\leq r+s
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sigma C^{a,c}(z,w):\sigma^{-1}=\sigma:b^{a}(z)b^{c}(w):\sigma^{-1}

(3.80)

\displaystyle=i_{z,w}\left(\frac{z+w}{w-z}\right)^{\delta_{ac}}\Xi^{a}(z)\Xi^{b}(w)e^{z\circ t^{(a)}+w\circ t^{(c)}}e^{2(z^{-1}\circ\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(a)}}+w^{-1}\circ\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(c)}})}S_{a}S_{c}-i_{z,w}\frac{\delta_{ac}}{z+w}.

Finally, the mixed terms, when
1\leq a\leq r<c\leq r+s
, are

\displaystyle\sigma C^{\pm a,c}(z,w):\sigma^{-1}

\displaystyle=\sigma:b^{\pm a}(z)b^{c}(w):\sigma^{-1}

(3.81)

\displaystyle=e^{\pm z\cdot t^{(a)}+w\circ t^{(c)}}\Xi^{+a}(z)\Xi^{c}(w)z^{\pm T_{a}}e^{\pm z^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(a)}}+w^{-1}\circ\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(c)}})}S_{a}S_{c}.

4
Hierarchies of KP type

4.1
The fermionic description of the KP, BKP and DKP hierarchies

The KP hierarchy.
Recall the charge decomposition (
3.3
) of the module
F
.
By definition, a non-zero element
\tau\in F^{(0))}
, satisfies the KP hierarchy if (cf. (
1.5
))

S(\tau\otimes\tau)=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}+\frac{1}{2}}\psi_{i}^{+}\tau\otimes\psi_{-i}^{-}\tau=0.

(4.1)

Note that (
4.1
) can be rewritten in terms of
s
pairs of charged free fermionic fields
\psi^{\pm j}(z)
, defined by (
3.17
), as follows.:

S(\tau\otimes\tau)=\sum_{j=1}^{s}\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}+\frac{1}{2}}\psi_{i}^{+j}\tau\otimes\psi_{-i}^{-j}\tau={\rm Res}_{z=0}\sum_{j=1}^{s}\psi^{+j}(z)\tau\otimes\psi^{-j}(z)\tau dz.

(4.2)

Equation (
4.1
) or (
4.2
) is the KP hierarchy in the fermionic picture. It becomes a hierarchy of differential equations if we apply the isomorphism
\sigma
, constructed in section
3.7.1
. Taking
\tau\in F^{(m)}
produces the same hierarchy.

The BKP hierarchy.

Recall from Section
3.2
that the spin module
F_{b}
is an irreducible
C\ell_{b}
-module
F_{b}
.
The following equation on a non-zero
\tau\in F_{b}
is called the BKP hierarchy in the fermionic picture (cf. (
1.7
)):

S_{B}(\tau\otimes\tau)=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\tilde{\phi}_{i}\tau\otimes\tilde{\phi}_{-i}\tau=\frac{1}{2}\tau\otimes\tau.

(4.3)

This equation describes the
B_{\infty}
-orbit of the vacuum vector
|0\rangle
, see
[
16
]
for more details.
Equation (
4.3
) can be rewritten in terms of
s
pairs of charged free fermionic fields
\psi^{\pm b}(z)
,
b=1,\ldots,s
, and
r
neutral twisted free fermionic fields
\tilde{\phi}^{c}(z)
,
c=s+1,\ldots,s+r
, as follows
If
r=2p+1
is odd, then (
4.3
) becomes

\displaystyle{\rm Res}_{z=0}

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{b=1}^{s}\left(\psi^{+b}(z)\otimes\psi^{-b}(z)+\psi^{-b}(z)\otimes\psi^{+b}(z)\right)+\right.

(4.4)

\displaystyle+\left.\sum_{c=s+1}^{s+2p+1}z^{-1}\tilde{\phi}^{c}(z)\otimes\tilde{\phi}^{c}(-z)\right)(\tau\otimes\tau)dz=\frac{1}{2}\tau\otimes\tau.

However, if
r=2p
is even, we have the extra generator
\sigma_{0}
so that (
4.3
) turns into:

\displaystyle\big\{\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}+{\rm Res}_{z=0}

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{b=1}^{s}\left(\psi^{+b}(z)\otimes\psi^{-b}(z)+\psi^{-b}(z)\otimes\psi^{+b}(z)\right)+\right.

(4.5)

\displaystyle+\left.\sum_{c=s+1}^{s+2p}z^{-1}\tilde{\phi}^{c}(z)\otimes\tilde{\phi}^{c}(-z)\right)dz\big\}(\tau\otimes\tau)=\frac{1}{2}\tau\otimes\tau.

The DKP hierarchy
Recall from Section
3.2
that the spin module
F_{d}
splits into two irreducible
d_{\infty}
-modules
F_{d}^{\overline{0}}\oplus F_{d}^{\overline{1}}
, . The highest weight vector of
F_{d}^{\overline{0}}
is
|\overline{0}\rangle=|0\rangle
and of
F_{d}^{\overline{1}}
is
|\overline{1}\rangle=\sqrt{2}\phi_{-\frac{1}{2}}|0\rangle
.
The following equation on the non-zero
\tau_{a}\in F_{d}^{a}
is called DKP hierarchy in the fermionic picture:

S_{D}(\tau_{a}\otimes\tau_{a})=\sum_{i\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}}\phi_{i}\tau_{a}\otimes\phi_{-i}\tau_{a}=0,\quad a=\overline{0}\ \mbox{or }\overline{1}.

(4.6)

This equation describes the
D_{\infty}
-orbit of the highest weight vector
|\overline{a}\rangle
, see
[
16
]
for more details. This group consists of (even) invertible elements
g
such that
g\phi_{j}g^{-1}=\sum_{i}a_{ij}\phi_{i}
where
\sum_{k}a_{kj}a_{-k,j}=\delta_{i,-j}
, i.e. that
g
leaves the bilinear form
(\cdot,\cdot)_{d}
of (
2.19
) invariant.

Equation (
4.6
) can be expressed in terms of
s
pairs of charged free fermionic fields
\psi^{\pm b}(z)
and
r
neutral twisted fields
\tilde{\phi}^{c}(z)
, introduced in Section
3.5
. If
r=2p
is even, then (
4.6
) becomes

\displaystyle{\rm Res}_{z=0}

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{b=1}^{s}\left(\psi^{+b}(z)\otimes\psi^{-b}(z)+\psi^{-b}(z)\otimes\psi^{+b}(z)\right)+\right.

(4.7)

\displaystyle+\left.\sum_{c=s+1}^{s+2p}z^{-1}\tilde{\phi}^{c}(z)\otimes\tilde{\phi}^{c}(-z)\right)(\tau_{a}\otimes\tau_{a})dz=0.

However, if
r=2p-1
is odd, there is an extra generator
\sigma_{0}
.
Then (
4.6
) turns into:

\displaystyle\big\{\sigma_{0}\otimes\sigma_{0}+{\rm Res}_{z=0}

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{b=1}^{s}\left(\psi^{+b}(z)\otimes\psi^{-b}(z)+\psi^{-b}(z)\otimes\psi^{+b}(z)\right)+\right.

(4.8)

\displaystyle+\left.\sum_{c=s+1}^{s+2p-1}z^{-1}\tilde{\phi}^{c}(z)\otimes\tilde{\phi}^{c}(-z)\right)dz\big\}(\tau_{a}\otimes\tau_{a})=0.

4.2
The symplectic bosons description of the CKP hierarchy

Recall from Section
3.3
that the Weyl module
F_{c}
splits into two irreducible
c_{\infty}
-modules
F_{c}^{\overline{0}}
and
F_{c}^{\overline{1}}
with highest weight vectors
|\overline{0}\rangle=|0\rangle
, respectively
|\overline{1}\rangle=b_{-\frac{1}{2}}|0\rangle
.
The CKP hierarchy (see
[
26
]
) in the fermionic picture is the following equation on
\tau\in F_{c}^{\overline{0}}
:

S_{C}(\tau\otimes\tau)=\sum_{i\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{i+\frac{1}{2}}b_{i}\tau\otimes b_{-i}\tau=0.

(4.9)

Equation (
4.9
) can be rewritten in terms of
s
charged fields
b^{\pm a}(z)
and
r
neutral fields
b^{c}(z)
.:

\displaystyle\sum_{a=1}^{s}\sum_{i\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle\left(b^{-a}_{i}\tau\otimes b_{-i}^{+a}\tau-b^{+a}_{i}\tau\otimes b_{-i}^{-a}\tau\right)+\sum_{c=s+1}^{r+s}\sum_{i\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{i+\frac{1}{2}}b^{c}_{i}\tau\otimes b^{c}_{-i}\tau=0.

(4.10)

In terms of the symplectic bosonic fields, it becomes

\displaystyle{\rm Res}_{z=0}\big\{\sum_{a=1}^{s}

\displaystyle\left(b^{-a}(z)\tau\otimes b^{+a}(z)\tau-b^{+a}(z)\tau\otimes b^{-a}(z)\tau\right)+\sum_{c=s+1}^{r+s}b^{c}(z)\tau\otimes b^{c}(-z)\tau\big\}dz=0.

(4.11)

Unfortunately, taking
\tau\in F_{c}^{\overline{1}}
, does not give the same hierarchy.

4.3
Bosonic description of the multicomponent KP, BKP, CKP and DKP hierarchies

The KP hierarchy

Equation (
4.1
) or (
4.2
) is the KP hierarchy in the fermionic picture. It becomes a hierarchy of differential equations if we apply the isomorphism
\sigma:F\xrightarrow{\sim}B_{s}
, constructed in (
3.7.1
) so that for
\tau\in F^{(0)}

\sigma(\tau)=\tau(q,t)=\sum_{|\underline{k}|=0}\tau_{\underline{k}}(t)q^{\underline{k}}

becomes a function in
t=(t_{1}^{(i)},t_{2}^{(i)},\ldots)
,
i=1,\dots,s
, and in the
sl_{s}
-root lattice,
with lattice points
\underline{k}=(k_{1},k_{2},\ldots k_{s})
with
|\underline{k}|=k_{1}+k_{2}+\cdots+k_{s}=0
, where
q^{\underline{k}}=q_{1}^{k_{1}}q_{2}^{k_{2}}\cdots q_{s}^{k_{s}}
. If
s=1
one obtains the classical KP-hierarchy. For
s>1
we obtain the
s
-component KP hierarchy.
Equation (
4.1
) describes the
GL_{\infty}
-group orbit of
|0\rangle

[
18
]
. Note that in the fermionic picture this equation is the same. It is only after we apply the isomorphism
\sigma
and
\tau\in B_{s}^{(0)}
that we obtain different descriptions for different choices of
s
.
Using the fermionic vertex operators (
3.33
) and (
4.2
) we rewrite (
4.1
) and obtain the
s
-component KP hierarchy. Let
\underline{e}_{i}
be the basis vector in
\mathbb{Z}^{s}
, wich has a 1 on the
i
-th place and zeros elsewhere and let
|\underline{k}|_{j}=k_{1}+k_{2}+\cdots+k_{j}
. Then, for each pair
\underline{k},\underline{\ell}
with
|\underline{k}|=|\underline{\ell}|=0
we have the
s
-component KP hierarchy of Hirota bilinear equations (
1.34
) on
\tau_{\underline{k}}(t)
,
|\underline{k}|=0
(cf. (
4.2
)),
describing the orbit
GL_{\infty}|0\rangle
in the bosonic picture. Taking
\tau\in F^{(m)}
, we obtain the same equations on
\tau_{\underline{k}}(t)
,
|\underline{k}|=m
for the
GL_{\infty}
orbit of
|m\rangle
.

The BKP hierarchy

In this case
F_{b}
is irreducible and
\sigma:F_{b}\xrightarrow{\sim}B_{s,r}
, where
B_{s,r}
,
r=2p
or
r=2p+1
, is given by (
3.67
) and
\sigma
constructed in (
3.7.1
).
We write

\sigma(\tau)=\tau(q,t,\theta)=\sum_{\underline{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{s},\underline{\ell}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{p}}\tau_{\underline{k},\underline{\ell}}(t)q^{\underline{k}}\theta^{\underline{\ell}},

where
q^{\underline{k}}=q_{1}^{k_{1}}q_{2}^{k_{2}}\cdots q_{s}^{k_{s}}
and
\theta^{\underline{\ell}}=\theta_{1}^{\ell_{1}}\theta_{2}^{\ell_{2}}\cdots\theta_{p}^{\ell_{p}}
.
Now using
\sigma
and substituting the vertex operators (
1.32
), (
3.71
)
and if
r=2p
, then use formula (
3.72
) for

\sigma\sigma_{0}\sigma^{-1}
,
we obtain:

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Res}_{z=0}\big(\sum_{b=1}^{s}\big((-1)^{|\underline{k}+\overline{\underline{k}}|_{b-1}}z^{k_{b}-\overline{k}_{b}-2+2\delta_{bs}}e^{z\cdot(t^{(b)}-\overline{t}^{(b)})}e^{z^{-1}\cdot(\tilde{\partial}_{\overline{t}^{(b)}}-\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(b)}})}\tau_{\underline{k}+\underline{e}_{s}-\underline{e}_{b},\underline{\ell}}(t)\tau_{\overline{\underline{k}}+\underline{e}_{b}-\underline{e}_{s},\overline{\underline{\ell}}}(\overline{t})+

(4.12)

\displaystyle+(-1)^{|\underline{k}+\overline{\underline{k}}|_{b-1}}z^{-k_{b}+\overline{k}_{b}-2-2\delta_{bs}}e^{z\cdot(\overline{t}^{(b)}-t^{(b)})}e^{z^{-1}\cdot(\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(b)}}-\tilde{\partial}_{\overline{t}^{(b)}})}\tau_{\underline{k}+\underline{e}_{s}+\underline{e}_{b},\underline{\ell}}(t)\tau_{\overline{\underline{k}}-\underline{e}_{b}-\underline{e}_{s},\overline{\underline{\ell}}}(\overline{t})\big)+

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{c=1}^{2p}(-1)^{{|\underline{k}+\overline{\underline{k}}|}+|\underline{\ell}+\overline{\underline{\ell}}|_{\left[\frac{c-1}{2}\right]}+(c-1)(\ell_{\left[\frac{c+1}{2}\right]}+\overline{\ell}_{\left[\frac{c+1}{2}\right]}+1)}z^{-1}e^{z\circ(t^{(s+c)}-\overline{t}^{(s+c)})}\times

\displaystyle e^{2(z^{-1}\circ(\tilde{\partial}_{\overline{t}^{(s+c)}}-\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(s+c)}}))}\tau_{\underline{k}+\underline{e}_{s},\underline{\ell}+\underline{e}_{\left[\frac{c+1}{2}\right]}}(t)\tau_{\overline{\underline{k}}-\underline{e}_{s},\overline{\underline{\ell}}+\underline{e}_{\left[\frac{c+1}{2}\right]}}(\overline{t})

\displaystyle+\frac{\delta_{r,2p+1}}{2}(-1)^{{|\underline{k}+\overline{\underline{k}}|}+|\underline{\ell}+\overline{\underline{\ell}}|}z^{-1}e^{z\circ(t^{(s+r)}-\overline{t}^{(s+r)})}e^{2(z^{-1}\circ(\tilde{\partial}_{\overline{t}^{(s+r)}}-\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(s+r)}}))}\tau_{\underline{k}+\underline{e}_{s},\underline{\ell}}(t)\tau_{\overline{\underline{k}}-\underline{e}_{s},\overline{\underline{\ell}}}(\overline{t})\big)dz

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\delta_{r,2p}}{2}(-1)^{{{|\underline{k}+\overline{\underline{k}}|}+|\underline{\ell}+\overline{\underline{\ell}}|}}\right)\tau_{\underline{k}+\underline{e}_{s},\underline{\ell}}(t)\tau_{\overline{\underline{k}}-\underline{e}_{s},\overline{\underline{\ell}}}(\overline{t}).

Here
[x]
stands for the largest integer smaller or equal to
x
.

The DKP hierachy

This case is similar to the BKP case, however in this case
F_{d}
splits into two irreducible
d_{\infty}
modules. Again we have two cases,
viz
r=2p
and
r=2p-1
, and we
write

\sigma(\tau_{a})=\tau^{a}(q,t,\theta)=\sum_{\underline{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{s},\underline{\ell}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{p},\,|\underline{k}|+|\underline{\ell}|=a\,{\rm mod}\,2}\tau_{\underline{k},\underline{\ell}}(t)q^{\underline{k}}\theta^{\underline{\ell}},

Then the fermionic DKP hierarchy turns into the following set of equations for all
\underline{k},\overline{\underline{k}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{s}
,
\underline{\ell},\overline{\underline{\ell}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{p}
, such that
|\underline{k}|+|\underline{\ell}|+1=a\mod 2
:

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Res}_{z=0}\big(\sum_{b=1}^{s}\big((-1)^{|\underline{k}+\overline{\underline{k}}|_{b-1}}z^{k_{b}-\overline{k}_{b}-2+2\delta_{bs}}e^{z\cdot(t^{(b)}-\overline{t}^{(b)})}e^{z^{-1}\cdot(\tilde{\partial}_{\overline{t}^{(b)}}-\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(b)}})}\tau_{\underline{k}+\underline{e}_{s}-\underline{e}_{b},\underline{\ell}}(t)\tau_{\overline{\underline{k}}+\underline{e}_{b}-\underline{e}_{s},\overline{\underline{\ell}}}(\overline{t})+

(4.13)

\displaystyle+(-1)^{|\underline{k}+\overline{\underline{k}}|_{b-1}}z^{-k_{b}+\overline{k}_{b}-2-2\delta_{bs}}e^{z\cdot(\overline{t}^{(b)}-t^{(b)})}e^{z^{-1}\cdot(\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(b)}}-\tilde{\partial}_{\overline{t}^{(b)}})}\tau_{\underline{k}+\underline{e}_{s}+\underline{e}_{b},\underline{\ell}}(t)\tau_{\overline{\underline{k}}-\underline{e}_{b}-\underline{e}_{s},\overline{\underline{\ell}}}(\overline{t})\big)+

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{c=1}^{r}(-1)^{{|\underline{k}+\overline{\underline{k}}|}+|\underline{\ell}+\overline{\underline{\ell}}|_{\left[\frac{c-1}{2}\right]}+(c-1)(\ell_{\left[\frac{c+1}{2}\right]}+\overline{\ell}_{\left[\frac{c+1}{2}\right]}+1)}z^{-1}e^{z\circ(t^{(s+c)}-\overline{t}^{(s+c)})}\times

\displaystyle e^{2(z^{-1}\circ(\tilde{\partial}_{\overline{t}^{(s+c)}}-\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(s+c)}}))}\tau_{\underline{k}+\underline{e}_{s},\underline{\ell}+\underline{e}_{\left[\frac{c+1}{2}\right]}}(t)\tau_{\overline{\underline{k}}-\underline{e}_{s},\overline{\underline{\ell}}+\underline{e}_{\left[\frac{c+1}{2}\right]}}(\overline{t})\big)dz

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta_{r,2p-1}}{2}\tau_{\underline{k}+\underline{e}_{s},\underline{\ell}+\underline{e}_{p}}(t)\tau_{\overline{\underline{k}}-\underline{e}_{s},\overline{\underline{\ell}}+\underline{e}_{p}}(\overline{t}).

In the above, we have used that
(-1)^{{{|\underline{k}+\overline{\underline{k}}|}+|\underline{\ell}+\overline{\underline{\ell}}|}}=1
.

Remark 4.1

Note that the equations of DKP hierarchy for
r=2p
appear as a subset of the equations of the BKP hierarchy for the same
r=2p
.
Namely, one only takes those
\underline{k}
,
\underline{\ell}
, and
\overline{\underline{k}}
,
\overline{\underline{\ell}}
, for which

|\underline{k}|+|\underline{\ell}|+1=|\underline{\overline{k}}|+|\underline{\overline{\ell}|}+1=a\mod 2
.
Since both
a=\overline{0}
and
a=\overline{1}
, appear in the BKP hierarchy we also find, besides these two DKP hierarchies, the so-called modified DKP equations. See (
[
16
]
, Section 2).

The CKP hierarchy

Now using the isomorphism
\sigma
, see
Subsection
3.7.2
,
we assume that
\tau(t,y,\xi)\in\sigma(F_{0})
, thus

\displaystyle\tau(t,\xi)\in

(4.14)

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}[t^{(a)}_{i},t^{(c)}_{j},\xi^{a}_{k},\xi^{c}_{\ell}|\,1\leq a\leq s<c\leq s+r,\,i\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0},\,j\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0,odd},\,0\neq k\in\mathbb{Z},\,\ell\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}].

with

\tau(t,\xi)=\sum_{w}\tau_{w}(t)w,

where

w=\xi^{1}_{k_{1}^{1}}\cdot\cdot\xi^{1}_{k_{i_{1}}^{1}}\xi^{1}_{\ell_{1}^{1}}\cdot\cdot\xi^{1}_{\ell_{j_{1}}^{1}}\xi^{2}_{k_{2}^{1}}\cdot\cdot\xi^{2}_{k_{i_{2}}^{2}}\xi^{2}_{\ell_{2}^{1}}\cdot\cdot\xi^{2}_{k_{j_{2}}^{2}}\cdot\cdot\xi^{s}_{k_{1}^{s}}\cdot\cdot\xi^{s}_{k_{i_{s}}^{s}}\xi^{s}_{\ell_{1}^{s}}\cdot\cdot\xi^{s}_{\ell_{j_{s}}^{s}}\xi^{s+1}_{m_{1}^{s+1}}\cdots\xi^{s+1}_{m_{j_{s+1}}}\cdot\cdot\xi^{s+r}_{m_{1}^{s+r}}\cdot\cdot\xi^{s+r}_{m_{j_{s+r}}},

(4.15)

such that
i_{a},j_{b}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,

k_{p}^{a}\in\mathbb{Z}_{<0}
,
\ell_{p}^{a}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, and
m^{c}_{q}\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
with
k_{p}^{a}<k_{p+1}^{a}
,
\ell_{p}^{a}<\ell_{p+1}^{a}
and
m^{c}_{q}<m^{c}_{q+1}
and

i_{1}+\cdots i_{s}+j_{1}+\cdots+j_{s+r}\in 2\mathbb{Z}
.

To describe the CKP hierarchy of differential equations, we will use the following notations.
Let
\xi^{b}_{j}
(resp.
\xi_{k}^{c}
) be one of the Grassmann variables appearing (resp. not appearing) in the expression (
4.15
). We write
w\backslash\xi^{b}_{j}
(resp
w\cup\xi^{c}_{k}
for the monomial that we get by removing
\xi^{b}_{j}
from
w
(resp. by adding
\xi^{c}_{k}
to
w
and writing it in the preferred lexicographical ordering). We substitute (
3.78
) in (
4.11
) and and thus obtain the
multicomponent CKP hierarchy in the bosonic picture.
Let
w
be given by (
4.15
) and
\overline{w}
by the same expression but with all
\xi^{b}_{j}
replaced by
{\overline{\xi}}^{b}_{\overline{j}}
, such that the number of Grassmann variables appearing in
w
and
\overline{w}
is odd, then we obtain

\displaystyle{\rm Res}_{z=0}

\displaystyle\bigg\{\sum_{a=1}^{s}\bigg[-z^{i_{a}-\overline{i}_{a}-j_{a}+\overline{j}_{a}}e^{z\cdot(t^{(a)}-\overline{t}^{(a)})}e^{z^{-1}\cdot(\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(a)}}-\tilde{\partial}_{\overline{t}^{(a)}})}\times

(4.16)

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{<0},\,k\neq k_{1}^{a},\ldots,k^{a}_{i_{a}}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!N_{a}(w,\xi_{k}^{a})z^{k}\tau_{w\cup\xi_{k}^{a}}(t)-\sum_{\ell=\ell_{1}^{a},\ldots,\ell^{a}_{j_{a}}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\ell N_{a}(w,\xi^{a}_{\ell})z^{\ell}\tau_{w\backslash\xi^{a}_{\ell}}(t)\right)\times

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0},\,\ell\neq\overline{\ell}_{1}^{a},\ldots,\overline{\ell}^{a}_{\overline{j}_{a}}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!N_{a}(\overline{w},\overline{\xi}_{\ell}^{a})z^{-\ell}\tau_{\overline{w}\cup\overline{\xi}_{\ell}^{a}}(\overline{t})-\sum_{k=\overline{k}_{1}^{a},\ldots,\overline{k}^{a}_{\overline{i}_{a}}}\!\!\!\!\!\!kN_{a}(\overline{w},\overline{\xi}^{a}_{k})z^{-k}\tau_{\overline{w}\backslash\overline{\xi}^{a}_{k}}(\overline{t})\right)

\displaystyle-(-z)^{j_{a}-\overline{j}_{a}-i_{a}+\overline{i}_{a}}e^{-z\cdot(t^{(a)}-\overline{t}^{(a)})}e^{-z^{-1}\cdot(\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(a)}}-\tilde{\partial}_{\overline{t}^{(a)}})}\times

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0},\,\ell\neq\ell_{1}^{a},\ldots,\ell^{a}_{j_{a}}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!N_{a}(w,\xi_{\ell}^{a})(-z)^{-\ell}\tau_{w\cup\xi_{\ell}^{a}}(t)-\sum_{k=k_{1}^{a},\ldots,k^{a}_{i_{a}}}\!\!\!\!\!\!kN_{a}(w,\xi^{a}_{k})(-z)^{-k}\tau_{w\backslash\xi^{a}_{k}}(t)\right)\times

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{k<0},\,k\neq\overline{k}_{1}^{a},\ldots,\overline{k}^{a}_{\overline{i}_{a}}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!N_{a}(\overline{w},\overline{\xi}_{k}^{a})(-z)^{k}\tau_{\overline{w}\cup\overline{\xi}_{k}^{a}}(\overline{t})-\sum_{\ell=\overline{\ell}_{1}^{a},\ldots,\overline{\ell}^{a}_{\overline{j}_{a}}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\ell N_{a}(\overline{w},\overline{\xi}^{a}_{\ell})(-z)^{\ell}\tau_{\overline{w}\backslash\overline{\xi}^{a}_{\ell}}(\overline{t})\right)\bigg]+

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{c=s+1}^{r+s}\bigg[e^{z\circ(t^{(c)}-\overline{t}^{(c)})}e^{2(z^{-1}\circ(\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(c)}}-\tilde{\partial}_{\overline{t}^{(c)}}))}\times

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{2}{m\in\frac 12+\mathbb Z_{\ge 0},}{m\ne m_1^c,\ldots, m^c_{ j_c}}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!N_{c}(w,\xi_{m}^{c})z^{-m-\frac{1}{2}}\tau_{w\cup\xi_{m}^{c}}(t)-\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{m=m_{1}^{c},\ldots,m^{c}_{j_{c}}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!(-1)^{m+\frac{1}{2}}2mN_{c}(w,\xi^{c}_{m})z^{m-\frac{1}{2}}\tau_{w\backslash\xi^{c}_{m}}(t)\right)\times

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{2}{m\in\frac 12+\mathbb Z_{\ge 0},}{ m\ne\overline m_1^c,\ldots, \overline m^c_{ \overline j_c}}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!N_{c}(\overline{w},\overline{\xi}_{m}^{c})(-z)^{-m-\frac{1}{2}}\tau_{\overline{w}\cup\overline{\xi}_{m}^{c}}(\overline{t})-\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{m=\overline{m}_{1}^{c},\ldots,\overline{m}^{c}_{\overline{j}_{c}}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!(-1)^{m+\frac{1}{2}}2mN_{c}(\overline{w},\overline{\xi}^{c}_{m})(-z)^{m-\frac{1}{2}}\tau_{\overline{w}\backslash\overline{\xi}^{c}_{m}}(t)\right)\bigg]\bigg\}dz

\displaystyle=0,

where

N_{b}(w,\xi_{n}^{b})=(-1)^{\{\mbox{the number of $\xi^{b}_{p}$ appearing in $w$ with index $p<n$}\}}.

4.4
The wave function, Sato-Wilson and Lax equations for the KP, BKP, CKP and DKP hierarchies

The KP hierarchy

We follow the exposition
[
15
]
, Section IV.
The first step is that we replace in (
1.34
