) all
t_{1}^{(j)}
and
\overline{t}_{1}^{(j)}
by

t_{1}^{(j)}+x
, respectively
\overline{t}_{1}^{(j)}+\overline{x}
, so that the tau-function also depends on the variable
x
. Introduce the
s\times s
-matrices

V^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)=P^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\pm z)R^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};\pm z)S^{\pm}(t,\pm z)e^{\pm xz},

where

\displaystyle P^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\pm z)_{ij}

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{|\underline{e}_{i}|_{j-1}}z^{\delta_{ij}-1}}{\tau_{\underline{\alpha}}(x,t)}e^{\mp z^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(j)}}}\left(\tau_{\underline{\alpha}\pm(\underline{e}_{i}-\underline{e}_{j})}(x,t)\right),

(4.17)

\displaystyle R^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};\pm z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{s}(-1)^{|\underline{\alpha}|_{j-1}}z^{\pm\alpha_{j}}E_{jj},

\displaystyle S^{\pm}(t,\pm z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{s}e^{\pm z\cdot t^{(j)}}E_{jj}.

Then (
1.34
) turns into

{\rm Res}_{z=0}\sum_{j=1}^{s}V^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)E_{jj}V^{-}(\underline{\beta};\overline{x},\overline{t},z)^{t}dz=0.

(4.18)

Let
\partial=\partial_{x}
and using the fundamental lemma (see
[
15
]
, Lemma 4.1), we deduce the following expression for the matrix pseudo-differential operators

\sum_{j=1}^{s}(P^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\partial)R^{+}(\underline{\alpha};\partial)E_{jj}R^{-}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)^{*}P^{-}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)^{*})_{-}=0.

(4.19)

(Note that one cannot simply substitute
\partial
for
\pm z
in the formulas (
4.17
). One first has to write
P^{\pm}(\pm z)
as
P^{\pm}(\pm z)=I+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}P^{\pm}_{k}(\pm z)^{-k}
, then
P^{\pm}(\partial)=1+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}P^{\pm}_{k}\partial^{-k}
. This means that
P^{\pm}(\pm z)
is the symbol of pseudo-differential operator
P^{\pm}(\partial)
.)

Taking
\underline{\alpha}=\underline{\beta}
, we deduce the Sato-Wilson equations

\displaystyle P^{-}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)^{*}

\displaystyle=P^{+}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)^{-1},

(4.20)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial P^{+}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)}{\partial t_{i}^{j}}

\displaystyle=-(P^{+}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)\partial^{i}E_{jj}P^{+}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)^{-1})_{-}P^{+}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial).

Now introducing the Lax operators

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle=L(\underline{\beta};t,x,\partial)=P^{+}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)\partial P^{+}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)^{-1},

(4.21)

\displaystyle C^{j}

\displaystyle=C^{j}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)=P^{+}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)E_{jj}P^{+}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)^{-1},

we deduce from (
4.20
) that

\frac{\partial L}{\partial t^{(j)}_{i}}=[(L^{i}C^{j})_{+},L],\quad\frac{\partial C^{k}}{\partial t_{i}^{(j)}}=[(L^{i}C^{j})_{+},C^{k}].

(4.22)

The BKP hierarchy (first approach)

In general one can define an
(2s+r)\times(2s+r)
-matrix valued wave function as in the previous case. However, in general the corresponding wave operators
P^{\pm}(\partial)
are not invertible. This is a problem. Therefore we take a different approach. If we assume that
s\neq 0
, and
r=0
or
s
arbitrary and
r=1
, there is no problem. In those cases the wave operators
P^{\pm}(\partial)
are invertible. The case that
r=0
suggest to take the following approach (the case
r=1
, suggests the second approach, which we will present later in this section). So assume from now on that
s\neq 0
.
If
r\geq 1
, we set in equation (
4.12
) all the variables
t_{j}^{(c)}=\overline{t}_{j}^{(c)}=0
for
c>s
and
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
. Next replace all
t_{1}^{(a)}
for
1\leq a\leq s
by
t_{1}^{(a)}+x
and similarly
\overline{t}_{1}^{(a)}
by by
\overline{t}_{1}^{(a)}+\overline{x}
.
Define the
2s\times 2s
-matrix valued wave function
V^{\pm}(\alpha;x,t,z)

by

\tilde{V}^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)=P^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\pm z)R^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};\pm z)S^{\pm}(t,\pm z)e^{\pm xz},

(4.23)

where for
1\leq i,j\leq s
and all
a=1,\ldots,r

\displaystyle P^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\pm z)_{ij}

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{|\underline{e}_{i}|_{j-1}}z^{\delta_{ij}-1}}{\tau_{\underline{\alpha}}(x,t)}e^{\mp z^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(j)}}}\left(\tau_{\underline{\alpha}\pm(\underline{e}_{i}-\underline{e}_{j})}(x,t)\right)\big|_{t^{(s+a)}=0},

(4.24)

\displaystyle P^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\pm z)_{s+i,j}

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{|\underline{e}_{i}|_{j-1}}z^{-\delta_{ij}-1}}{\tau_{\underline{\alpha}}(x,t)}e^{\mp z^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(j)}}}\left(\tau_{\underline{\alpha}\mp(\underline{e}_{i}+\underline{e}_{j})}(x,t)\right)\big|_{t^{(s+a)}=0},

\displaystyle P^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\pm z)_{i,s+j}

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{|\underline{e}_{i}|_{j-1}}(-z)^{-\delta_{ij}-1}}{\tau_{\underline{\alpha}}(x,t)}e^{\pm((-z^{-1})\cdot\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(j)}})}\left(\tau_{\underline{\alpha}\pm(\underline{e}_{i}+\underline{e}_{j})}(x,t)\right)\big|_{t^{(s+a)}=0},

\displaystyle P^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\pm z)_{s+i,s+j}

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{|\underline{e}_{i}|_{j-1}}(-z)^{\delta_{ij}-1}}{\tau_{\underline{\alpha}}(x,t)}e^{\pm((-z^{-1})\cdot\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(j)}})}\left(\tau_{\underline{\alpha}\mp(\underline{e}_{i}-\underline{e}_{j})}(x,t)\right)\big|_{t^{(s+a)}=0},

and

\displaystyle R^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};\pm z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{s}(-1)^{|\underline{\alpha}|_{j-1}}\left(z^{\pm\alpha_{j}}E_{jj}+(-z)^{\mp\alpha_{j}}E_{s+j,s+j}\right),

(4.25)

\displaystyle S^{\pm}(t,\pm z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{s}\big(e^{\pm z\cdot t^{(j)}}E_{jj}+e^{\mp(-z)\cdot t^{(j)}}E_{s+j,s+j}\big).

For
r\geq 1
define the
2s\times r
-matrix valued functions

W^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)=(W^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t)_{ij},z)_{1\leq i\leq 2s,1\leq j\leq r},=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}W^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t)^{n}z^{-n},

with

\displaystyle W^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)_{i,2c-1}=\sqrt{-1}(-1)^{|\underline{k}|+|\underline{\ell}|_{c-1}}\frac{e^{\pm 2((z^{-1})\circ\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(s+2c-1)}})}\tau_{\underline{k}\pm\underline{e}_{i},\underline{\ell}+\underline{e}_{c}}(x,t)}{\tau_{\underline{k},\underline{\ell}}(x,t)}\big|_{t^{(s+a)}=0},

(4.26)

\displaystyle W^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)_{s+i,2c-1}=\sqrt{-1}(-1)^{|\underline{k}|+|\underline{\ell}|_{c-1}}\frac{e^{\pm 2((z^{-1})\circ\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(s+2c-1)}})}\tau_{\underline{k}\mp\underline{e}_{i},\underline{\ell}+\underline{e}_{c}}(x,t)}{\tau_{\underline{k},\underline{\ell}}(x,t)}\big|_{t^{(s+a)}=0},

\displaystyle W^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)_{i,2c}=(-1)^{|\underline{k}|+|\underline{\ell}|_{c}}\frac{e^{\pm 2((z^{-1})\circ\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(s+2c)}})}\tau_{\underline{k}\pm\underline{e}_{i},\underline{\ell}+\underline{e}_{c}}(x,t)}{\tau_{\underline{k},\underline{\ell}}(x,t)}\big|_{t^{(s+a)}=0}\big|_{t^{s+a}=0},

\displaystyle W^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)_{s+i,2c}=(-1)^{|\underline{k}|+|\underline{\ell}|_{c}}\frac{e^{\pm 2((z^{-1})\circ\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(s+2c)}})}\tau_{\underline{k}\mp\underline{e}_{i},\underline{\ell}+\underline{e}_{c}}(x,t)}{\tau_{\underline{k},\underline{\ell}}(x,t)}\big|_{t^{(s+a)}=0},

for
1\leq c\leq p
. If
r=2p
that is all, but if
r=2p+1
we define

\displaystyle W^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)_{i,r}=\sqrt{-1}(-1)^{|\underline{k}|+|\underline{\ell}|}\frac{e^{\pm 2((z^{-1})\circ\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(s+r)}})}\tau_{\underline{k}\pm\underline{e}_{i},\underline{\ell}+\underline{e}_{p+1}}(x,t)}{\tau_{\underline{k},\underline{\ell}}(x,t)}\big|_{t^{(s+a)}=0},

(4.27)

\displaystyle W^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)_{s+i,r}=\sqrt{-1}(-1)^{|\underline{k}|+|\underline{\ell}|}\frac{e^{\pm 2(z^{-1}\circ\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(s+r)}})}\tau_{\underline{k}\mp\underline{e}_{i},\underline{\ell}+\underline{e}_{p+1}}(x,t)}{\tau_{\underline{k},\underline{\ell}}(x,t)}\big|_{t^{(s+a)}=0},

and the
2s\times 1
matrix
T^{\pm}(\alpha;x,t)

\displaystyle T^{\pm}(\alpha;x,t)_{i1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau_{\underline{k}\pm\underline{e}_{i},\underline{\ell}}(x,t)}{\tau_{\underline{k},\underline{\ell}}(x,t)}\big|_{t^{s+a}=0},

(4.28)

\displaystyle T^{\pm}(\alpha;x,t)_{s+i,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau_{\underline{k}\mp\underline{e}_{i},\underline{\ell}}(x,t)}{\tau_{\underline{k},\underline{\ell}}(x,t)}\big|_{t^{s+a}=0}.

We thus obtain the following bilinear identity for the wave function:

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Res}_{z=0}\sum_{j=1}^{s}V^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)\left(E_{jj}-E_{s+j,s+j}\right)V^{-}(\underline{\beta};\overline{x},\overline{t},z)^{t}dz=

(4.29)

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\delta_{r0}}{2})

\displaystyle W^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t)^{0}W^{-}(\underline{\beta};\overline{x},\overline{t})^{0t}+\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\delta_{r,2p}}{2}(-1)^{{{|\underline{\alpha}-\underline{\beta}}|}}\right)T^{+}(\alpha;x,t)T^{-}(\beta;\overline{x},\overline{t})^{t}.

Using the fundamental lemma, we obtain that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{s}

\displaystyle\left(P^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\partial)R^{+}(\underline{\alpha}-\underline{\beta},\partial)\left(E_{jj}-E_{s+j,s+j}\right)P^{-}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)^{*}\right)_{-}=

(4.30)

\displaystyle(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\delta_{r0}}{2})W^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t)^{0}\partial^{-1}W^{-}(\underline{\beta};x,t)^{0t}+

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\delta_{r,2p}}{2}(-1)^{{{|\underline{\alpha}-\underline{\beta}}|}}\right)T^{+}(\alpha;x,t)\partial^{-1}T^{-}(\beta;x,t)^{t}.

Taking
\underline{\alpha}=\underline{\beta}
, it is straightforward to check that the right-hand side of (
4.30
) is equal to
0
.

Let
\partial=\partial_{x}
and using the fundamental lemma (see
[
15
]
, Lemma 4.1), we deduce the following expression for the matrix pseudo-differential operators

\sum_{j=1}^{s}(P^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\partial)R^{+}(\underline{\alpha};\partial)\left(E_{jj}-E_{s+j,s+j}\right)R^{-}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)^{*}P^{-}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)^{*})_{-}=0.

(4.31)

Letting

J=\sum_{j=1}^{s}E_{jj}-E_{s+j,s+j}=J^{-1},

(4.32)

we thus get

\displaystyle JP^{-}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)^{*}

\displaystyle=P^{+}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)^{-1}J,

(4.33)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial P^{+}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)}{\partial t_{i}^{j}}

\displaystyle=-(P^{+}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)\left(\partial^{i}E_{jj}-(-\partial)^{i}E_{s+j,s+j}\right)P^{+}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)^{-1})_{-}P^{+}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial).

Now introducing the Lax operators

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle=L(\underline{\beta};t,x,\partial)=P^{+}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)J\partial P^{+}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)^{-1},

(4.34)

\displaystyle D^{j}

\displaystyle=D^{j}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)=P^{+}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)\left(E_{jj}-E_{s+j,s+j}\right)P^{+}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)^{-1},

\displaystyle E^{j}

\displaystyle=(D^{j})^{2}=E^{j}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)=P^{+}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)\left(E_{jj}+E_{s+j,s+j}\right)P^{+}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)^{-1},

we deduce from (
4.33
) that

\frac{\partial L}{\partial t_{i}^{(j)}}=[(L^{i}D^{j})_{+},L],\quad\frac{\partial D^{k}}{\partial t_{i}^{(j)}}=[(L^{i}D^{j})_{+},D^{k}],\quad\frac{\partial E^{k}}{\partial t_{i}^{(j)}}=[(L^{i}D^{j})_{+},E^{k}].

(4.35)

Let

N=\sum_{j=1}^{s}\left(E_{j,s+j}+E_{s+,j}\right),

(4.36)

then it is straightforward to check, using (
4.24
), that

P^{-}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)=NP^{+}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)N

Now let

K=JN=\sum_{j=1}^{s}\left(E_{j,s+j}-E_{s+j,j}\right),

(4.37)

then

L(\partial)^{*}=KL(\partial)K^{-1},\quad D^{j}(\partial)^{*}=-KD^{j}(\partial)K^{-1},\quad E^{j}(\partial)^{*}=KE^{j}(\partial)K^{-1}.

Example 4.2

Let’s consider the special case
s=1
.
In this case

\displaystyle L=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix}\partial+\begin{pmatrix}u_{11}^{(1)}&u_{12}^{(1)}\\
u_{21}^{(1)}&-u_{11}^{(1)}\end{pmatrix}\partial^{-1}+\begin{pmatrix}u_{11}^{(2)}&-\frac{1}{2}u_{12x}^{(1)}\\
-\frac{1}{2}u_{21x}^{(1)}&u_{11}^{(2)}+u_{11x}^{(1)}\end{pmatrix}\partial^{-2}+

\displaystyle+\begin{pmatrix}u_{11}^{(3)}&u_{12}^{(3)}\\
u_{21}^{(3)}&-u_{11}^{(3)}-2u_{11x}^{(2)}-u_{11xx}^{(1)}\end{pmatrix}\partial^{-3}+\cdots,

D=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}0&u_{12}^{(1)}\\
u_{21}^{(1)}&0\end{pmatrix}\partial^{-2}+\begin{pmatrix}0&-u_{12x}^{(1)}\\
-u_{21x}^{(1)}&0\end{pmatrix}\partial^{-3}+\cdots

We obtain the following set of equations from the Lax equations for
L
:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial u_{11}^{(1)}}{\partial t_{2}}

\displaystyle=\left(2u_{11}^{(2)}+u_{11x}^{(1)}\right)_{x}\,,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial u_{12}^{(1)}}{\partial t_{2}}

\displaystyle=2(u_{11}^{(1)}u_{12}^{(1)}+u_{12}^{(3)})\,,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial u_{21}^{(1)}}{\partial t_{2}}

\displaystyle=-2(u_{21}^{(1)}u_{11}^{(1)}+u_{21}^{(3)})\,,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial u_{11}^{(1)}}{\partial t_{3}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(6(u_{11}^{(1)})^{2}+6u_{11}^{(3)}+3u_{12}^{(1)}u_{21}^{(1)}+6u_{11x}^{(2)}+2u_{11xx}^{(1)}\right)_{x}\,,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial u_{21}^{(1)}}{\partial t_{3}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(-12u_{11}^{(2)}u_{21}^{(1)}+6u_{21x}^{(3)}-u_{21xxx}^{(1)}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial u_{12}^{(1)}}{\partial t_{3}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(12(u_{11}^{(2)}+u_{11x}^{(1)})u_{12}^{(1)}+6u_{12x}^{(3)}-u_{12xxx}^{(1)}\right)\,.

The BKP hierarchy (second approach)

In this case we assume that
r\geq 1
, in this case
s
can be 0.
We follow the approach of the previous case, setting
t^{(s+c)}_{j}=0
, but only for
1\leq c<r
, we do not put
t^{(s+r)}_{j}=0
, but leave them in the equation. Next we replace
t^{(a)}_{1}
by
t_{1}^{(a)}+x
, for
1\leq a\leq s
and
a=s+r
and define the
(2s+1)\times(2s+1)
-matrix valued wave functions

\tilde{V}^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)=P^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\pm z)R^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};\pm z)S^{\pm}(t,\pm z)e^{\pm xz},

(4.38)

where for
1\leq i,j\leq 2s
, the coefficients
P^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\pm z)_{ij}
are given by (
4.24
).
The other coefficients are as follows (
a=1,\ldots,r-1
)

\displaystyle P^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\pm z)_{2s+1,j}

\displaystyle=\frac{z^{-1}}{\tau_{\underline{\alpha}}(x,t)}e^{\mp z^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(j)}}}\left(\tau_{\underline{\alpha}\mp\underline{e}_{j}}(x,t)\right)\big|_{t^{(s+a)}=0},

(4.39)

\displaystyle P^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\pm z)_{2s+1,s+j}

\displaystyle=\frac{(-z)^{-1}}{\tau_{\underline{\alpha}}(x,t)}e^{\pm((-z^{-1})\cdot\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(j)}})}\left(\tau_{\underline{\alpha}\pm\underline{e}_{j}}(x,t)\right)\big|_{t^{(s+a)}=0},

\displaystyle P^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\pm z)_{i,2s+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau_{\underline{\alpha}}(x,t)}e^{\pm 2(z^{-1}\circ\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(s+r)}})}\left(\tau_{\underline{\alpha}\pm\underline{e}_{i}}(t)\right)\big|_{t^{(s+a)}=0},

\displaystyle P^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\pm z)_{s+i,2s+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau_{\underline{\alpha}}(x,t)}e^{\pm 2((z^{-1})\circ\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(s+r)}})}\left(\tau_{\underline{\alpha}\mp\underline{e}_{i}}(t)\right)\big|_{t^{(s+a)}=0},

\displaystyle P^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\pm z)_{2s+1,2s+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau_{\underline{\alpha}}(x,t)}e^{\pm 2((z^{-1})\circ\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(s+r)}})}\left(\tau_{\underline{\alpha}}(t)\right)\big|_{t^{(s+a)}=0},

where
\underline{\alpha}=(\underline{k},\underline{\ell})
and

|\underline{\alpha}|=|\underline{k}|+|\underline{\ell}|
.
Here,

\displaystyle R^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};\pm z)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{|\underline{\alpha}|}E_{2s+1,2s+1}+\sum_{j=1}^{s}(-1)^{|\underline{\alpha}|_{j-1}}\left(z^{\pm\alpha_{j}}E_{jj}+(-z)^{\mp\alpha_{j}}E_{s+j,s+j}\right),

(4.40)

\displaystyle S^{\pm}(t,\pm z)

\displaystyle=e^{2(z\circ t^{(s+r)})}E_{2s+1,2s+1}+\sum_{j=1}^{s}\big(e^{\pm(z\cdot t^{(j)})}E_{jj}+e^{\mp((-z)\cdot t^{(j)})}E_{s+j,s+j}\big).

We define the
(2s+1)\times(r-1)
-matrix
W^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)=\left(W^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)\right)_{i,j}
, where

W^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)_{i,j}
for
i=1,\dots 2s
is given as in
(
4.26
)
and
define

\displaystyle W^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)_{2s+1,2c-1}=

\displaystyle\sqrt{-1}(-1)^{|\underline{k}|+|\underline{\ell}|_{c-1}}\frac{e^{\pm 2((z^{-1})\circ\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(s+2c-1)}})}\tau_{\underline{k},\underline{\ell}+\underline{e}_{c}}(x,t)}{\tau_{\underline{k},\underline{\ell}}(x,t)}\big|_{t^{(s+a)}=0},

(4.41)

\displaystyle W^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)_{2s+1,2c}=

\displaystyle(-1)^{|\underline{k}|+|\underline{\ell}|_{c}}\frac{e^{\pm 2((z^{-1})\circ\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(s+2c)}})}\tau_{\underline{k},\underline{\ell}+\underline{e}_{c}}(x,t)}{\tau_{\underline{k},\underline{\ell}}(x,t)}\big|_{t^{(s+a)}=0}.

Define also the
(2s+1)\times 1
-matrix
T^{\pm}(\alpha;x,t)
, where
T^{\pm}(\alpha;x,t)_{2s+1,1}=1
and all the other coefficients are given by
(
4.28
).
Then we obtain

\displaystyle{\rm Res}_{z=0}

\displaystyle\tilde{V}^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)J(z)\tilde{V}^{-}(\underline{\beta};\overline{x},\overline{t},z)^{t}dz=

(4.42)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}W^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t)^{0}W^{-}(\underline{\beta};\overline{x},\overline{t})^{0t}+\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\delta_{r,2p}}{2}(-1)^{{{|\underline{\alpha}-\underline{\beta}}|}}\right)T^{+}(\alpha;x,t)T^{-}(\beta;\overline{x},\overline{t})^{t},

where

J(z)=\frac{1}{2}E_{2s+1,2s+1}z^{-1}+\sum_{i=1}^{s}(E_{jj}-E_{s+j,s+j}).

(4.43)

We thus obtain

\displaystyle\left(P^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\partial)R^{+}(\underline{\alpha}-\underline{\beta},\partial)J(\partial)P^{-}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)^{*}\right)_{-}=

(4.44)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}W^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t)^{0}\partial^{-1}W^{-}(\underline{\beta};x,t)^{0t}+\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\delta_{r,2p}}{2}(-1)^{{{|\underline{\alpha}-\underline{\beta}}|}}\right)T^{+}(\alpha;x,t)\partial^{-1}T^{-}(\beta;x,t)^{t}.

Now taking
\underline{\alpha}=\underline{\beta}
, it is straightforward to check that if
r=2p+1
the part with the
W^{\pm}
is equal to zero,
and that the
T^{\pm}
are equal to the constant coefficients
P^{\pm}-I+E_{2s+1,2s+1}
. If
r=2p
, the
T^{\pm}
part drops out and many things cancel in the
W^{\pm}
expression, except for the last column, which is the
2p-1
-th one, of both
W^{\pm}
. A careful inspection gives for both cases that

\displaystyle{\rm Res}_{z=0}

\displaystyle\tilde{V}^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)R^{+}(\underline{\alpha};z)J(z)R^{-}(\underline{\beta};-z)\tilde{V}^{-}(\underline{\beta};\overline{x},\overline{t},z)^{t}dz=

(4.45)

\displaystyle={\rm Res}_{z=0}P^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t)^{0}J(z)P^{-}(\underline{\beta};\overline{x},\overline{t})^{0t}dz,

where
P^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t)^{0}
is the constant coefficient of
P^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)
.
Now choosing
\underline{\alpha}=\underline{\beta}
and using the fundamental lemma, we deduce that

\left(P^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\partial)J(\partial)P^{-}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\partial)^{*}\right)_{-}=\frac{1}{2}P^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t)^{0}E_{2s+1,2s+1}\partial^{-1}P^{-}(\underline{\alpha};x,t)^{0t},

which gives that

P^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\partial)J(\partial)P^{-}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\partial)^{*}=P^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t)^{0}J(\partial)P^{-}(\underline{\alpha};x,t)^{0t}.

(4.46)

Note that
P^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t)^{0}
is invertible and
hence it makes sense to redefine the wave function and
P^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\partial)

\displaystyle\hat{V}^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)

\displaystyle=(P^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t)^{0})^{-1}\tilde{V}^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z),

(4.47)

\displaystyle\hat{P}^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\partial)

\displaystyle=(P^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t)^{0})^{-1}P^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\partial).

Then (
4.45
) turns into

\displaystyle{\rm Res}_{z=0}

\displaystyle\hat{V}^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)R^{+}(\underline{\alpha};z)J(z)R^{-}(\underline{\alpha};-z)\hat{V}^{-}(\underline{\alpha};\overline{x},\overline{t},z)^{t}dz={\rm Res}_{z=0}\,J(z)dz,

(4.48)

which means that

\hat{P}^{-}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\partial)^{*}=J(\partial)^{-1}\hat{P}^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\partial)^{-1}J(\partial).

(4.49)

Next differentiate (
4.48
) by
t_{j}^{a}
, where
j
is odd if
a=s+r
, then we obtain for
\underline{\alpha}=\underline{\beta}

\displaystyle\big[\left(\frac{\partial\hat{P}^{+}(\underline{\alpha})}{\partial t_{j}^{a}}+\hat{P}^{+}(\underline{\alpha})\left((1-\delta_{a,s+r})(E_{aa}\partial^{j}-E_{s+a,s+a}(-\partial)^{j})+2\delta_{a,s+r}E_{2s+1,2s+1}\partial^{j}\right)\right)\times

(4.50)

\displaystyle J(\partial)\hat{P}^{-}(\underline{\alpha})^{*}\big]_{-}=0.

Using (
4.46
), this turms into

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\hat{P}^{+}(\underline{\alpha})}{\partial t_{j}^{(a)}}\hat{P}^{+}(\underline{\alpha})^{-1}=

\displaystyle-\bigg(\hat{P}^{+}(\underline{\alpha})\bigg((1-\delta_{a,s+r})(E_{aa}\partial^{j}-E_{s+a,s+a}(-\partial)^{j})+

(4.51)

\displaystyle+2\delta_{a,s+r}E_{2s+1,2s+1}\partial^{j}\bigg)\hat{P}^{+}(\underline{\alpha})^{-1}J(\partial)\bigg)_{-}J(\partial)^{-1}.

Finally, following
[
16
]
, we define a new splitting of the algebra of matrix pseudo-differential operators, viz.

P(\partial)_{\geq}=(P(\partial)J(\partial))_{+}J(\partial)^{-1},\quad P(\partial)_{<}=P(\partial)-P(\partial)_{\geq},

(4.52)

Then (
4.51
), turns into

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\hat{P}^{+}(\underline{\alpha})}{\partial t_{j}^{(a)}}

\displaystyle=-\bigg(\hat{P}^{+}(\underline{\alpha})\bigg((1-\delta_{a,s+r})(E_{aa}\partial^{j}-E_{s+a,s+a}(-\partial)^{j})+

(4.53)

\displaystyle+2\delta_{a,s+r}E_{2s+1,2s+1}\partial^{j}\bigg)\hat{P}^{+}(\underline{\alpha})^{-1}\bigg)_{<}\hat{P}^{+}(\underline{\alpha}).

Define the following Lax operators (
1\leq a\leq s
):

\displaystyle L(\underline{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\hat{P}^{+}(\underline{\alpha})\left(E_{2s+1,2s+1}+\sum_{i=1}^{s}\left(E_{ii}-E_{s+i,s+i}\right)\right)\partial\hat{P}^{+}(\underline{\alpha})^{-1},

(4.54)

\displaystyle\ D^{s+r}(\underline{\alpha})

\displaystyle=2\hat{P}^{+}(\underline{\alpha})E_{2s+1,2s+1}\hat{P}^{+}(\underline{\alpha})^{-1},

\displaystyle D^{a}(\underline{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\hat{P}^{+}(\underline{\alpha})(E_{aa}-E_{s+a,s+a})\hat{P}^{+}(\underline{\alpha})^{-1}

\displaystyle E^{a}(\underline{\alpha})

\displaystyle=D^{a}(\underline{\alpha})^{2}=\hat{P}^{+}(\underline{\alpha})(E_{aa}+E_{s+a,s+a})\hat{P}^{+}(\underline{\alpha})^{-1}.

Using (
4.53
), it is straightforward to verify that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial L(\underline{\alpha})}{\partial t_{j}^{(a)}}=

\displaystyle[\left(L(\underline{\alpha})^{j}D^{a}(\underline{\alpha})\right)_{\geq},L(\underline{\alpha})],

(4.55)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial D^{b}(\underline{\alpha})}{\partial t_{j}^{(a)}}=

\displaystyle[\left(L(\underline{\alpha})^{j}D^{a}(\underline{\alpha})\right)_{\geq},D^{b}(\underline{\alpha})],

\displaystyle\frac{\partial E^{b}(\underline{\alpha})}{\partial t_{j}^{(a)}}=

\displaystyle[\left(L(\underline{\alpha})^{j}D^{a}(\underline{\alpha})\right)_{\geq},E^{b}(\underline{\alpha})].

Remark 4.3

Note that one gets the wave-functions and the Lax equations of the first approach of the BKP here as the
2s\times 2s
-principal minor, which one gets by deleting the last row and column.

Since (cf. (
4.36
))

P^{-}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\partial)=(N+E_{2s+1,2s+1})P^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\partial)(N+E_{2s+1,2s+1}),

this also holds for
\hat{P}^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\partial)
, and thus

\hat{P}^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\partial)^{*-1}=K(\partial)\hat{P}^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\partial)K(\partial)^{-1},

where

K(\partial)=-J(\partial)^{*-1}(N+E_{2s+1,2s+1})=2\partial E_{s+1,s+1}+\sum_{a=1}^{s}E_{s+a,a}-E_{a,s+a}.

Then

D^{s+r*}=K(\partial)D^{s+r}K(\partial)^{-1},\quad D^{a*}=-K(\partial)D^{a}K(\partial)^{-1},\quad E^{a*}=K(\partial)E^{a}K(\partial)^{-1},

a=1,\ldots,s
,
and

L^{*}=(L(D^{s+r}+\sum_{a=1}^{s}E^{a}))^{*}=K(\partial)(-D^{s+r}+\sum_{a=1}^{s}E^{a})LK(\partial)^{-1}.

In particular if
s=0
, then
L^{*}=-\partial L\partial^{-1}
. Note that the classical 1-component BKP hierarchy is the case that
s=0
and
r=1
.

The DKP hierarchy (first approach)

We assume that if
r\neq 0

r=2p
or
r=2p-1
and take equation (
4.13
) and set all the variables
t_{2j+1}^{(c)}=\overline{t}_{2j+1}^{(c)}=0
for
c>s
and
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. Next replace all
t_{1}^{(a)}
for
1\leq a\leq s
by
t_{1}^{(a)}+x
and similarly
\overline{t}_{1}^{(a)}
by by
\overline{t}_{1}^{(a)}+\overline{x}
.
Define the wave function
V^{\pm}(\alpha;x,t,z)
as in the first approach of the BKP case case by (
4.23
), but now for
\underline{\alpha}=(\underline{k},\underline{\ell})
, where we also substitute
t_{2j+1}^{(s+c)}=0
. Define the
s\times r
-matrix, which we assume to be zero if
r=0
,

W^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)=(W^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t)_{ij},z)_{1\leq i\leq 2s,1\leq j\leq r},=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}W^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t)^{n}z^{-n},

with coefficients as in (
4.26
) and the matrix
T^{\pm}
by

\displaystyle T^{\pm}(\alpha;x,t)_{i1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau_{\underline{k}\pm\underline{e}_{i},\underline{\ell}+\underline{e}_{p}}(x,t)}{\tau_{\underline{k},\underline{\ell}}(x,t)}\big|_{t^{(s+a)}=0},

(4.56)

\displaystyle T^{\pm}(\alpha;x,t)_{s+i,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau_{\underline{k}\mp\underline{e}_{i},\underline{\ell}+\underline{e}_{p}}(x,t)}{\tau_{\underline{k},\underline{\ell}}(x,t)}\big|_{t^{(s+a)}=0}.

We thus obtain the following bilinear identity for the wave function:

\displaystyle{\rm Res}_{z=0}\sum_{j=1}^{s}V^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)\left(E_{jj}-E_{s+j,s+j}\right)V^{-}(\underline{\beta};\overline{x},\overline{t},z)^{t}dz=

(4.57)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}W^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t)^{0}W^{-}(\underline{\beta};\overline{x},\overline{t})^{0t}+\frac{\delta_{r,2p-1}}{2}T^{+}(\alpha;x,t)T^{-}(\beta;\overline{x},\overline{t})^{t}.

Now using the fundamental lemma, we obtain that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{s}\left(P^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\partial)R^{+}(\underline{\alpha}-\underline{\beta},\partial)\left(E_{jj}-E_{s+j,s+j}\right)P^{-}(\underline{\beta};x,t,\partial)^{*}\right)_{-}=

(4.58)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}W^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t)^{0}\partial^{-1}W^{-}(\underline{\beta};x,t)^{0t}+\frac{\delta_{r,2p-1}}{2}T^{+}(\alpha;x,t)\partial^{-1}T^{-}(\beta;x,t)^{t}.

Choosing
\underline{\alpha}=\underline{\beta}
, gives that the right-hand side of (
4.58
) is equal to 0, hence we obtain the same equations as in the first approach of the BKP case.

The DKP hierarchy (second approach)

Now we assume that
r\geq 1
, in this case
s
can be 0.
r
is either
r=2p
or
r=2p-1
.
We follow the approach of the previous case,s, now setting
t^{(s+c)}_{j}=0
, but only for
1\leq c<r
, we do not put
t^{(s+r)}_{j}=0
. Next we replace
t^{(a)}_{1}
by
t_{1}^{(a)}+x
, for
1\leq a\leq s
and
a=s+r
and define the
(2s+1)\times(2s+1)
-matrix valued wave functions

\tilde{V}^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)=P^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\pm z)R^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};\pm z)S^{\pm}(t,\pm z)e^{\pm xz},

(4.59)

Where the coefficients
P^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,\pm z)
are given by (
4.24
) and (
4.39
) and the other matrices by (
4.25
). The
(2s+1)\times(r-1)
is

W^{\pm}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)
is again defined as in (
4.27
) and (
4.41
) and
T^{\pm}\underline{\alpha};x,t)
as in the BKP second approach case.
Then we obtain the following bilinear identity:

\displaystyle{\rm Res}_{z=0}

\displaystyle\tilde{V}^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t,z)J(z)\tilde{V}^{-}(\underline{\beta};\overline{x},\overline{t},z)^{t}dz=

(4.60)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}W^{+}(\underline{\alpha};x,t)^{0}W^{-}(\underline{\beta};\overline{x},\overline{t})^{0t}+\frac{\delta_{r,2p-1}}{2}T^{+}(\alpha;x,t)T^{-}(\beta;\overline{x},\overline{t})^{t},

where
J(z)
is as in (
4.43
).
One can now continue as in the second approach of the BKP case and nothing changes. This is not surprising considering the first approach in the DKP case and considering Remark
4.1
.

Remark 4.4

Note that the first approach gives the same wave-functions and Lax operators in both the BKP as DKP hierarchy as in the case
r=0
. The second approach with
r>1
does not differ from the case
r=1
. Hence both approaches seem to be superfluous. This however will not be the case if we consider reductions to affine Lie algebras. The case
r=0
will produce a differential operator like in the Gelfand-Dickey case, but the first approach for
r>0
, will produce a Lax operator which is of constrained KP-type.

The CKP hierarchy

As in the previous cases, we replace
t^{b}_{1}
by
t_{1}^{b}+x
, for
1\leq b\leq r+s
, and introduce
(2s+r)\times(2s+r)
-matrix valued wave functions

V^{\pm}(x,t,z)=P^{\pm}(x,t,\pm z)S^{\pm}(t,\pm z)e^{\pm xz},

(4.61)

where

\displaystyle S^{\pm}(t,\pm z)=

\displaystyle\sum_{a=1}^{s}\big(e^{\pm(z\cdot t^{(a)})}E_{aa}+e^{\mp((-z)\cdot t^{(a)})}E_{s+a,s+a}\big)+\sum_{c=s+1}^{r+s}e^{\pm(z\circ t^{(c)})}E_{a+c,a+c},

\displaystyle P^{\pm}(x,t,\pm z)=

\displaystyle\left(P^{\pm}(x,t,\pm z)_{i,j}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq r+2s},

and (
1\leq a\neq b\leq s\leq c,d\leq r+s
)

\displaystyle\tau_{0}(x,t)P^{\pm}(x,t,\pm z)_{a,a}

\displaystyle=e^{\pm z^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(a)}}}\left(\tau_{0}(x,t)-\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\tau_{\xi^{a}_{\mp k}\cup\xi^{a}_{\pm 1}}(x,t)z^{-k-1}\right),

(4.62)

\displaystyle\tau_{0}(x,t)P^{\pm}(x,t,\pm z)_{a,b}

\displaystyle=e^{\pm z^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(b)}}}\left(\mp\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\tau_{\xi^{b}_{\mp k}\cup\xi^{a}_{\pm 1}}(x,t)z^{-k}\right),

\displaystyle\tau_{0}(x,t)P^{\pm}(x,t,\pm z)_{a,s+a}

\displaystyle=e^{\mp((-z^{-1})\cdot\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(a)}})}\left(-\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\tau_{\xi^{a}_{\pm k}\cup\xi^{a}_{\pm 1}}(x,t)(-z)^{1-k}\right),

\displaystyle\tau_{0}(x,t)P^{\pm}(x,t,\pm z)_{a,s+b}

\displaystyle=e^{\mp((-z^{-1})\cdot\tilde{\partial}_{t_{k}^{(b)}})}\left(\pm\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\tau_{\xi^{b}_{\pm k}\cup\xi^{a}_{\pm 1}}(x,t)(-z)^{-k}\right),

\displaystyle\tau_{0}(x,t)P^{\pm}(x,t,\pm z)_{a,s+c}

\displaystyle=e^{\pm 2(z^{-1}\circ\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(s+c)}})}\left(-\sum_{m\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\!\!\!\!\tau_{\xi_{\pm 1}^{a}\cup\xi_{m}^{c}}(t)(\pm z)^{-m-\frac{1}{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\tau_{0}(x,t)P^{\pm}(x,t,\pm z)_{s+a,a}

\displaystyle=e^{\pm z^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(a)}}}\left(-\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\tau_{\xi^{a}_{\mp k}\cup\xi^{a}_{\mp 1}}(x,t)z^{1-k}\right),

\displaystyle\tau_{0}(x,t)P^{\pm}(x,t,\pm z)_{s+c,a}

\displaystyle=e^{\pm z^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(a)}}}\left(\mp\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\tau_{\xi^{a}_{\mp k}\cup\xi^{c}_{\frac{1}{2}}}(x,t)z^{-k}\right),

\displaystyle\tau_{0}(x,t)P^{\pm}(x,t,\pm z)_{s+c,s+c}

\displaystyle=e^{\pm 2(z^{-1}\circ\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(s+c)}})}\left(\tau_{0}(x,t)-\sum_{m\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}\!\!\!\!\tau_{\xi_{\frac{1}{2}}^{c}\cup\xi_{m}^{c}}(t)(\pm z)^{-m-\frac{1}{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\tau_{0}(x,t)P^{\pm}(x,t,\pm z)_{s+c,s+d}

\displaystyle=e^{\pm 2(z^{-1}\circ\tilde{\partial}_{t^{(s+r)}})}\left(-\sum_{m\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\!\!\!\!\tau_{\xi_{\frac{1}{2}}^{c}\cup\xi_{m}^{d}}(t)(\pm z)^{-m-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

The other coefficients are given by:

\displaystyle P^{\pm}(x,t,\partial)_{s+a,s+a}

\displaystyle=P^{\mp}(x,t,\partial)_{a,a},

(4.63)

\displaystyle P^{\pm}(x,t,,\partial)_{s+a,s+b}

\displaystyle=P^{\mp}(x,t,\partial)_{a,b},

\displaystyle P^{\pm}(x,t,\partial)_{s+a,b}

\displaystyle=P^{\mp}(x,t,\partial)_{a,s+b},

\displaystyle P^{\pm}(x,t,\partial)_{s+a,s+c}

\displaystyle=P^{\mp}(x,t,\partial)_{a,s+c},

\displaystyle P^{\pm}(x,t,\partial)_{s+c,s+a}

\displaystyle=P^{\mp}(x,t,\partial)_{s+c,a}.

Then (
4.16
) gives

{\rm Res}_{z=0}V^{+}(x,t,z)V^{-}(\overline{x},\overline{t},z)^{T}dz=0.

(4.64)

Note that
P^{\pm}(x,t,\partial)=I_{r+2s}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}P^{\pm}_{k}(x,t)\partial^{-k}
and that

P^{\pm}(x,t,\partial)=(N+I)P^{\mp}(x,t,\partial)(N+I),

(4.65)

where (cf. (
4.36
))

N=\sum_{a=1}^{s}\left(E_{a,s+a}+E_{s+a,a}\right)\quad\mbox{and }I=\sum_{c=s+1}^{s+r}E_{s+c,s+c}.

Hence, applying the fundamental lemma gives
that

\displaystyle P^{\mp}(x,t,\partial)^{-1}

\displaystyle=P^{\pm}(x,t,\partial)^{*}

(4.66)

\displaystyle=\left((N+I)P^{\mp}(x,t,\partial)(N+I)\right)^{*}

\displaystyle=(N+I)P^{\mp}(x,t,\partial)^{*}(N+I).

Next differntiate (
4.61
) by
t^{a}_{k}
,
t^{c}_{\ell}
for
1\leq a\leq s
,
s+1\leq c\leq r+s
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{odd>0}
and apply the fundamental lemma, this gives the following Sato-Wilson equation:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial P^{+}(x,t,\partial)}{\partial t^{(a)}_{k}}

\displaystyle=-\left(P^{+}(x,t,\partial)\left(\partial^{k}E_{aa}-(-\partial)^{k}E_{s+a,s+a}\right)P^{+}(x,t,\partial)^{-1}\right)_{-}P^{+}(x,t,\partial),

(4.67)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial P^{+}(x,t,\partial)}{\partial t^{(c)}_{\ell}}

\displaystyle=-\left(P^{+}(x,t,\partial)\partial^{\ell}E_{s+c,s+c}P^{+}(x,t,\partial)^{-1}\right)_{-}P^{+}(x,t,\partial).

Define the following Lax operators

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle=L(x,t,\partial)=P^{+}(x,t,\partial)\left(\sum_{a=1}^{s}\left(\partial E_{aa}-\partial E_{s+a,s+a}\right)+\sum_{c=s+1}^{s+c}\partial E_{s+c,s+c}\right)P^{+}(x,t,\partial)^{-1},

(4.68)

\displaystyle C^{b}

\displaystyle=C^{b}(x,t,\partial)=P^{+}(x,t,\partial)E_{bb}P^{+}(x,t,\partial)^{-1},\qquad 1\leq b\leq r+2s,

\displaystyle D^{a}

\displaystyle=D^{a}(x,t,\partial)=P^{+}(x,t,\partial)(E_{aa}-E_{s+a,s+a})P^{+}(x,t,\partial)^{-1},

\displaystyle E^{a}

\displaystyle=(D^{a})^{2}=E^{a}(x,t,\partial)=P^{+}(x,t,\partial)(E_{aa}+E_{s+a,s+a})P^{+}(x,t,\partial)^{-1}.

Now let
a=1,\ldots s
,
c=s+1,\ldots r+s
,
b=1,\ldots r+2s
, then we have the following Lax Equations

\displaystyle\frac{\partial L}{\partial t^{(a)}_{j}}

\displaystyle=[(L^{j}D^{a}_{+},L],\quad\frac{\partial C^{b}}{\partial t^{(a)}_{j}}=[(L^{j}D^{a})_{+},C^{b}],

(4.69)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial L}{\partial t^{(c)}_{j}}

\displaystyle=[(L^{j}C^{s+c})_{+},L],\qquad\frac{\partial C^{b}}{\partial t^{(c)}_{j}}=[(L^{j}C^{s+c}_{+},C^{b}].

From (
4.66
) we deduce that

C^{s+b*}=(N+I)C^{s+b}(N+I),\quad E^{a*}=(N+I)E^{a}(N+I),\quad D^{a*}=-(N+I)D^{a}(N+I),

1
\leq a\leq s<b\leq r+s
,
and

\displaystyle L^{*}

\displaystyle=((\sum_{a=1}^{s}E^{a}+\sum_{b=s+1}^{r+s}C^{s+b})L)^{*}=(\sum_{a=1}^{s}E^{a}+\sum_{b=s+1}^{r+s}C^{s+b})L

\displaystyle=(N+I)((\sum_{a=1}^{s}E^{a}-\sum_{b=s+1}^{r+s}C^{s+b})L)(N+I).

There are two special cases, viz. if
s=0
or
r=0
, which give that

\displaystyle s=0:

\displaystyle L(x,t,\partial)^{*}=-L(x,t,\partial),\qquad C^{c}(x,t,\partial)^{*}=C^{c}(x,t,\partial),

\displaystyle r=0:

\displaystyle L(x,t,\partial)^{*}=NL(x,t,\partial)N,\qquad D^{a}(x,t,\partial)^{*}=-ND^{a}(x,t,\partial)N,

Note that the classical 1-component CKP case (cf.
[
26
]
,
[
21
]
) corresponds to
s=0
and
r=1
, hence the case that
L^{*}=-L
.

5
Conjugacy classes of the Weyl group for classical Lie algebras and generating fields for
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}
and
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}^{(2)}

5.1
Weyl group elements and the twisted realizations of
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be one of the classical Lie algebras over
\mathbb{C}
and let
G
be the corresponding classical complex Lie group. Choose a Cartan subalgebra
\mathfrak{h}
in
\mathfrak{g}
and let
W\in GL(\mathfrak{h})
be the Weyl group. Fix
w\in W
and let
\mathfrak{h}_{0}
be the fixed point set of
w
in
\mathfrak{h}
. We can lift
w
(non-uniquely) to an element
\tilde{w}\in G
of order
N
, such that

\tilde{w}=Se^{2\pi\sqrt{-1}h_{w}}S^{-1},

(5.1)

where
S\in G
and
h_{w}\in\mathfrak{h}
is such that

(h_{w}|\mathfrak{h}_{0})=0,

(5.2)

in all cases, except for
so_{2n+1}
. For the level one
so_{2n+1}
-module with the highest weight
\Lambda_{n}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}
, condition (
5.2
) is replaced by

(h_{w}-\Lambda_{n}|\mathfrak{h}_{0})=0.

(5.3)

Consider the
\mathbb{Z}_{N}=\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}
-gradation of
\mathfrak{g}
defined by
\sigma=e^{2\pi\sqrt{-1}{\rm ad}\,h_{w}}
:

\mathfrak{g}=\bigoplus_{\overline{j}\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}}\mathfrak{g}_{\overline{j}},\quad\mbox{where }\mathfrak{g}_{\overline{j}}=\{g\in\mathfrak{g}\,|\,[h_{w},g]=\frac{j}{N}g\ \mbox{ for some }j\in\mathbb{Z},\ \overline{j}\equiv j\,{\rm mod}\,N\}.

(5.4)

Define the twisted affine Lie algebra
\hat{L}(\mathfrak{g},\sigma)
as the following subalgebra of
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}
:

\hat{L}(\mathfrak{g},\sigma)=\mathbb{C}K\oplus\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}t^{j}\mathfrak{g}_{j\,{\rm mod}\,N}.

(5.5)

The Lie algebra
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}
and
\hat{L}(\mathfrak{g},\sigma)
are isomorphic for any inner automorphism
\sigma
of
\mathfrak{g}
(
[
13
]
, Chapter 8). Explicitly the isomorphism
\tilde{\phi}_{h_{w}}:\hat{L}(\mathfrak{g},\sigma)\to\hat{\mathfrak{g}}
is given by
[
19
]
,
[
22
]

\tilde{\phi}_{h_{w}}(t^{j}g)=\hat{g}(j):=g(j)-\delta_{j,0}(h_{w}|g)K\quad\mbox{for }g\in\mathfrak{g}_{j\,{\rm mod}N},

(5.6)

where

g(j)=t^{-{\rm ad}\,h_{w}}(t^{\frac{j}{N}}g)\in\hat{\mathfrak{g}},\quad g\in\mathfrak{g}_{j\,{\rm mod}N}.

(5.7)

Each element
g\in\mathfrak{g}
decomposes with respect to the
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
-gradation (
5.4
)
as
g=\sum_{\overline{j}\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}}g_{\overline{j}}
, and we consider the corresponding generating field

g(z)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\hat{g}_{j\,{\rm mod}\,N}(j)z^{-j-1}.

(5.8)

Let
d=\dim\mathfrak{g}
and let
g^{1},g^{2},\ldots,g^{d}
be the standard basis of
\mathfrak{g}
, i.e. the root vectors together with a basis of
\mathfrak{h}
. Then we obtain a new basis
a^{i}=S^{-1}g^{i}S
, which has the same commutation relations as the
g^{i}
.
One of the main constructions of this paper will be the construction of the vertex operators for
a^{i}(z)
. Note that the standard Cartan subalgebra
\mathfrak{h}
gets conjugated to a non-standard one

\mathfrak{h}_{\sigma}

by
S^{-1}
. Hence if
h^{1},h^{2},\ldots h^{n}
is a basis of
\mathfrak{h}
, the modes of the vertex operators for
(S^{-1}h^{i}S)(z)
, for
1\leq i\leq n
(see formula (
5.8
)), together with
K
span a non-standard Heisenberg subalgebra of
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}
. Of course for
w=1
we get the standard homogeneous
Heisenberg subalgebra
(\mathfrak{h}\otimes\mathbb{C}[t,t^{-1}])\oplus\mathbb{C}K
.

Example 5.1

(The Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}=gl_{2}
)

There are two elements in the Weyl group, namely the identity

id
and the element
w
that interchanges
\epsilon_{1}
and
\epsilon_{2}
. The first case is related to the homogeneous realization and the second to the principal realization of the subalgebra
\hat{sl}_{2}
.
It is easy to find a lift in both cases satisfying (
5.2
):

I=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},\quad\tilde{w}=\begin{pmatrix}0&-i\\
-i&0\end{pmatrix},\ \mbox{respectively}.

Then

S=I
in the first case and
S=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}-1&1\\
1&1\end{pmatrix}
for the second one, which leads respectively to the following choices of
h_{w}
:

I=\exp(2\pi\sqrt{-1}h_{1}),\ \mbox{where $h_{1}=0$, and }\tilde{w}=\exp(2\pi\sqrt{-1}h_{w}),\ \mbox{ where }h_{w}=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{4}&0\\
0&-\frac{1}{4}\end{pmatrix}.

In the first case we have 4 fields, for
\hat{gl}_{2}
, that generate the algebra, namely
e_{ij}(z)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}t^{k}e_{ij}z^{-k-1}
,
1\leq i,j\leq 2
. For
\hat{sl}_{2}
we have 3 fields,
namely
e_{12}(z)
,
e_{21}(z)
and
(e_{11}-e_{22})(z)
.
In the second case we have only two fields, for
\hat{gl}_{2}
, namely

\displaystyle\left(S^{-1}e_{22}S\right)(z)=-\left(S^{-1}e_{11}S\right)(-z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{t^{k}}{2}\begin{pmatrix}1&z^{-1}\\
tz^{-1}&1\end{pmatrix}z^{-2k-1},

\displaystyle\left(S^{-1}e_{12}S\right)(z)=-\left(S^{-1}e_{21}S\right)(-z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{t^{k}}{2}\begin{pmatrix}-1&-z^{-1}\\
tz^{-1}&1\end{pmatrix}z^{-2k-1}.

For
\hat{sl}_{2}
we have two fields:
(S^{-1}e_{12}S)(z)
and

\left(S^{-1}(e_{22}-e_{11})S\right)(z)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}{t^{k}}\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
t&0\end{pmatrix}z^{-2k-2}

The modes of the second field, together with
K
, form a basis of the so called principal Heisenberg subalgebra of
\hat{sl}_{2}
.

5.2
gl_{n}

Recall that the conjugacy classes of the Weyl group
W=S_{n}
for
gl_{n}
are in one-to-one correspondence with the partitions

\lambda=(\lambda_{1}\geq\lambda_{2}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{s}>0)

(5.9)

of
n
,
i.e.

|\lambda|=\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}+\cdots+\lambda_{s}=n.

(5.10)

We can decompose each element
w\in W
in a product of disjoint cycles of length
\lambda_{i}
. Then two elements with the same cycle type are conjugate.
Let
|\lambda|_{0}=0
and denote for
j=1,2\ldots s
,

|\lambda|_{j}=\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}+\cdots+\lambda_{j},

(5.11)

thus
|\lambda|=|\lambda|_{s}
.
We can associate to a partition
\lambda
of
n
the standard element
w_{\lambda}\in W
, namely in the basis
\{\epsilon_{i}\}
of
\mathfrak{h}
:

w_{\lambda}:\epsilon_{|\lambda|_{j}}\mapsto\epsilon_{|\lambda|_{j-1}+1},\ \mbox{and }\epsilon_{|\lambda|_{j-1}+i}\mapsto\epsilon_{|\lambda|_{j-1}+i+1},\ i=1,2,\ldots,\lambda_{j}-1,\quad 1\leq j\leq s.

(5.12)

We obtain this
w_{\lambda}
if we restrict

{\rm Ad}\,\tilde{w}_{\lambda}
to the Cartan subalgebra
\mathfrak{h}
, where

\tilde{w}_{\lambda}=\sum_{a=1}^{s}\left(e_{1,\lambda_{a}}^{aa}+\sum_{i=1}^{\lambda_{a}-1}e_{i+1,i}^{aa}\right)\in GL_{n},

(5.13)

where we redefine the basis vectors as follows:

e_{ij}^{ab}=e_{|\lambda|_{a-1}+i,|\lambda|_{b-1}+j},\quad 1\leq i\leq\lambda_{a},\ 1\leq j\leq\lambda_{b},\ 1\leq a,b\leq s.

(5.14)

So for the diagonal block related to the part of
\lambda_{j}
this is the Coxeter element in the Weyl group of
gl_{\lambda_{j}}
. Hence, it will be useful to study this case first.

Example 5.2

(One cycle, the principal Heisenberg algebra)

Assume that
s=1
, i.e.
\lambda=(n)
and let

\tilde{w}_{(n)}^{\prime}=e_{1n}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}e_{i+1,i}.

Let
\omega=e^{\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}
. The vectors
(\omega^{j},\omega^{2j},\cdots,\omega^{nj})^{T}
are eigenvectors of
\tilde{w}
with the eigenvalue
\omega^{-j}
. Thus let

S=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\omega^{ij}e_{ij},\quad\mbox{ then }S^{-1}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\omega^{-ij}e_{ij}

and

S^{-1}\tilde{w}_{(n)}^{\prime}S=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\omega^{-j}e_{jj}=\exp\left(2\pi\sqrt{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{n}-\frac{j}{n}e_{jj}\right).

Hence we could choose
h_{w}^{\prime}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}-\frac{j}{n}e_{jj}
, then
{\rm Ad}\,\tilde{w}_{(n)}=Se^{2\pi\sqrt{-1}{\rm ad}\,h_{w}}S^{-1}
. However,
(h_{w}^{\prime}|I_{n})\neq 0
, but we can always add a multiple of
I_{n}
to
h_{w}^{\prime}
, hence we choose

h_{w}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{n-2j+1}{2n}e_{jj},\quad\mbox{so that }(h_{w}|\mathfrak{h}_{0})=0.

Note that this means that we let

\tilde{w}_{(n)}=\omega^{\frac{n+1}{2}}\tilde{w}_{(n)}^{\prime}.

Now, let

a_{k\ell}=S^{-1}e_{k\ell}S=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\omega^{j\ell-ik}e_{ij}\in gl_{n},

then

a_{k\ell}(z)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\omega^{j\ell-ik}(t^{m}e_{ij})z^{-mn+i-j-1}.

Using (
2.22
) and (
3.2
),
we find that

r(a_{k\ell})(z)=\frac{\omega^{-k}}{n}\sum_{m,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\omega^{j\ell-ik}:\psi^{+}_{-\ell n-i+\frac{1}{2}}\psi^{-}_{(\ell+m)n+j-\frac{1}{2}}:z^{-mn+i-j-1}.

In terms of the charged fermionic fields

\psi^{\pm}(z)=\sum_{i\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}}\psi_{i}^{\pm}z^{-i-\frac{1}{2}},

(5.15)

this becomes

r(a_{k\ell})(z)=\frac{1}{n}:\psi^{+}(\omega^{-k}z)\psi^{-}(\omega^{-\ell}z):,

and in particular

\alpha(z)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\alpha_{j}z^{-j-1}=nr(a_{nn})(z)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}r((te_{n1}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}e_{i,i+1})^{j})z^{-j-1}=:\psi^{+}(z)\psi^{-}(z):

will be the generating field for the principal Heisenberg algebra, i.e.

[\alpha_{j},\alpha_{k}]=j\delta_{jk},\quad\alpha_{j}|0\rangle=0,\quad\mbox{for }j\geq 0.

The general construction follows from this example.
Consider the partition(
5.9
) of
n
. Introduce the associated relabeling,
j\equiv|\lambda|_{a-1}+1,\ldots,|\lambda|_{a}\mod n
, induced by (
5.14
), (
2.22
) and (
3.2
):

\displaystyle\psi^{+a}_{j\lambda_{a}-i+\frac{1}{2}}=\psi^{+}_{jn-|\lambda|_{a-1}-i+\frac{1}{2}},\quad\psi^{-a}_{j\lambda_{a}+i-\frac{1}{2}}=\psi^{-}_{jn+|\lambda|_{a-1}+i-\frac{1}{2}},

(5.16)

for
1\leq i\leq\lambda_{a}
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,
a=1,\ldots,s
.
Let
\omega_{a}=e^{\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{\lambda_{a}}}
. It is straightforward to deduce the following proposition from the above example.

Proposition 5.3

(a) The element

\tilde{w}_{\lambda}=\left(\sum_{a=1}^{s}\omega_{a}^{\frac{\lambda_{a}+1}{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{\lambda_{a}}e^{aa}_{jj}\right)\tilde{w}_{\lambda}^{\prime}\in GL_{n}

for
\tilde{w}_{\lambda}^{\prime}
as in (
5.13
), is equal to
S^{-1}\exp(2\pi\sqrt{-1}h_{w})S
, where

h_{w}=\sum_{a=1}^{s}\sum_{i=1}^{\lambda_{a}}\frac{\lambda_{a}-2j+1}{2\lambda_{a}}e_{jj}^{aa}\in\mathfrak{h}

satisfies
(
5.2
),
and

S=\sum_{a=1}^{s}\sum_{i=1}^{\lambda_{a}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda}_{a}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{\lambda_{a}}\omega_{a}^{ij}e_{ij}^{aa},\quad S^{-1}=\sum_{a=1}^{s}\sum_{i=1}^{\lambda_{a}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda}_{a}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{\lambda_{a}}\omega_{a}^{-ij}e_{ij}^{aa}.

(b) The order
N
of
\exp(2\pi\sqrt{-1}{\rm ad}\,h_{w})
is equal to the least common multiple
N^{\prime}
of
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\ldots,\lambda_{s}
if
N^{\prime}(\frac{1}{\lambda_{a}}+\frac{1}{\lambda_{b}})\in 2\mathbb{Z}
for all
1\leq a,b\leq s
and
2N^{\prime}
otherwise.

(c) The elements

a_{k,\ell}^{ab}:=S^{-1}e_{k\ell}^{ab}S=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda_{a}\lambda_{b}}}\sum_{i=1}^{\lambda_{a}}\sum_{j=1}^{\lambda_{b}}\omega_{a}^{-ik}\omega_{b}^{j\ell}e_{ij}^{ab}

form a new basis of
gl_{n}
and the representation
r
in
F
of their generating fields

a_{k,\ell}^{ab}(z)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda_{a}\lambda_{b}}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{i=1}^{\lambda_{a}}\sum_{j=1}^{\lambda_{b}}\left(\omega_{a}^{-ik}\omega_{b}^{j\ell}t^{m}e_{ij}^{ab}\right)z^{-mN+{i}{\frac{N}{\lambda_{a}}}-{j}{\frac{N}{\lambda_{b}}}-1},

are equal to

r(a_{k,\ell}^{ab})(z)=\frac{\omega_{a}^{-k}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{a}\lambda_{b}}}:\psi^{+a}(\omega_{a}^{-k}z^{\frac{N}{\lambda_{a}}})\psi^{-b}(\omega_{b}^{-\ell}z^{\frac{N}{\lambda_{b}}}):z^{\frac{N}{\lambda_{a}}-1},

(5.17)

where

\psi^{\pm a}(z)=\sum_{i\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}}\psi_{i}^{\pm a}z^{-i-\frac{1}{2}}

are the generating fields
for the relabeld generators
\psi_{j}^{\pm a}
of the Clifford algebra
C\ell
,
j\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
and
1\leq a\leq s
.

(d) The representation of
\hat{gl}_{n}
is given in terms of vertex operators by formula (
3.66
), by substitution of roots of unity
\omega_{a}
and powers of
z
as given
in formula (
5.17
). In particular, the free bosonic fields are

\alpha^{b}(z)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\alpha_{j}^{b}z^{-j-1}=:\psi^{+b}(z)\psi^{-b}(z):\quad 1\leq b\leq s,

so that
\alpha^{b}(z)=\lambda_{b}z^{\frac{\lambda_{a}}{N}-1}r(a_{\lambda_{b}\lambda_{b}}^{bb})(z^{\frac{\lambda_{a}}{N}})
and their modes satisfy the commutation relations of a Heisenberg Lie algebra of "rank"
s
:

[\alpha^{a}_{j},\alpha^{b}_{k}]=j\delta_{ab}\delta_{jk},\quad j,k\in\mathbb{Z},\ a,b=1,\ldots,s,\quad\alpha_{j}^{a}|0\rangle=0,\quad\mbox{for }j\geq 0.\qquad\qquad\qquad\hfill\square

Let

\hat{\mathfrak{a}}_{\lambda}
be the span of
K
and the elements appearing as the modes of the fields
a^{bb}_{\lambda_{b}\lambda_{b}}
for
1\leq b\leq s
.
It is a Heisenberg subalgebra of
\hat{gl}_{n}
.
Then the
\hat{gl}_{n}
-module

F^{(m)}
, when restricted to the subalgebra
\hat{\mathfrak{a}}_{\lambda}

remains irreducible when
\lambda=(n)
and is not irreducible, otherwise.
It was shown
[
19
]
, that the representation remains irreducible under
\hat{\mathfrak{a}}_{\lambda}
and the centralizer of
\hat{\mathfrak{a}}_{\lambda}
in
SL_{n}(\mathbb{C}[t,t^{-1}])
. This means in this case that we have to add the operators that describe the group action of the elements

\sum_{b=1}^{s}\frac{1}{\lambda_{b}}a^{bb}_{\lambda_{b}\lambda_{b}}(k_{b}),\quad k_{b}\in\mathbb{Z},\quad k_{1}+\cdots+k_{s}=0.

These are the operators
Q_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots Q_{s}^{k_{s}}
, wich are products of
Q_{i}Q_{i+1}^{-1}
for
i=1,\ldots s-1
and their inverses.
This gives the (twisted) group algebra of the root lattice of
sl_{s}
.

The
q
-dimension formula for the
\hat{gl}_{n}
module
F
now differs from the formula of the
a_{\infty}
-module (
3.39
), since the gradation is different, see e.g.
[
22
]
.
Let
N
be the least common multiple of
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{s}
, then
[
22
]

\dim_{q}F^{(m)}=\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{2}{j_1, \ldots, j_s\in\mathbb Z}{j_1+\cdots+j_s=m}}q^{\frac{N}{2}(\frac{j_{1}^{2}}{\lambda_{1}^{2}}+\frac{j_{2}^{2}}{\lambda_{2}^{2}}+\cdots+\frac{j_{s}^{2}}{\lambda_{s}^{2}})}\prod_{a=1}^{s}\prod_{i=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{1-q^{\frac{jN}{\lambda_{a}}}}.

The module
F^{(m)}
when restricted to
\hat{sl}_{n}
is no longer irreducible. However, it is straightforward to calculate the
q
-dimension formula of the irreducible component
F_{sl_{n}}^{(m)}
, that contains
|m\rangle
. For that one only has to remove all

\alpha_{j\lambda_{1}}^{1}+\alpha_{j\lambda_{2}}^{2}+\cdots+\alpha_{j\lambda_{s}}^{1}s\quad\mbox{for all }j\in\mathbb{Z}.

Since such an element has energy
jN
, this gives,

\dim_{q}F_{sl_{n}}^{(m)}=\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{2}{j_1, \ldots, j_s\in\mathbb Z}{j_1+\cdots+j_s=m}}q^{\frac{N}{2}(\frac{j_{1}^{2}}{\lambda_{1}^{2}}+\frac{j_{2}^{2}}{\lambda_{2}^{2}}+\cdots+\frac{j_{s}^{2}}{\lambda_{s}^{2}})}\prod_{j=1}^{\infty}(1-q^{jN})\prod_{a=1}^{s}\prod_{i=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{1-q^{\frac{jN}{\lambda_{a}}}}.

5.3
sp_{2n}

Recall that the conjugacy classes of the Weyl group
W
of
sp_{2n}
are in one-to-one correspondence with the pair of partitions

\lambda=(\lambda_{1}\geq\lambda_{2}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{s}>0)\quad\mbox{and }\mu=(\mu_{1}\geq\mu_{2},\geq\cdots\geq\mu_{r}>0),

(5.18)

of
n
,
i.e.

|\lambda|+|\mu|=\lambda_{1}+\cdots+\lambda_{s}+\mu_{1}+\cdots+\mu_{r}=n.

(5.19)

Namely, each element
w\in W
decomposes in a product of
s
disjoint positive cycles of length
\lambda_{i}
, and
r
disjoint negative cycles of length
\mu_{j}
, such that (
5.19
) holds. Two elements with the same cycle type are conjugate.

We can associate to a pair of partition
(\lambda,\mu)
of
n
a standard element
w_{(\lambda,\mu)}\in W
, namely:

\displaystyle w_{(\lambda,\mu)}:

\displaystyle\epsilon_{|\lambda|_{i}}\mapsto\epsilon_{|\lambda|_{i-1}+1},\ \epsilon_{|\lambda|+|\mu|_{j}}\mapsto-\epsilon_{|\lambda|+|\mu|_{j-1}+1},

(5.20)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{|\lambda|_{i-1}+k}\mapsto\epsilon_{|\lambda|_{i-1}+k+1},\quad k=1,2,\ldots,\lambda_{i}-1,\quad 1\leq i\leq s,

\displaystyle\epsilon_{|\lambda|+|\mu|_{j-1}+k}\mapsto\epsilon_{|\lambda|+|\mu|_{j-1}+k+1},\quad k=1,2,\ldots,\mu_{j}-1,\quad 1\leq j\leq r.

This suggests to redefine the elements that span
sp_{2n}
as follows.
Let
m_{a}=\lambda_{a}
,
M_{a}=|\lambda|_{a-1}
for
1\leq a\leq s
and
m_{a}=\mu_{a-s}
,
M_{a}=|\lambda|+|\mu|_{a-s-1}
for
s<a\leq r+s
and
M_{r+s+1}=|\lambda|+|\mu|
,. Let (
1\leq a,b\leq s+r
,
1\leq i\leq m_{a}
,
1\leq j\leq m_{b}
)

\displaystyle e_{ij}^{+a,-b}=

\displaystyle e_{M_{a}+i,M_{b}+j}-(-1)^{M_{a}+M_{b}+i+j}e_{2n+1-M_{b}-j,2n+1-M_{a}-i}

(5.21)

\displaystyle e_{ij}^{+a,+b}=

\displaystyle(-1)^{M_{a}+M_{b}+i+j}e_{ji}^{+b,+a}=e_{M_{a}+i,2n+1-M_{b}-j}-(-1)^{M_{a}+M_{b}+i+j+1}e_{M_{b}+j,2n+1-M_{a}-i}

\displaystyle e_{ij}^{-a,-b}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{M_{a}+M_{b}+i+j}e_{ji}^{-b,-a}=e_{2n+1-M_{a}-i,M_{b}+j}-(-1)^{M_{a}+M_{b}+i+j+1}e_{2n+1-M_{b}-j,M_{a}+i}.

Before finding the lift of the element (
5.20
), we first investigate two special cases, which we present as examples.

Example 5.4

(One positive cycle)

In this case
s=1
and
r=0
, hence

w_{((n),\emptyset)}:\epsilon_{n}\mapsto\epsilon_{1},\quad\epsilon_{k}\mapsto\epsilon_{k+1},\quad k=1,2,\ldots,n.

(5.22)

We let

\tilde{w}=\tilde{w}_{((n),\emptyset)}=e_{1,n}-(-1)^{n}e_{2n,n+1}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}(e_{i+1,i}-e_{2n-i,2n+1-i}).

Then it is straightforward to check that
(\tilde{w}_{((n),\emptyset)}u,\tilde{w}_{((n),\emptyset)}v)_{sp_{2n}}=(u,v)_{sp_{2n}}
for
u,v\in\mathbb{C}^{2n}
, i.e.
\tilde{w}\in Sp_{2n}
, and that
{\rm Ad}\,\tilde{w}_{((n),\emptyset)}|_{\mathfrak{h}}=w_{((n),\emptyset)}
.

The matrix
\tilde{w}
has eigenvectors
(\omega^{j},\omega^{2j},\ldots\omega^{nj},0,\cdots,0)^{T}
and
(0,\ldots,0,(-\omega^{j})^{n},(-\omega^{j})^{n-1},\ldots,-\omega^{j})^{T}
with eigenvalue
\omega^{-j}
, where as before
\omega=e^{\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}
.
Set

S=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}(\omega^{ij}e_{ij}+(-1)^{i+j}\omega^{-ij}e_{2n+1-i,2n+1-j}),

then

S^{-1}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}(\omega^{-ij}e_{ij}+(-1)^{i+j}\omega^{ij}e_{2n+1-i,2n+1-j}),

and

\displaystyle(Sv_{k},Sv_{2n+1-\ell})_{sp_{2n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\omega^{ki}(-1)^{i+\ell}\omega^{-j\ell}(v_{i},v_{2n+1-j})_{sp_{2n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(-1)^{\ell}\omega^{(k-\ell)i}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\ell}\delta_{k\ell}=(v_{k},v_{2n+1-\ell})_{sp_{2n}}.

Thus also
S\in Sp_{2n}
and

\tilde{w}_{((n),\emptyset)}=S\sum_{j=1}^{n}\omega^{-j}(e_{jj}-e_{2n+1-j,2n+1-j})S^{-1}.

So if we define, analogously to the principal case for
gl_{n}
,

h_{w}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{n-2j+1}{2n}(e_{jj}-e_{2n+1-j,2n+1-j}),

then
S^{-1}{\rm Ad}\,\tilde{w}S=\exp(2\pi\sqrt{-1}{\rm ad}\,h_{w})
.
Note that condition (
5.2
) is satisfied since
\mathfrak{h}_{0}=\mathbb{C}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(e_{ii}-e_{2n+1-i,2n+1-i})
.

Let

\displaystyle a_{k\ell}^{+-}

\displaystyle=S^{-1}e_{k\ell}^{+-}S=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}(\omega^{-k})^{i}(\omega^{\ell})^{j}e_{ij}^{+-},

\displaystyle a_{k\ell}^{++}

\displaystyle=S^{-1}e_{k\ell}^{++}S=(-1)^{\ell}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}(\omega^{-k})^{i}(-\omega^{-\ell})^{j}e_{ij}^{++},

\displaystyle a_{k\ell}^{--}

\displaystyle=S^{-1}e_{k\ell}^{--}S=(-1)^{k}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}(-\omega^{k})^{i}(\omega^{\ell})^{j}e_{ij}^{--}.

Then

\displaystyle a_{k\ell}^{+-}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\left((\omega^{-k})^{i}(\omega^{-\ell})^{-j}t^{m}e_{ij}^{+-}\right)z^{-mn+i-j-1},

\displaystyle a_{k\ell}^{++}(z)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\ell}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\left((\omega^{-k})^{i}(-\omega^{-\ell})^{j}t^{m}e_{ij}^{++}\right)z^{-(m+1)n+i+j-2},

\displaystyle a_{k\ell}^{--}(z)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\left((-\omega^{-k})^{-i}(\omega^{-\ell})^{-j}t^{m}e_{ij}^{--}\right)z^{-(m-1)n-i-j}.

Using (
2.27
) and (
3.9
), we can calculate

\displaystyle r(a_{k\ell}^{+-})(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell,m\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}(\omega^{-k})^{i}(-\omega^{-\ell})^{-j}:b_{-2\ell n-i+\frac{1}{2}}b_{2(\ell+m)n+j-\frac{1}{2}}:z^{-mn+i-j-1},

\displaystyle r(a_{k\ell}^{++})(z)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\ell+1}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell,m\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}(\omega^{-k})^{i}(\omega^{-\ell})^{j}:b_{-2\ell n-i+\frac{1}{2}}b_{2(\ell+m+1)n-j+\frac{1}{2}}:z^{-(m+1)n+i+j-2},

\displaystyle r(a_{k\ell}^{--})(z)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}(-\omega^{-k})^{-i}(-\omega^{\ell})^{-j}:b_{-2(\ell-1)n+i-\frac{1}{2}}b_{2(\ell+m)n+j-\frac{1}{2}}:z^{-(m-1)n-i-j}.

Thus, relabeling the
b_{j}
as follows:

\ b^{+}_{\ell n-i+\frac{1}{2}}=b_{2\ell n-i+\frac{1}{2}},\quad b^{-}_{\ell n+i-\frac{1}{2}}=(-1)^{i}b_{2\ell n+i-\frac{1}{2}},

(5.23)

and their generating series as in (
5.15
), we find that

\displaystyle r(a_{k\ell}^{+-})(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega^{-k}}{n}:b^{+}(\omega^{-k}z)b^{-}(\omega^{-\ell}z):,

\displaystyle r(a_{k\ell}^{++})(z)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\ell+1}\frac{\omega^{-k-\ell}}{n}:b^{+}(\omega^{-k}z)b^{+}(\omega^{-\ell}z):,,

\displaystyle r(a_{k\ell}^{--})(z)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k}\frac{1}{n}:b^{-}(\omega^{-k}z)b^{-}(\omega^{-\ell}z):.

Note that the
b^{\pm}_{j}
defined by (
5.23
) satisfy

b^{\pm}_{i}b^{\pm}_{j}-b^{\pm}_{j}b^{\pm}_{i}=0,\quad b^{+}_{i}b^{-}_{j}-b^{-}_{j}b^{+}=\delta_{i,-j}\quad i,j\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z},

and

b^{\pm}_{i}|0\rangle=0\quad\mbox{for }i>0.

Again letting
\alpha(z)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\alpha_{j}z^{-j-1}=nr(a_{nn}^{+-})(z)
, this defines the Heisenberg subalgebra of
r(\hat{sp}_{2n})
, with commutation relations

[\alpha_{j},\alpha_{k}]=-j\delta_{jk},\quad\alpha_{j}|0\rangle=0,\quad\mbox{for }j\geq 0.

Finally,

[\alpha_{j},b^{\pm}(z)]=\mp z^{j}b^{\pm}(z).

Example 5.5

(One negative cycle, the principal Heisenberg subalgebra)

In this case
r=1
and
s=0
, hence

w_{(\emptyset,(n))}:\epsilon_{n}\mapsto-\epsilon_{1},\quad\epsilon_{k}\mapsto\epsilon_{k+1},\quad k=1,2,\ldots,n-1.

We choose

\tilde{w}=\tilde{w}_{(\emptyset,(n))}=e_{2n,n}+(-1)^{n}e_{1,n+1}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}(e_{i+1,i}-e_{2n-i,2n+1-i}).

Then it is straightforward to check that
{\rm Ad}\,\tilde{w}|_{\mathfrak{h}}=w_{(\emptyset,(n))}
and that this element satisfies

(\tilde{w}u,\tilde{w}v)_{sp_{2n}}=(u,v)_{sp_{2n}}\quad u,v\in\mathbb{C}^{2n}.

Thus
\tilde{w}\in{Sp}_{2n}
.
Let
\omega=e^{\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{4n}}
. The matrix
\tilde{w}
has eigenvectors

(\omega^{j},\omega^{2j},\ldots,\omega^{nj},(-1)^{n},(-1)^{n}(-\omega^{-j})^{1},(-1)^{n}(-\omega^{-j})^{2},\ldots,(-1)^{n}(-\omega^{-j})^{n-2},(-1)^{n}(-\omega^{-j})^{n-1})^{T},

with eigenvalues
\omega^{-j}
, for
j=1,3,5,\ldots 4n-1
.
Hence, we can diagonalize
\tilde{w}
by
S=(S_{ij})_{1\leq i,j\leq 2n}
, where

S_{i,j}=\frac{\omega^{\frac{n}{2}}(-\omega^{2j-1})^{i}}{\sqrt{2n}},\quad S_{2n+1-i,j}=\frac{\omega^{\frac{n}{2}}(\omega^{1-2j})^{n-i}}{\sqrt{2n}},\quad\mbox{for }1\leq i\leq n,\ 1\leq j\leq 2n,

so that
{\rm Ad}\,\tilde{w}=S(\exp(2\pi\sqrt{-1}{\rm ad}\,h_{w})S^{-1}
, for

h_{w}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{2n-2i+1}{4n}(e_{ii}-e_{2n+1-i,2n+1-i})

Note that
S^{-1}=(T_{ij})_{1\leq i,j\leq 2n}
, where

T_{i,j}=\frac{\omega^{-\frac{n}{2}}(-\omega^{2i-1})^{-j}}{\sqrt{2n}},\quad T_{i,2n+1-j}=\frac{\omega^{-\frac{n}{2}}(\omega^{1-2i})^{j-n}}{\sqrt{2n}},\quad\mbox{for }1\leq i\leq 2n,\ 1\leq j\leq n.

Moreover, it is straightforward to check that
(Su,Sv)_{sp_{2n}}=(u,v)_{sp_{2n}}
for
u,v\in\mathbb{C}^{2n}
, hence
S\in Sp_{2n}
.
Thus

\displaystyle a_{k\ell}^{+-}

\displaystyle=S^{-1}e_{k\ell}^{+-}S=\frac{(-\omega)^{k-\ell}}{2n}\sum_{i,j=1}^{2n}(\omega^{-2k})^{i}(\omega^{2\ell})^{j}(e_{ij}-(-1)^{i+j}e_{2n+1-j,2n+1-i}),

\displaystyle a_{k\ell}^{++}

\displaystyle=S^{-1}e_{k\ell}^{++}S=\frac{(-\omega)^{k}\omega^{n-\ell}}{2n}\sum_{i,j=1}^{2n}(\omega^{-2k})^{i}(-\omega^{2\ell})^{j}(e_{ij}-(-1)^{i+j}e_{2n+1-j,2n+1-i})

\displaystyle a_{k\ell}^{--}

\displaystyle=S^{-1}e_{k\ell}^{--}S=\frac{(-\omega)^{-\ell}\omega^{k-n}}{2n}\sum_{i,j=1}^{2n}(-\omega^{-2k})^{i}(\omega^{2\ell})^{j}(e_{ij}-(-1)^{i+j}e_{2n+1-j,2n+1-i}),

and the corresponding generating fields are

\displaystyle a_{k\ell}^{+-}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{(-\omega)^{k-\ell}}{2n}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{2n}\left((\omega^{-2k})^{i}(\omega^{2\ell})^{j}t^{m}(e_{ij}-(-1)^{i+j}e_{2n+1-j,2n+1-i})\right)z^{-2mn+i-j-1},

\displaystyle a_{k\ell}^{++}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{(-\omega)^{k}\omega^{n-\ell}}{2n}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{2n}\left((\omega^{-2k})^{i}(-\omega^{2\ell})^{j}t^{m}(e_{ij}-(-1)^{i+j}e_{2n+1-j,2n+1-i})\right)z^{-2mn+i-j-1},

\displaystyle a_{k\ell}^{--}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{(-\omega)^{-\ell}\omega^{k-n}}{2n}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\left((-\omega^{-2k})^{i}(\omega^{2\ell})^{j}t^{m}(e_{ij}-(-1)^{i+j}e_{2n+1-j,2n+1-i})\right)z^{-2mn+i-j-1}.

Using (
2.27
) and (
3.9
), we can express them in terms of the
b_{j}
:

\displaystyle r(a_{k\ell}^{+-})(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{(-\omega)^{k-\ell}}{2n}\sum_{\ell,m\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{2n}(\omega^{-2k})^{i}(-\omega^{2\ell})^{j}:b_{-2\ell n-i+\frac{1}{2}}b_{2(\ell+m)n+j-\frac{1}{2}}:z^{-2mn+i-j-1},

\displaystyle r(a_{k\ell}^{++})(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{(-\omega)^{k}\omega^{n-\ell}}{2n}\sum_{\ell,m\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{2n}(\omega^{-2k})^{i}(\omega^{2\ell})^{j}:b_{-2\ell n-i+\frac{1}{2}}b_{2(\ell+m)n+j-\frac{1}{2}}:z^{-2mn+i-j-1},

\displaystyle r(a_{k\ell}^{--})(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{(-\omega)^{-\ell}\omega^{k-n}}{2n}\sum_{\ell,m\in\mathbb{Z}}(-\omega^{-2k})^{i}(-\omega^{2\ell})^{j}:b_{-2\ell n-i+\frac{1}{2}}b_{2(\ell+m)n+j-\frac{1}{2}}:z^{-2mn+i-j-1}.

Defining the generating field of the
\tilde{\phi}_{j}
as
b(z)=\sum_{j\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}}b_{j}z^{-j-\frac{1}{2}}
, we find that for
\delta,\epsilon=+,-
:

r(a^{\delta,\epsilon}_{k,\ell})(z)=\frac{c^{\delta,\epsilon}_{k,\ell}}{2n}:b(\delta\omega^{-2k}z)b(\epsilon\omega^{-2\ell}z):,

where

c^{+-}_{k,\ell}=(-\omega)^{-k-\ell},\quad c^{++}_{k,\ell}=\sqrt{-1}(-\omega)^{-k}\omega^{-\ell},\quad c^{--}_{k,\ell}=\sqrt{-1}(-\omega)^{-\ell}\omega^{-k}.

Furthermore,

\alpha(z):=nr(a^{+-}_{nn})(z)=\sum_{j\in 1+2\mathbb{Z}}\alpha_{j}z^{-j}=\frac{1}{2}:b(z)b(-z):

is the generating field for the Heisenberg subalgebra. The modes
of this field satisfy

[\alpha_{j},\alpha_{k}]=-\frac{j}{2}\delta_{jk},\quad\alpha_{j}|0\rangle=0\quad\mbox{for }j>0.

Finally,

[\alpha_{j},b(z)]=z^{j}b(z).

The general construction follows from these two examples. Namely, it is straightforward to deduce the following proposition.

Proposition 5.6

let

w_{(\lambda,\mu)}
be given by (
5.20
).
Set
\lambda_{s+a}=:\mu_{a}
and

\omega_{a}=e^{\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{\lambda_{a}}}
if
1\leq a\leq s
and

\omega_{a}=e^{\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{2\lambda_{a}}}
if
s+1\leq a\leq r+s

. Then

(a) The element

\displaystyle\tilde{w}_{(\lambda,\mu)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{a=1}^{s}\omega_{a}^{\frac{\lambda_{a}+1}{2}}\big(e_{M_{a}+1,M_{a+1}}-(-1)^{\lambda_{a}}e_{2n-M_{a},2n-M_{a+1}+1}+

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{\lambda_{a}-1}(e_{M_{a}+i+1,M_{a}+i}-E_{2n-M_{a}-i,2n+1-Ma-i})\big)+

\displaystyle+\sum_{a=s+1}^{r+s}\big(e_{2n-M_{a},M_{a+1}}+(-1)^{\lambda_{a}}e_{M_{a}+1,N-M_{a+1}+1}+

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{\lambda_{a}-1}(e_{M_{a}+i+1,M_{a}+i}-e_{2n-M_{a}-i,2n+1-Ma-i})\big)\in Sp_{2n}

is a lift of
w_{(\lambda,\mu)}
and

\tilde{w}_{(\lambda,\mu)}=S^{-1}\exp(2\pi\sqrt{-1}h_{w})S
, where

h_{w}=\sum_{a=1}^{s}\sum_{j=1}^{\lambda_{a}}\frac{\lambda_{a}-2j+1}{2\lambda_{a}}\epsilon_{M_{a}+j}+\sum_{b=s+1}^{r+s}\sum_{j=1}^{\lambda_{b}}\frac{\lambda_{b}-j+\frac{1}{2}}{2\lambda_{b}}\epsilon_{M_{b}+j}\in\mathfrak{h}

satisfies
(
5.2
),
and

\displaystyle S=

\displaystyle\sum_{a=1}^{s}\sum_{i=1}^{\lambda_{a}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda}_{a}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{\lambda_{a}}(\omega_{a}^{ij}e_{M_{a}+i,M_{a}+j}+(-1)^{i+j}\omega^{-ij}e_{2n+1-M_{a}-i,2n+1-M_{a}-j})+

\displaystyle+\sum_{a=s+1}^{r+s}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\lambda_{a}}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{\lambda_{a}}\big((-1)^{i}\omega_{a}^{\frac{\lambda_{a}}{4}}(\omega_{a}^{-i})^{\frac{1}{2}-j}e_{M_{a}+i,M_{a}+j}+(-1)^{i}\omega_{a}^{\frac{\lambda_{a}}{4}}(\omega_{a}^{-i})^{\frac{1}{2}-\lambda_{a}-j}e_{M_{a}+i,2n-M_{a+1}+j}+

\displaystyle+\omega_{a}^{\frac{\lambda_{a}}{4}}(\omega^{\lambda_{a}-i})^{\frac{1}{2}-j}e_{2n+1-M_{a}-i,M_{a}+j}+\omega_{a}^{\frac{\lambda_{a}}{4}}(\omega^{\lambda_{a}-i})^{\frac{1}{2}-\lambda_{a}-j}e_{2n+1-M_{a}-i,2n-M_{a+1}+j}\big).

(b) The order
N
of
\exp(2\pi\sqrt{-1}{\rm ad}\,h_{w})
is equal to the least common multiple
N^{\prime}
of
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\ldots,\lambda_{s},2\lambda_{s+1},2\lambda_{s+2},\ldots 2\lambda_{r+s}
if
N^{\prime}(\frac{1}{\lambda_{a}}+\frac{1}{\lambda_{b}})\in 2\mathbb{Z}
for all
1\leq a,b\leq s
,
N^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2\lambda_{a}}+\frac{1}{2\lambda_{b}})\in 2\mathbb{Z}
for all
s+1\leq a,b\leq r+s
,
N^{\prime}(\frac{1}{\lambda_{a}}+\frac{1}{2\lambda_{b}})\in 2\mathbb{Z}
for all
1\leq a\leq s
,
s+1\leq b\leq r+s
and
2N^{\prime}
otherwise.

(c) The elements

a_{k,\ell}^{\delta a,\epsilon b}:=S^{-1}e_{k\ell}^{\delta a,\epsilon b}S=

form a new basis of
sp_{2n}
and the representation
r
in
F_{c}
of their generating fields

a_{k\ell}^{\delta a,\epsilon b}(z)

are equal to

r(a_{k,\ell}^{\delta_{a},\epsilon b})(z)=L_{k}^{\delta a}R_{\ell}^{\epsilon b}:B^{\delta a}_{k}(z)B^{\epsilon b}_{\ell}(z):,

where

\displaystyle L_{k}^{+a}=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\frac{(-1)^{k+M_{a}}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{a}}}&1\leq a\leq s,\\
\frac{\gamma_{a}^{-1}}{\sqrt{2\lambda_{a}}}\omega_{a}^{-\frac{k}{2}+\frac{\lambda_{a}}{4}}&s<a\leq r+s,\\
\end{cases}\qquad L_{k}^{-a}=\begin{cases}\frac{\omega_{a}^{-k}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{a}}}&1\leq a\leq s,\\
\frac{(-1)^{k}\gamma_{a}^{-1}}{\sqrt{2\lambda_{a}}}\omega_{a}^{-\frac{k}{2}-\frac{\lambda_{a}}{4}}&s<a\leq r+s\\
\end{cases}

\displaystyle R_{k}^{+a}=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\frac{(-1)^{k+M_{a}+1}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{a}}}\omega_{a}^{-k}&1\leq a\leq s,\\
\frac{\gamma_{a}}{\sqrt{2\lambda_{a}}}\omega_{a}^{-\frac{k}{2}+\frac{3\lambda_{a}}{4}}&s<a\leq r+s,\\
\end{cases}\qquad R_{k}^{-a}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda_{a}}}&1\leq a\leq s,\\
\frac{(-1)^{k}\gamma_{a}}{\sqrt{2\lambda_{a}}}\omega_{a}^{-\frac{k}{2}+\frac{\lambda_{a}}{4}}&s<a\leq r+s,\\
\end{cases}

where
\gamma_{a}=1
if
M_{a}
is even and
\gamma_{a}=i
if
M_{a}
is odd,and

B_{k}^{\pm a}(z)=\begin{cases}b^{\pm a}(\omega_{a}^{-k}z^{\frac{N}{\lambda_{a}}})z^{\frac{N}{2\lambda_{a}}-\frac{1}{2}}&1\leq a\leq s,\\
b^{a}(\pm\omega_{a}^{-k}z^{\frac{N}{2\lambda_{a}}})z^{\frac{N}{4\lambda_{a}}-\frac{1}{2}}&s<a\leq s+r,\\
\end{cases}

where the

b^{\pm a}(z)=\sum_{i\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}}b_{i}^{\pm a}z^{-i-\frac{1}{2}},\quad b^{s+b}(z)=\sum_{i\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}}b^{s+b}z^{-i-\frac{1}{2}},\quad 1\leq a\leq s,\ 1\leq b\leq r,

are the generating fields
for the relabeld generators of the Weyl algebra
C\ell_{c}
(
1\leq a\leq s
,
1\leq b\leq r
,
1\leq i\leq\lambda_{a}
,
1\leq j\leq\lambda_{s+b}
,
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
):

\displaystyle b^{+a}_{\ell\lambda_{a}-i+\frac{1}{2}}=b_{2\ell n-M_{a}-i+\frac{1}{2}},\quad b^{-a}_{\ell\lambda_{a}+i-\frac{1}{2}}=(-1)^{M_{a}+i}b_{2\ell n+M_{a}+i-\frac{1}{2}},

(5.24)

\displaystyle b^{s+b}_{2\ell\lambda_{s+b}-j+\frac{1}{2}}=\gamma_{s+b}b_{2\ell n-M_{s+b}-j+\frac{1}{2}},\quad b^{s+b}_{2\ell\lambda_{s+b}+j-\frac{1}{2}}=\gamma_{s+b}b_{2\ell n+M_{s+b}+j-\frac{1}{2}}.

They satisfy (
1\leq a,c\leq s,\ 1\leq b,d\leq r,\quad i,j\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}
)

\displaystyle b^{\pm a}_{i}b^{\pm c}_{j}-b^{\pm c}_{j}b^{\pm a}_{i}

\displaystyle=0,

(5.25)

\displaystyle b^{+a}_{i}b^{-c}_{j}-b^{-c}_{j}b^{+a}

\displaystyle=\delta_{ac}\delta_{i,-j}

\displaystyle b_{i}^{s+b}b_{j}^{s+d}-b_{j}^{s+d}b_{i}^{s+b}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{i-\frac{1}{2}}\delta_{bd}\delta_{i,-j},

\displaystyle b^{\pm a}_{i}b^{s+b}_{j}-b^{s+b}_{j}b^{\pm a}_{i}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle b^{\pm a}_{i}|0\rangle=b_{i}^{s+b}|0\rangle

\displaystyle=0\quad i>0.

(d) The representation of
\hat{sp}_{n}
is given in terms of vertex operators by formulas (
3.79
), (
3.80
) and (
3.81
) by substitution of roots of unity
\omega_{a}
and powers of
z
as given
in the above formulas. In particular, the Heisenberg algebra, which is described
by the fields
\alpha^{a}(z)
are defined as in (
3.41
)
and they satisfy (
3.42
).

The
q
dimension formula differs from the one given in (
3.60
), since the gradation is different. Let
x^{2}=1
and
N
be the least common multiple of
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{s}
,
2\mu_{1},\ldots,2\mu_{r}
, then

\displaystyle\dim_{q}F^{\overline{0}}

\displaystyle+xF^{\overline{1}}=\prod_{a=1}^{s}\prod_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(1-xq^{\frac{(j-\frac{1}{2})N}{\lambda_{a}})^{2}}}\prod_{b=1}^{r}\prod_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{1-xq^{\frac{(j-\frac{1}{2})N}{\mu_{b}}}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{a=1}^{s}\prod_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{1-q^{\frac{jN}{\lambda_{a}}}}\sum_{j,k=0}^{\infty}\frac{q^{\frac{kN}{2\lambda_{a}}}x^{k}}{(1-q^{\frac{N}{\lambda_{a}}})\cdots(1-q^{\frac{kN}{\lambda_{a}}})}q^{jk\frac{N}{\lambda_{a}}}\frac{q^{\frac{jN}{2\lambda_{a}}}x^{j}}{(1-q^{\frac{N}{\lambda_{a}}})\cdots(1-q^{\frac{jN}{\lambda_{a}}})}\times

\displaystyle\prod_{b=1}^{r}\prod_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{1+xq^{\frac{N(j-\frac{1}{2})}{\mu_{b}}}}{1-q^{\frac{(2j-1)N}{\mu_{b}}}}.

5.4
so_{2n}

Recall that the conjugacy classes of the Weyl group for
so_{2n}
are in one-to-one correspondence with the pair of partitions

\lambda=(\lambda_{1}\geq\lambda_{2}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{s}>0),\mu=(\mu_{1}\geq\mu_{2},\geq\cdots\geq\mu_{r}>0),

(5.26)

of
n
, with
r\in 2\mathbb{Z}
,
i.e.

|\lambda|+|\mu|=\lambda_{1}+\cdots+\lambda_{s}+\mu_{1}+\cdots+\mu_{r}=n.

(5.27)

We can decompose each element
w\in W
in
s
disjoint positive cycles of length
\lambda_{i}
, and
r
disjoint negative cycles of length
\mu_{j}
such that (
5.27
) holds. It is straightforward to show that two elements with the same cycle type are conjugate under
O_{2n}
.

We relabel the basis as follows.
Let
m_{a}=\lambda_{a}
,
M_{a}=|\lambda|_{a-1}
for
1\leq a\leq s
and
m_{a}=\mu_{a-s}
,
M_{a}=|\lambda|+|\mu|_{a-s-1}
for
s<a\leq r+s
and
M_{r+s+1}=|\lambda|+|\mu|
, then set (
1\leq a,b\leq s+r
,
1\leq i\leq m_{a}
,
1\leq j\leq m_{b}
)

\displaystyle e_{ij}^{+a,-b}=

\displaystyle e_{M_{a}+i,M_{b}+j}-e_{2n+1-M_{b}-j,2n+1-M_{a}-i}

(5.28)

\displaystyle e_{ij}^{+a,+b}=

\displaystyle-=e_{M_{a}+i,2n+1-M_{b}-j}-e_{M_{b}+j,2n+1-M_{a}-i}

\displaystyle e_{ij}^{-a,-b}

\displaystyle=-e_{ji}^{-b,-a}=e_{2n+1-M_{a}-i,M_{b}+j}-e_{2n+1-M_{b}-j,M_{a}+i}.

We can associate, as for
sp_{2n}
to a pair of partition
(\lambda,\mu)
of
n
a standard element
w_{(\lambda,\mu)}\in W
, namely the one given by (
5.20
), but now we want to lift this to
SO_{2n}
.
As in the case of
sp_{2n}
we will first investigate two examples.

Example 5.7

(One positive cycle)

In this case
s=1
and
r=0
and we have the same element as in
(
5.22
). However now we have to lift this to
SO_{2n}
.
Let
\omega=e^{\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{n}}
choose

\tilde{w}_{((n),\emptyset)}=\omega^{\frac{n+1}{2}}e_{1,n}+\omega^{-\frac{n+1}{2}}e_{2n,n+1}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}(\omega^{\frac{n+1}{2}}e_{i+1,i}+\omega^{-\frac{n+1}{2}}e_{2n-i,2n+1-i}).

Clearly,
(\tilde{w}_{((n),\emptyset)}v,\tilde{w}_{((n),\emptyset)}w)_{so_{2n}}=(v,w)_{so_{2n}}
and we can diagonalize
\tilde{w}_{((n),\emptyset)}
by choosing

S=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}(\omega^{ij}e_{ij}+\omega^{-ij}e_{2n+1-i,2n+1-j}).

Then

S^{-1}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}(\omega^{-ij}e_{ij}+\omega^{ij}e_{2n+1-i,2n+1-j})

and

\tilde{w}_{((n),\emptyset)}=Se^{2\pi\sqrt{-1}h_{w}}S^{-1}
with

h_{w}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{n-2j+1}{2n}(e_{jj}-e_{2n+1-j,2n+1-j}).

Let
a_{k\ell}^{\delta\epsilon}=S^{-1}e_{k\ell}^{\delta\epsilon}S
, then

\displaystyle a_{k\ell}^{+-}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}(\omega^{-k})^{i}(\omega^{-\ell})^{-j}t^{m}e_{ij}^{+-}z^{-mn+i-j-1},

(5.29)

\displaystyle a_{k\ell}^{++}(z)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}(\omega^{-k})^{i}(\omega^{-\ell})^{j}t^{m}e_{ij}^{++}z^{-(m+1)n+i+j-2},

\displaystyle a_{k\ell}^{--}(z)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}(\omega^{-k})^{-i}(\omega^{-\ell})^{-j}t^{m}e_{ij}^{--}z^{-(m-1)n-i-j}.

Using (
2.27
) and (
2.23
), we can calculate

\displaystyle r(a_{k\ell}^{+-})(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell,m\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}(\omega^{-k})^{i}(\omega^{-\ell})^{-j}:\phi_{-2\ell n-i+\frac{1}{2}}\phi_{2(\ell+m)+j-\frac{1}{2}}:z^{-mn+i-j-1},

\displaystyle r(a_{k\ell}^{++})(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\ell,m\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}(\omega^{-k})^{i}(\omega^{-\ell})^{j}:\phi_{-2\ell n-i+\frac{1}{2}}\phi_{2(\ell+m+1)-j+\frac{1}{2}}:z^{-(m+1)n+i+j-2},

\displaystyle r(a_{k\ell}^{--})(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}(\omega^{-k})^{-i}(\omega^{-\ell})^{-j}:\phi_{-2(\ell-1)n+i-\frac{1}{2}}\phi_{2(\ell+m)+j-\frac{1}{2}}:z^{-(m-1)n-i-j}.

Thus, relabeling the
\tilde{\phi}_{j}
as follows:

\psi^{+}_{\ell n-i+\frac{1}{2}}=\phi_{2\ell n-i+\frac{1}{2}},\quad\psi^{-}_{\ell n+i-\frac{1}{2}}=\phi_{2\ell n+i-\frac{1}{2}},

(5.30)

and their generating series as in (
5.15
), we find that

\displaystyle r(a_{k\ell}^{+-})(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega^{-k}}{n}:\psi^{+}(\omega^{-k}z)\psi^{-}(\omega^{-\ell}z):,

(5.31)

\displaystyle r(a_{k\ell}^{++})(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega^{-k-\ell}}{n}:\psi^{+}(\omega^{-k}z)\psi^{+}(\omega^{-\ell}z):,,

\displaystyle r(a_{k\ell}^{--})(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}:\psi^{-}(\omega^{-k}z)\psi^{-}(\omega^{-\ell}z):.

Note that the
\psi^{\pm}_{j}
defined by (
5.30
) satisfy

\psi^{\pm}_{i}\psi^{\pm}_{j}+\psi^{\pm}_{j}\psi^{\pm}_{i}=0,\quad\psi^{+}_{i}\psi^{-}_{j}+\psi^{-}_{j}\psi^{+}=\delta_{i,-j}\quad i,j\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z},

and

\psi^{\pm}_{i}|0\rangle=0\quad\mbox{for }i>0.

Again defining
\alpha(z)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\alpha_{j}z^{-j-1}=nr(a_{nn}^{+-})(z)
, this defines the Heisenberg algebra, with commutation relations

[\alpha_{j},\alpha_{k}]=j\delta_{jk},\quad\alpha_{j}|0\rangle=0,\quad\mbox{for }j\geq 0.

Finally,

[\alpha_{j},\psi^{\pm}(z)]=\pm z^{j}\psi^{\pm}(z).

Example 5.8

(Two negative cycles)

In this case
s=0
and
r=2
, hence
\mu=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})
with
\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}=n
.
The special case that
\mu=(n-1,1)
corresponds to the principal realization.
Take the following lift of
w_{(\emptyset,(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}))}
in
O_{2n}
:

\displaystyle\tilde{w}_{(\emptyset,(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}))}=

\displaystyle e_{1,2n+1-\mu_{1}}+e_{2n,\mu_{1}}+e_{\mu_{1}+1,2n+1-\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}}+e_{2n-\mu_{1},\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}}+

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{\mu_{1}-1}e_{i+1,i}+e_{2n-i,2n+1-i}+\sum_{i=1}^{\mu_{2}-1}e_{\mu_{1}+i+1,\mu_{1}+i}+e_{2n-i-\mu_{1},2n+1-i-\mu_{1}}.

Let
\omega_{j}=e^{\frac{\pi i}{\mu_{j}}}
, and
\gamma_{i}=1
if
\mu_{i}
is even and
\gamma_{i}=\sqrt{-1}
if
\mu_{i}
is odd.
Let

\displaystyle S=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{4\mu_{1}}}\sum_{k=1}^{\mu_{1}}[(-1)^{k}(\gamma_{1}e_{k1}+\gamma_{1}^{-1}e_{2n+1-k,1}+\gamma_{1}^{-1}e_{2n+1-k,2n}+\gamma_{1}e_{k,2n})+

\displaystyle+(e_{k,\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}}+e_{2n+1-k,\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}}+e_{2n+1-k,2n+1-\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}}+e_{k,2n+1-\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}})]+

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{4\mu_{2}}}\sum_{k=1}^{\mu_{2}}\sqrt{-1}[(-1)^{k}(\gamma_{2}e_{\mu_{1}+k,1}+\gamma_{2}^{-1}e_{2n+1-\mu_{1}-k,1}+-\gamma_{2}^{-1}e_{2n+1-\mu_{1}-k,2n}-\gamma_{2}e_{\mu_{1}+k,2n})+

\displaystyle+(e_{\mu_{1}+k,\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}}+e_{2n+1-\mu_{1}-k,\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}}-e_{2n+1-\mu_{1}-k,2n+1-\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}}-e_{\mu_{1}+k,2n+1-\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}})]+

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mu_{1}}}\sum_{i=1}^{\mu_{1}}\sum_{j=2}^{\mu_{1}}(\omega_{1}^{(j-\mu_{1}-1)i}e_{ij}+\omega_{1}^{(j-\mu_{1}-1)(\mu_{1}+i)}e_{2n+1-i,j}+\omega_{1}^{-(j-\mu_{1}-1)(i-\mu_{1})}e_{i,2n+1-j}+

\displaystyle+\omega_{1}^{-(j-\mu_{1}-1)i}e_{2n+1-i,2n+1-j})+

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mu_{2}}}\sum_{i=1}^{\mu_{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{\mu_{2}-1}(\omega_{2}^{(j-\mu_{2})i}e_{\mu_{1}+i,\mu_{1}+j}+\omega_{2}^{(j-\mu_{2})(\mu_{2}+i)}e_{2n+1-\mu_{1}-i,\mu_{1}+j}+

\displaystyle+\omega_{2}^{-(j-\mu_{2})(i-\mu_{2})}e_{\mu_{1}+i,2n+1-\mu_{1}-j}+\omega_{2}^{-(j-\mu_{2})i}e_{2n+1-\mu_{1}-i,2n+1-\mu_{1}-j}).

It is straightforward to check that
S
is indeed in
O_{2n}
and hence, the inverse of this matrix is equal to

\displaystyle S^{-1}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{4\mu_{1}}}\sum_{k=1}^{\mu_{1}}[(-1)^{k}(\gamma_{1}e_{1,k}+\gamma_{1}e_{2n,k}+\gamma_{1}^{-1}e_{1,2n+1-k}+\gamma_{1}^{-1}e_{2n,2n+1-k})+

\displaystyle+e_{\mu_{1}+\mu_{2},k}+e_{2n+1-\mu_{1}-\mu_{2},k}+e_{\mu_{1}+\mu_{2},2n+1-k}+e_{2n+1-\mu_{1}-\mu_{2},2n+1-k})]+

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{4\mu_{2}}}\sum_{k=1}^{\mu_{2}}\sqrt{-1}[(-1)^{k}(-\gamma_{2}e_{1,\mu_{1}+k}+\gamma_{2}e_{2n,\mu_{1}+k}-\gamma_{2}^{-1}e_{1,2n+1-\mu_{1}-k}+\gamma_{2}^{-1}e_{2n,2n+1-\mu_{1}-k})+

\displaystyle+(-e_{\mu_{1}+\mu_{2},\mu_{1}+k}+e_{2n+1-\mu_{1}-\mu_{2},\mu_{1}+k}-e_{\mu_{1}+\mu_{2},2n+1-\mu_{1}-k}+e_{2n+1-\mu_{1}-\mu_{2},2n+1-\mu_{1}-k})]+

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mu_{1}}}\sum_{i=1}^{\mu_{1}}\sum_{j=2}^{\mu_{1}}(\omega_{1}^{(j-\mu_{1}-1)i}e_{ji}+\omega_{1}^{-(j-\mu_{1}-1)i}e_{2n+1-j,2n+1-i}+\omega_{1}^{-(j-\mu_{1}-1)(\mu_{1}+i)}e_{2n+1-j,i}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad++

\displaystyle+\omega_{1}^{(j-\mu_{1}-1)(i-\mu_{1})}e_{j,2n+1-i}+

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mu_{2}}}\sum_{i=1}^{\mu_{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{\mu_{2}-1}(\omega_{2}^{(j-\mu_{2})i}e_{\mu_{1}+j,\mu_{1}+i}+\omega_{2}^{-(j-\mu_{2})i}e_{2n+1-\mu_{1}-j,2n+1-\mu_{1}-i}

\displaystyle+\omega_{2}^{-(j-\mu_{2})(\mu_{2}+i)}e_{2n+1-\mu_{1}-j,\mu_{1}+i}+\omega_{2}^{(j-\mu_{2})(i-\mu_{2})}e_{\mu_{1}+j,2n+1-\mu_{1}-i}).

Then

S^{-1}\tilde{w}_{(\emptyset,(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}))}S=\exp\left(2\pi\sqrt{-1}h_{w}\right)
, where

h_{w}=\sum_{i=1}^{\mu_{1}}\frac{\mu_{1}-i+1}{2\mu_{1}}(e_{ii}-e_{2n+1-i,2n+1-i})+\sum_{i=1}^{\mu_{2}}\frac{\mu_{2}-i}{2\mu_{2}}(e_{\mu_{1}+i,\mu_{1}+i}-e_{2n+1-\mu_{1}-i,2n+1-\mu_{1}-i}).

S
and
S^{-1}
suggest the following relabeling of the
\tilde{\phi}_{j}
, namely (
1\leq j\leq\mu_{1}-1
,
1\leq k\leq\mu_{2}-1
):

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}^{1}_{2\ell\mu_{1}-j}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{1}^{-1}\phi_{2\ell n-j-\frac{1}{2}},\qquad\tilde{\phi}^{1}_{2\ell\mu_{1}+j}=\gamma_{1}(-1)^{j}\phi_{2\ell n+j+\frac{1}{2}},

(5.32)

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}^{1}_{2\ell\mu_{1}+\mu_{1}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma_{1}^{-1}}{\sqrt{2}}(\phi_{2\ell n+\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}-\frac{1}{2}}+\phi_{2(\ell+1)-\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}+\frac{1}{2}}),

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}^{1}_{2\ell\mu_{1}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\phi_{2\ell n+\frac{1}{2}}+\phi_{2\ell n-\frac{1}{2}}),

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}^{2}_{2\ell\mu_{2}-k}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{2}^{-1}\phi_{2\ell n-\mu_{1}-k+\frac{1}{2}},\qquad\tilde{\phi}^{2}_{2\ell\mu_{2}+k}=\gamma_{2}(-1)^{k}\phi_{2\ell n+\mu_{1}+k-\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}^{2}_{2\ell\mu_{2}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{-1}{2}}(\phi_{2\ell-\frac{1}{2}}-\phi_{2\ell n+\frac{1}{2}}),

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}^{2}_{2\ell\mu_{2}+\mu_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma_{2}^{-1}\sqrt{-1}}{\sqrt{2}}(\phi_{2(\ell+1)n-\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}+\frac{1}{2}}-\phi_{2\ell n+\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}-\frac{1}{2}}).

We find that the new
\tilde{\phi}^{a}_{j}
defined by (
5.32
) satisfy

\tilde{\phi}^{a}_{j}\tilde{\phi}^{b}_{k}+\tilde{\phi}_{k}^{b}\tilde{\phi}_{j}^{a}=(-1)^{j}\delta_{ab}\delta_{j,-k}\quad a,b=1,2,\ j,k\in\mathbb{Z},

and

\tilde{\phi}_{j}^{a}|0\rangle=0,\qquad j>0.

Defining the fields

\tilde{\phi}^{b}(z)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\tilde{\phi}_{j}^{b}z^{-j},

then for
a^{\delta b,\epsilon c}_{k,\ell}:=S^{-1}e^{\delta b,\epsilon c}_{k,\ell}S
, with

b,c=1,2
,
\delta,\epsilon=+,-
,
1\leq k\leq\mu_{b}
and
1\leq\ell\leq\mu_{c}
, we have

\displaystyle r(a^{\delta b,\epsilon c}_{k,\ell})(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}r(a^{\delta b,\epsilon c}_{k,\ell}(j)_{j\,{\rm mod}\,N})z^{-j-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma_{b}^{\delta 1}\gamma_{c}^{\epsilon 1}(-1)^{k+\ell}z^{-1}}{2\sqrt{\mu_{b}\mu_{c}}}:\tilde{\phi}^{b}(\delta\omega_{b}^{-k}z^{\frac{N}{2\mu_{b}}})\tilde{\phi}^{b}(\epsilon\omega_{c}^{-\ell}z^{\frac{N}{2\mu_{c}}}):\qquad b,c=1,2,\ \delta,\epsilon=+,-.

where
N=2\,{\rm lcm}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})
, is the order of the automorphism
\exp(2\pi\sqrt{-1}{\rm ad}\,h_{w})
(see
[
25
]
for more details).
Let

\alpha^{b}(z)=\sum_{k\in 1+2\mathbb{Z}}\alpha_{k}^{b}z^{-k}=-\lambda_{b}z^{\frac{2\lambda_{b}}{N}}r(a^{+b,-b}_{\lambda_{b},\lambda_{b}})(z^{\frac{2\lambda_{b}}{N}})=:\frac{1}{2}:\tilde{\phi}^{b}(z)\tilde{\phi}^{b}(-z):.

be the generating field for the Heisenberg algebra. The modes
of the field satisfy

[\alpha_{j},\alpha_{k}]=\frac{j}{2}\delta_{jk},\quad\alpha_{j}|0\rangle=0,\quad\mbox{for }j\geq 0.

Finally,

[\alpha_{j},\tilde{\phi}(z)]=z^{j}\tilde{\phi}(z).

The general description follows from the above two examples.
It is straightforward to deduce the following proposition.

Proposition 5.9

Let

w_{(\lambda,\mu)}
be given by (
5.20
), where now
r
is even.
Set
\lambda_{s+a}=:\mu_{a}
and

\omega_{a}=e^{\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{\lambda_{a}}}
if
1\leq a\leq s
and

\omega_{a}=e^{\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{2\lambda_{a}}}
is
s+1\leq a\leq r+s
.
Then

(a) The element

\displaystyle\tilde{w}_{(\lambda,\mu)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{a=1}^{s}\big(\omega_{a}^{\frac{\lambda_{a}+1}{2}}e_{M_{a}+1,M_{a+1}}+\omega_{a}^{-\frac{\lambda_{a}+1}{2}}e_{2n-M_{a},2n-M_{a+1}+1}+\sum_{i=1}^{\lambda_{a}-1}(\omega_{a}^{\frac{\lambda_{a}+1}{2}}e_{M_{a}+i+1,M_{a}+i}+

\displaystyle+\omega^{-\frac{\lambda_{a}+1}{2}}e_{2n-M_{a}-i,2n+1-M_{a}-i})\big)+\sum_{a=1}^{\frac{r}{2}}\big(e_{M_{s+2a-1}+1,2n+1-M_{s+2a}}+

\displaystyle+e_{2n-M_{s+2a},M_{s+2a}}+e_{M_{s+2a}+1,2n+1-M_{s+2a+1}}+e_{2n-M_{s+2a},M_{s+2a+1}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{\lambda_{s+2a-1}-1}e_{M_{s+2a-1}+i+1,M_{s+2a-1}+i}+e_{2n-M_{s+2a-1}-i,2n+1-M_{s+2a-1}-i}+

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{\lambda_{s+2a}-1}e_{M_{s}+{2a}+i+1,M_{s+2a}+i}+e_{2n-M_{s+2a}-i,2n+1-M_{s+2a}-i}.\big)\in O_{2n}

is a lift of
w_{(\lambda,\mu)}
and

\tilde{w}_{(\lambda,\mu)}=S^{-1}\exp(2\pi\sqrt{-1}h_{w})S
, where
